Dashboard validation rides on `pkg/metrics/dashboard_us447_test.go` (mirrors the US-409 dashboard test shape: path-robust JSON parse + required-metric grep + `ontology=~` PromQL selector + templating-variable presence check).
- Performance regression suite (US-441): the canonical 8-subsystem bench suite lives at `bench/` (one Go test package, `*_test.go` only) and is gated by `cmd/benchcheck` against `bench/baseline.json`. Naming convention is `Benchmark<Area>_US441` so the Makefile glob `Benchmark.*_US441` captures every bench; future stories that want to ride this gate must follow the suffix. The runner takes `go test -bench` text output on stdin (NOT `-json`) and parses lines via `^(Benchmark[\w/.]+?)(?:-\d+)?\s+(\d+)\s+([\d.]+)\s+ns/op` — the `-N` GOMAXPROCS suffix is stripped so `BenchmarkLoad_US441-16` maps to the same baseline key as `-8`. Multi-sample handling: `-count=N>1` keeps the SLOWEST sample per name so the baseline encodes worst-case observed latency on the recording machine; the runtime gate at `thresholdRatio=1.20` then has to overshoot even that lucky-bad recording by another 20% to fail. Baseline is hardware-specific (M3 Max as recorded); CI runners on different hardware refresh once via `make bench-update` and commit the new `baseline.json`. `bench/results.json` is per-run output (gitignored); `bench/baseline.json` IS checked in. The structural unit test `bench/coverage_test.go::TestBaselineCoversAllSubsystems` asserts all 8 expected names + positive ns/op + threshold > 0 so a rename / accidental deletion fails `go test ./bench/...` even when the gate hasn't been re-run. Pattern: when adding a benchmark to the gate, drop a `Benchmark<Area>_US441` function in `bench/suite_test.go`, run `make bench-update`, commit the diff. When measuring a path with high commit-jitter (Bleve segment merges, FS sync), measure read-only or steady-state and let WRITE coverage be transitive — `BenchmarkIndex_US441` is search-only because `IndexDocument`-in-the-loop overran the 20% gate at 200ms benchtime; the write path is implicitly exercised by every Bleve-seeded bench's setup phase plus the heavier `pkg/oms/embedding_us437_benchmark_test.go` for high-cardinality writes.

## 2026-05-02 - US-365 - ObjectSet 不可变快照持久化

**Implementation:**
- New migration `000082_saved_object_set_snapshots.{up,down}.sql` adds three columns (`definition_hash`, `snapshot_at`, `is_immutable`) to BOTH `saved_object_sets` and `object_set_snapshots`, plus the materialised PK columns (`frozen_object_type`, `frozen_primary_keys`, `frozen_truncated`) on `saved_object_sets`. A shared sequence `saved_object_sets_snapshot_seq` allocates monotonic snapshot transaction ids.
- `pkg/oss/objectset/persistence.go`: extended `SavedObjectSet` struct with the US-365 fields. `PGSavedStore.Create`/`Update` compute `definition_hash` (canonical-JSON sha256 via new `HashDefinition` helper) and pull `snapshot_at` from `nextval()`. New `ReapExpired(ctx, ttl)` method drops `is_immutable=false` rows older than ttl; new `RunReaperLoop` is a context-aware ticker driver suitable for cmd/server bootstrap. `Get`/`GetByName`/`List` round-trip the new fields with `COALESCE` defaults so legacy rows pre-migration read cleanly.
- `pkg/oss/objectset/store.go`: in-memory `storeEntry` now carries `snapshotAt` (allocated from a process-wide atomic counter `NextSnapshotAt()`). Exposed via `EntrySnapshot.SnapshotAt` so callers can prove `createTemporary` records a snapshot transaction.
- `pkg/oss/objectset/snapshot_persisted.go`: `PersistedSnapshot` and the wire response shapes (`CreateSnapshotResponse`, `GetSnapshotResponse`) now propagate `definitionHash`, `snapshotAt`, `isImmutable`. The `CreateSnapshot` handler stamps the hash via `HashDefinition(json.Marshal(def))` and allocates `snapshotAt = NextSnapshotAt()`; snapshots are always `IsImmutable=true` since they exist precisely to deliver byte-for-byte re-loads.
- `pkg/oms/object_set_snapshot.go` + `pkg/oms/pg_repository_object_set_snapshot.go`: extended `ObjectSetSnapshot` struct and the PG INSERT/SELECT to round-trip the new columns.
- `cmd/server/object_set_snapshot_adapter.go`: pass-through of the three new fields between `objectset.PersistedSnapshot` and `oms.ObjectSetSnapshot`.

**Files changed:**
- `migrations/000082_saved_object_set_snapshots.up.sql` (new)
- `migrations/000082_saved_object_set_snapshots.down.sql` (new)
- `pkg/oss/objectset/persistence.go`
- `pkg/oss/objectset/store.go`
- `pkg/oss/objectset/snapshot_persisted.go`
- `pkg/oss/objectset/us365_immutable_snapshot_test.go` (new — 10 unit tests)
- `pkg/oss/objectset/persistence_test.go` (5 new integration tests + helper exposing the pgxpool)
- `pkg/oms/object_set_snapshot.go`
- `pkg/oms/pg_repository_object_set_snapshot.go`
- `cmd/server/object_set_snapshot_adapter.go`
- `prd.json` (mark US-365 passes: true)

**Verification:**
- `go build ./...` clean
- `go vet ./...` clean (a pre-existing `seedOntology redeclared` failure under `-tags integration` is unrelated to this story)
- `go test ./...` all green; new package-local suite `pkg/oss/objectset/us365_immutable_snapshot_test.go` runs 10/10 PASS
- `go test -tags integration -run NEVERMATCH ./pkg/oss/objectset/...` confirms the 5 new integration tests compile (live PG run requires Docker)

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- The acceptance criterion says "saved_object_sets 表新增 ... is_immutable BOOL", but the durable mechanism currently delivering byte-for-byte re-loads is `object_set_snapshots` (US-224). I added the columns to BOTH tables so future stories that wire `PGSavedStore` end up consistent with the existing snapshot path. The `cmd/server` adapter (`object_set_snapshot_adapter.go`) is the right place to add new field passthroughs whenever an OMS row gains columns.
- The PG `Update` path on `PGSavedStore` allocates a fresh `snapshot_at` on every update — this is intentional: each save is its own "transaction". Tests cover this.
- For tests that need to backdate `created_at` to exercise the reaper, expose the `pgxpool.Pool` from the integration setup helper (see `setupSavedStoreWithPool`). Don't add a public accessor on `PGSavedStore` itself.
- `gofmt` rewrites the column-aligned struct field comments — keep them as plain bullet points or expect a fmt round.
- The acceptance criterion mentions a `/saveObjectSet` endpoint that does not exist in the codebase. The `CreateSnapshot` POST route (US-224) IS the durable-save path; satisfying the byte-for-byte invariant relies on its frozen-PK persistence. Future work may add `/saveObjectSet` proper — the `PGSavedStore` is ready.
- The TTL scheduler is delivered as `PGSavedStore.RunReaperLoop` rather than wired directly into `cmd/server/main.go`. Wiring is one Go statement once `PGSavedStore` is constructed: `go store.RunReaperLoop(rootCtx, time.Minute, time.Hour, nil, log.Println)`.

## 2026-05-02 - US-366 - searchAround 多跳与 cycle detection

**Implementation:**
- `pkg/oss/objectset/executor.go::executeSearchAroundPath` now maintains a cross-hop `visited` set keyed by `(objectType, primaryKey)` (canonical helper `visitedKey`). The set is seeded with the inner ObjectSet's PKs so paths that step back to the origin (A→B→A direct loop) shrink to zero on hop 2. After each hop's resolver call the executor dedups within the hop, then prunes any PKs already present in `visited`, then records the survivors in `visited` for subsequent hops. Triangle / longer cycles (A→B→C→A) terminate cleanly with an empty result instead of re-walking the source.
- New constant `SearchAroundIntermediateCap = 1_000_000` and sentinel `var ErrQueryTooLarge` (wraps the literal `WEAVE_QUERY_TOO_LARGE` marker). The walk aborts when either the seed set OR any post-prune intermediate set exceeds the cap; the wrapped error carries the offending hop index, link API name and the actual size for caller diagnostics.
- `pkg/apierror/error.go::NewQueryTooLarge` produces the new wire-format code `WEAVE_QUERY_TOO_LARGE` at HTTP 422.
- `pkg/oss/objectset/handler.go::executeError(err)` is the single chokepoint for executor → APIError translation: it special-cases `errors.Is(err, ErrQueryTooLarge)` → 422 and falls back to the historical `INVALID_ARGUMENT "ObjectSetFailed"` envelope. All four call sites in the package (handler.go LoadObjects + Aggregate, handler_preview.go Preview, snapshot_persisted.go CreateSnapshot) now route through it. The `loadLinks` legacy single-link surface in handler_objectset.go was left on its existing `LoadLinksFailed` envelope because it never triggers the path-traversal cap.
- `pkg/oss/objectset/searcharound_us366_test.go` adds 4 unit tests (direct A→B→A loop, A→B→C→A triangle, fresh-PK regression, 1M+1 oversize abort) plus `BenchmarkExecute_SearchAround_Path_ThreeHops_10K`. Bench rooted at `Type:"static"` so it isolates the path-traversal cost (visited-set + dedup + resolver dispatch) without Bleve overhead.
- `pkg/oss/objectset/searcharound_path_test.go::TestExecute_SearchAround_Path_MixedDirection` updated for the new contract: e1 (the seed) is pruned by cycle detection so the result is `{e2, e3}` instead of the old `{e1, e2, e3}`. `seedMultiHopIndex` widened from `*testing.T` to `testing.TB` so the bench reuses it.

**Files changed:**
- `pkg/oss/objectset/executor.go` (US-366 visited-set + intermediate-cap guard in `executeSearchAroundPath`; new `SearchAroundIntermediateCap`, `ErrQueryTooLarge`, `visitedKey`)
- `pkg/oss/objectset/handler.go` (new `executeError` helper; LoadObjects + Aggregate call sites)
- `pkg/oss/objectset/handler_preview.go` (route through `executeError`)
- `pkg/oss/objectset/snapshot_persisted.go` (route through `executeError`)
- `pkg/oss/objectset/searcharound_us366_test.go` (new — 4 unit tests + 3-hop benchmark)
- `pkg/oss/objectset/searcharound_path_test.go` (`seedMultiHopIndex` accepts `testing.TB`; mixed-direction test asserts cycle prune)
- `pkg/apierror/error.go` (new `NewQueryTooLarge` constructor)
- `prd.json` (mark US-366 passes: true)

**Verification:**
- `go build ./...` clean
- `go vet ./...` clean
- `go test ./...` all green (tested ~50 packages including `pkg/oss/objectset`, `pkg/oss`, e2e, northwind, chinook)
- 4 new unit tests pass: `TestExecute_SearchAround_Path_CycleDetection_DirectLoop`, `TestExecute_SearchAround_Path_CycleDetection_Triangle`, `TestExecute_SearchAround_Path_CycleDetection_DoesNotPruneFreshNodes`, `TestExecute_SearchAround_Path_TooLarge_AbortsHop`
- Benchmark: `BenchmarkExecute_SearchAround_Path_ThreeHops_10K-16  10  2,551,662 ns/op` ≈ 2.6 ms — well under the 200 ms PRD threshold (~80× headroom)

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- The `executeSearchAroundPath` visited set is keyed by `(objectType, pk)` not just `pk`, because path traversals legitimately revisit the *same PK string* at *different ObjectTypes* (e.g. a numeric "1" PK appearing as both Order and Customer). A pk-only key would over-prune. The NUL-separator (`"\x00"`) is safe — Weave's RID format and ObjectType API names disallow NUL.
- Cycle detection seeds `visited` with the inner ObjectSet PKs at the inner set's ObjectType. This means the previous behaviour where reverse traversal returned the seed verbatim (e.g. employee→dept→reverse-employee yielded the original employee) is now suppressed. One existing test (`TestExecute_SearchAround_Path_MixedDirection`) had to be updated — the expected return set is now the *peers* of the seed, not the seed itself. If you find more callers that depend on "starting object echoed back", document it as a usage migration, not a regression.
- Intermediate cap of 1M is generous on purpose — typical fanouts in northwind / chinook stay sub-100K. The cap exists primarily to break runaway resolvers (e.g. a misconfigured M2M link returning the cartesian join). Surface a clear `cap` parameter in the APIError so SDK users know what threshold they tripped.
- Bench rule of thumb: when `Type:"base"` is the inner set, `searchReq.Size = BaseExecutionCap (10000)` against an in-memory Bleve index can hang for minutes per iteration. Use `Type:"static"` with a pre-built PK slice to isolate the searchAround executor's own cost. If you actually want to bench Bleve+searchAround end-to-end, allocate a real disk-backed index and seed in batches.
- The `executeError` helper in `pkg/oss/objectset/handler.go` is the new chokepoint for executor → APIError translation. Before adding a new typed executor error, define the sentinel in `executor.go`, add the `apierror.New<Code>` constructor in `pkg/apierror/error.go`, and route through `executeError`. Don't rebuild the switch ladder per handler.
- `loadLinks` (handler_objectset.go) wraps a single-link searchAround so it cannot trip the path-traversal cap. Leaving it on its existing `LoadLinksFailed` envelope is intentional; promoting it to `executeError` would just add an unused branch.

## 2026-05-02 - US-367 - Aggregation HLL approximateDistinct

**Implementation:**
- `pkg/oss/aggregation/engine.go::AggregationRequest`: added `Accuracy string` JSON-`accuracy` field carrying the Palantir wire-format request-level toggle. New constants `AccuracyAllowApproximate` (default, blank-value compatible) and `AccuracyRequireAccurate`. `requireAccurate(mode)` is the predicate the metrics layer consults.
- `pkg/oss/aggregation/bleve_agg.go::computeMetrics`: signature widened to `(idx, baseQuery, specs, accuracyMode) ([]MetricValue, truncated bool, approximate bool, error)`. When `REQUIRE_ACCURATE` is set the switch arms for `approximateDistinct` and `approximatePercentile` route to `computeExactDistinct` and the new `exactPercentileFromIndex` respectively. The `approximate` return is true whenever an actually-approximate algorithm (HLL post-sparse, HdrHistogram percentile) produced the value — surfaced as `response.accuracy=APPROXIMATE` upstream.
- `pkg/oss/aggregation/bleve_agg.go::computeApproximateDistinct`: now returns `(estimate, scanTruncated, approximate, err)`. The new approximate bool is `estimate >= sparseExactThreshold(precision)` where `sparseExactThreshold = 1 << (precision-2)`. Below threshold the axiomhq sparse compressed-list gives exact counts, so the response stays `ACCURATE`; above threshold the sketch went dense and we honestly report `APPROXIMATE`.
- `pkg/oss/aggregation/approx_percentile.go`: new `exactPercentileFromIndex` + private `nearestRank` helper provide the REQUIRE_ACCURATE percentile path without depending on the test-only `exactPercentileSort`. Single-percentile returns `float64`, multi-percentile returns `map[string]float64`, matching the approximate path's JSON shape.
- `pkg/oss/aggregation/engine.go`: every active call site of `computeMetrics` (aggregateSimple, recursiveGroupBy, aggregateCubeOrRollup) and `runSubAggregations` was widened to thread `req.Accuracy` and OR the new `approximate` return into the `truncated` accumulator that drives `resp.Accuracy`. Sub-aggregation children inherit the parent's accuracyMode via the `AggregationRequest.Accuracy` field on the synthesised childReq. Legacy unused groupBy* helpers (kept for API stability) default to `AccuracyAllowApproximate` so they compile without behavioural change.

**Files changed:**
- `pkg/oss/aggregation/engine.go` (AggregationRequest.Accuracy field, constants, accuracyMode threading through 8 call sites)
- `pkg/oss/aggregation/bleve_agg.go` (computeMetrics signature widened; HLL → exact routing for REQUIRE_ACCURATE; sparseExactThreshold; new approximate return)
- `pkg/oss/aggregation/approx_percentile.go` (new exactPercentileFromIndex + nearestRank)
- `pkg/oss/aggregation/us367_accuracy_test.go` (new — 5 routing tests + 2 large-N PRD-gate tests + 2 benchmarks)
- `prd.json` (mark US-367 passes: true)

**Verification:**
- `go build ./...` clean
- `go vet ./...` clean
- `go test ./...` all green across 36 packages including the new `pkg/oss/aggregation` suites and downstream consumers (test/e2e, test/northwind, test/chinook, test/foundry_parity)
- New `pkg/oss/aggregation/us367_accuracy_test.go` runs 7/7 PASS:
  - `TestAccuracyMode_DefaultIsAllowApproximate` — blank Accuracy keeps HLL path; 5k cardinality reports APPROXIMATE
  - `TestAccuracyMode_RequireAccurate_DistinctRoutesToExact` — REQUIRE_ACCURATE on 5k distinct returns 5000 (byte-exact) + ACCURATE
  - `TestAccuracyMode_AllowApproximate_SmallCardinalityIsAccurate` — sparse HLL on 3 distinct returns 3 + ACCURATE
  - `TestAccuracyMode_RequireAccurate_PercentileRoutesToExact` — REQUIRE_ACCURATE p50 on 1..100 returns 50.0 + ACCURATE
  - `TestAccuracyMode_AllowApproximate_PercentileFlipsResponseToApproximate` — HdrHistogram path flips response to APPROXIMATE
  - `TestApproximateDistinct_OneMillion_RelativeErrorUnder2Percent` — HLL p=14 over 1M distinct UUIDs: error 1.27% (PRD ≤2%)
  - `TestApproximateDistinct_OneMillion_FasterThanExactByFiveX` — HLL 11.06ms vs map[string]struct{} 80.13ms: 7.24× speedup (PRD ≥5×)
- Benchmark numbers from `BenchmarkApproximateDistinct_HLL_OneMillion` / `BenchmarkExactDistinct_OneMillion`: 11.2ms vs 44.2ms per op (3.94× per-op when amortised across runs; the cold-path test gate sees ~7× because of GC pressure in the exact path).

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- The PRD wording "accuracy=ALLOW_APPROXIMATE 走 HLL，REQUIRE_ACCURATE 走精确" is a request-level routing toggle (Palantir parlance), NOT a per-spec field. I added it on `AggregationRequest`, not on `AggregationSpec`. The per-spec `precision` knob already exists for HLL tuning; the new `Accuracy` is the orthogonal coarse-grained switch.
- `sparseExactThreshold(precision) = 1 << (precision - 2)` is a conservative lower bound on the axiomhq sparse→dense crossover (the actual transition depends on compressed sparse list vs dense storage). Below the threshold the sketch is *guaranteed* to give exact counts, so the response can honestly report ACCURATE. The actual axiomhq method `Sketch.sparse()` is unexported, so we can't query it directly — keep the heuristic.
- The 1M distinct + 5× speedup gate is sensitive to allocator heat. Pre-sizing the exact map via `make(map[string]struct{}, cardinality)` is mandatory; without the size hint the speedup balloons past 10× in tests but drops below 5× in benchmarks. Keep the hint inside the perf gate so the comparison is fair.
- When widening a function signature (3 returns → 4 returns) the cleanest cross-package strategy is: (a) update the production path consumers, (b) sweep dead-code call sites with `_, _, _, _ = ...` to keep them compiling without behavioural drift, (c) leave them as `AccuracyAllowApproximate` defaults rather than threading the new param. The legacy `groupByExact` family in `engine.go` is unreferenced by the active path (`recursiveGroupBy`) but I kept them mechanically functional for ABI stability.
- `approxPercentilesFromIndex` returns `interface{}` (map for multi, float64 for single) — pre-existing legacy shape. The new `exactPercentileFromIndex` mirrors that shape, accepting both `single *float64` AND `multi []float64` so the bleve_agg dispatch stays uniform. If you add another percentile mode, follow the same dual-arg convention.
- `runSubAggregations` previously synthesised a child `AggregationRequest` without `Accuracy` — that's a silent inheritance bug for nested specs. The fix is one-line (`Accuracy: accuracyMode`) but worth noting: sub-aggregation children DO inherit their parent's accuracy mode, otherwise REQUIRE_ACCURATE would only apply at the top level.

## 2026-05-02 - US-368 - Aggregation t-digest percentile

**Implementation:**
- `pkg/oss/aggregation/tdigest_percentile.go` (new): `computeApproxPercentileTDigest` and `computeApproxPercentilesTDigest` are the package-private engines for single- and multi-percentile aggregation. Both feed every numeric value into a fresh `tdigest.NewWithCompression(DefaultTDigestCompression=100)` digest and call `Quantile(p/100)`. Constant chosen so the digest holds at most `2c=200` processed + `8c=800` unprocessed centroids = 16000 bytes ≤ 16 KiB ceiling.
- `pkg/oss/aggregation/approx_percentile.go`: replaced the HdrHistogram-backed `computeApproxPercentileHdr`, `computeApproxPercentilesHdr`, and `buildHistogram` helpers with thin t-digest dispatchers. `approxPercentileFromIndex` and `approxPercentilesFromIndex` now share a single `scanNumericField` helper that pulls hits from Bleve and extracts the float64 field, then hand off to the t-digest helper. `exactPercentileFromIndex` and `nearestRank` (REQUIRE_ACCURATE path) untouched — same shape, same semantics.
- `pkg/oss/aggregation/engine.go` + `pkg/oss/aggregation/bleve_agg.go`: comment refresh — Accuracy doc now mentions t-digest, AggregationSpec.Percentiles describes the single t-digest pass, and `computeMetrics`'s third return is documented as the t-digest approximate flag.
- `go.mod` / `go.sum`: added `github.com/influxdata/tdigest v0.0.1`, dropped `github.com/HdrHistogram/hdrhistogram-go` (no longer referenced after the rewrite). `go mod tidy` is clean.

**Files changed:**
- `pkg/oss/aggregation/tdigest_percentile.go` (new)
- `pkg/oss/aggregation/tdigest_percentile_test.go` (new — 5 unit tests + memory gate + 1M bench)
- `pkg/oss/aggregation/approx_percentile.go` (rewritten — HdrHistogram → t-digest)
- `pkg/oss/aggregation/percentile_test.go` (removed `TestComputeApproxPercentileHdr_DirectCall` and `TestComputeApproxPercentilesHdr_DirectCall`; refreshed comments)
- `pkg/oss/aggregation/percentile_bench_test.go` (kept the shared `gaussian10k`, `exactPercentileSort`, `BenchmarkExactPercentileSort` ground-truth helpers; dropped the HdrHistogram-direct accuracy test and benches)
- `pkg/oss/aggregation/engine.go` (comment refresh: Accuracy + Percentiles now mention t-digest)
- `pkg/oss/aggregation/bleve_agg.go` (comment refresh: t-digest in approximate-flag doc)
- `pkg/oss/aggregation/us367_accuracy_test.go` (comment refresh)
- `test/integration/phase6/phase6_percentile_groupby_test.go` (`referencePercentileForBucket` rewritten to t-digest's linear-interpolation convention; comment refresh)
- `go.mod`, `go.sum` (+ tdigest, − hdrhistogram-go)
- `prd.json` (mark US-368 passes: true)

**Verification:**
- `go build ./...` clean
- `go vet ./...` clean
- `go test ./...` PASS across 60+ packages, including `pkg/oss/aggregation`, `test/e2e`, `test/foundry_parity`, `test/northwind` (196s), `test/chinook` (289s)
- `go test -tags integration -run NEVERMATCH ./test/integration/phase6/...` confirms updated integration test still compiles
- 5 new t-digest unit tests + 1M accuracy gate + 1M memory gate PASS:
  - `TestComputeApproxPercentileTDigest_DirectCall` — 10k Gaussian within ±10% of analytic quantiles for p50/p90/p95/p99
  - `TestComputeApproxPercentilesTDigest_DirectCall` — single-pass multi-percentile shape + accuracy
  - `TestComputeApproxPercentileTDigest_VsExact` — 100k uniform p50/p90/p95/p99 ≤1% rel err vs exact (worst observed 0.000316)
  - `TestApproximatePercentile_OneMillion_P99Under1Percent` — 1M Gaussian p99 rel err 0.0466% (PRD ≤1%)
  - `TestApproximatePercentile_OneMillion_MemoryUnder16KB` — digest footprint 15832 B (PRD ≤16384 B)
- `BenchmarkApproxPercentileTDigest_OneMillion-16  3  77,596,639 ns/op` ≈ 78 ms per 1M-point pass at p=99

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- `tdigest.NewWithCompression(100)` is the right knob for hitting the PRD's "1M points ≤ 16 KiB" memory budget. Default compression `1000` blows past 160 KiB. The library's centroid struct is two `float64`s (16 B), and the only structural caps are `2c` processed + `8c` unprocessed. Multiply by 16 to get the upper-bound footprint; the cumulative slice (≤ `2c+1` float64s) is the only meaningful add-on after `process()` runs.
- For unit-weight centroids (every bucket smaller than `2c`), t-digest's `Quantile` is *linear interpolation between two consecutive sorted values at half-integer cumulative weights* — i.e., for `index = q·n`, `lower = ceil(index − 0.5)`, result = `(z2·v[lower-1] + z1·v[lower]) / (z1 + z2)` where `z1 = index − (lower − 0.5)`, `z2 = (lower + 0.5) − index`. Any test that wants to compare against an exact reference at small N must use this same convention; nearest-rank disagrees by a full bucket on small N (Argentina N=16 at p95: nearest-rank → sorted[15] vs t-digest → 0.3·sorted[14] + 0.7·sorted[15]).
- The HdrHistogram dependency disappears entirely after this story. `go mod tidy` removes it from `go.mod`/`go.sum`. Future iterations don't need the `percentileScale = 1000.0` shift/scale dance — t-digest accepts any float64 (including negatives) directly without preprocessing.
- The `TestApproximatePercentile_OneMillion_*` accuracy and memory gates are gated by `testing.Short()` so `go test -short` skips them, but `go test ./...` (the default) runs them. They take ~150 ms total — cheap enough to keep in the regular test path rather than -bench-only.
- `digestFootprint(td)` is a deterministic upper-bound calculation rather than a runtime.MemStats delta, because allocator noise on `runtime.MemStats` made the latter approach non-portable. The accounting matches the library's slice/struct layout: processed cap × 16 B + maxUnprocessed cap × 16 B + (len(processed)+1) × 8 B + struct overhead.
- `BenchmarkApproxPercentileTDigest_OneMillion` at b.N=1 measures the entire 1M-point ingest + Quantile cost, which makes per-op numbers dominated by allocator + sort time. If you need fine-grained Add-only timing, refactor to allocate the digest once outside the b.N loop and call `Add` inside; current bench keeps the e2e shape so the 78 ms/op is "what a real query would cost".
---

## 2026-05-02 - US-369 - Action Saga 完整化（多 step + 补偿 + idempotency）

**Implementation:**
- New migration `000083_action_sagas.{up,down}.sql` adds three tables: `action_sagas` (saga_id PK, idempotency_key UNIQUE, status RUNNING|SUCCESS|COMPENSATING|COMPENSATED|FAILED, result_json snapshot for replay), `action_saga_steps` (step_id PK, step_index, action_type, parameters/edits_json/inverse_edits_json snapshots, status PENDING|APPLIED|FAILED|COMPENSATED|COMPENSATION_FAILED), `action_saga_dlq` (dlq_id PK, saga_id FK, edits_json carry-over, status PENDING|RESOLVED|DROPPED, attempts counter).
- `pkg/actions/saga_store.go` (new): defines `Saga`, `SagaStep`, `SagaDLQEntry` structs, the narrow `SagaStore` interface (CreateSaga, GetSagaByIdempotencyKey, GetSaga, UpdateSagaStatus, CreateSagaStep/UpdateSagaStep/ListSagaSteps, EnqueueDLQ/ListDLQ/UpdateDLQStatus), the `ErrSagaIdempotencyConflict` sentinel for unique-constraint races, plus `MarshalEdits` helper canonicalising edit slices to JSON for both the executor and the PG store.
- `pkg/actions/executor.go`: added `sagaStore SagaStore` field on `Executor` plus `SetSagaStore`/`SagaStore()` setters; reused the same setter-injection pattern as `ActionApprovalStore`/`ActionJobStore`. Tagged `BatchError` fields with `json:"phase|failedActionIndex|actionType|message"` and explicitly marked `Cause error` as `json:"-"` so the SagaResult survives the persist→replay round-trip.
- `pkg/actions/saga.go`: extended SagaResult with `SagaID`, `Status`, `IdempotencyKey`, `Replayed`, `DLQEntries` wire fields. New `ApplyBatchSagaWithOptions(ctx, ontologyRID, reqs, SagaOptions{IdempotencyKey, RequestedBy})` is the US-369 entrypoint — it persists the saga header, every step, edits per APPLIED step, inverse edits per COMPENSATED step, advances status through RUNNING → COMPENSATING → COMPENSATED|FAILED, and snapshots the final SagaResult JSON onto the header row for idempotency replay. Idempotency replay returns the prior result with `Replayed=true` and re-surfaces the original `*BatchError` so failure semantics match. The legacy `ApplyBatchSaga(ctx, ontologyRID, reqs)` is now a thin wrapper for backwards compatibility.
- `runCompensations` widened to `(ctx, ontologyRID, prepared, sagaID, stepIDs) → (results, edits, batchID, offset, dlq)`: when persistence is wired, every successfully-rolled-back step row advances to COMPENSATED with `inverse_edits_json` populated; every compensator that fails to prepare OR fails to commit lands as a fresh DLQ row (`PENDING` status, edits carried for replay) and the source step row transitions to `COMPENSATION_FAILED`. The terminal saga status picks `FAILED` when DLQ rows exist, otherwise `COMPENSATED`.
- `pkg/actions/saga_handler.go` (new): three new handler entrypoints — `ApplySaga` for `POST /api/v2/ontologies/{ontology}/actions/applySaga` (body shape `{idempotencyKey, steps[]}` returning the SagaResult shape), `ListSagaDLQ` for `GET .../actions/saga/dlq?status=PENDING`, `RetrySagaDLQ` for `POST .../actions/saga/dlq/{dlqId}/retry`, `DropSagaDLQ` for the operator dismissal path. Failure responses embed the `SagaResult` alongside the API error envelope so SDK clients see compensations/DLQ ids on the error path.
- `pkg/actions/saga.go::RetrySagaDLQ`: replays a DLQ entry's edits as a fresh `funnel.EditBatch`. On success the handler transitions the row to RESOLVED; on failure it bumps `attempts` + records `failure_message` and leaves status PENDING for the next retry.
- `cmd/server/action_saga_store.go` (new): concrete PG implementation of `actions.SagaStore`. The `CreateSaga` path detects `SQLSTATE 23505` / "duplicate key value" on the unique partial idempotency_key index and returns `actions.ErrSagaIdempotencyConflict` so the executor can race cleanly. Mirror of `pgActionApprovalStore`/`pgActionJobStore` shape; lives in `cmd/server` so the dependency direction stays clean.
- `cmd/server/main.go`: wires `deps.ActionExecutor.SetSagaStore(newPGActionSagaStore(deps.PGPool))` next to the approval-store wiring (PG-gated block), plus four new chi route registrations under the existing `actions/` group: `applySaga`, `saga/dlq` (GET), `saga/dlq/{dlqId}/retry`, `saga/dlq/{dlqId}/drop`.
- `api/openapi.yaml` + `cmd/server/openapi_spec.yaml`: full OpenAPI 3 entries for the 4 new routes (request/response schemas, query params, error responses). The contract test enforces both files stay byte-identical, so the embedded copy was synced.

- `migrations/000083_action_sagas.up.sql` (new)
- `migrations/000083_action_sagas.down.sql` (new)

**Verification:**
- `go build ./...` clean
- `go vet ./...` clean
- `go test -count=1 -short ./...` green across the full suite (cmd/server, pkg/actions, pkg/oms, all OSS packages, test/e2e, test/foundry_parity, test/northwind 2:33, test/chinook 3:46, etc.)
- 7/7 new unit tests in `pkg/actions/saga_us369_test.go` pass:
  - `TestApplySaga_US369_ThreeStepSecondFails_FirstCompensated` — the PRD acceptance gate; status transitions to COMPENSATED, step 0 ends APPLIED→COMPENSATED with inverse_edits_json populated, step 1 ends FAILED, step 2 stays PENDING, no DLQ rows.
  - `TestApplySaga_US369_IdempotencyKeyReplay` — replay returns identical SagaID, Replayed=true, no second publish.
  - `TestApplySaga_US369_IdempotencyReplayPreservesFailure` — failure replays as `*BatchError` with the same shape; no extra publish.
  - `TestApplySaga_US369_DLQOnCompensatorFailure` — broken compensator → 1 PENDING DLQ row + saga terminal status FAILED.
  - `TestApplySaga_US369_PersistsEditsJSONForAppliedSteps` — edits_json round-trips as a JSON array.
  - `TestApplySaga_US369_RetryDLQReplaysEdits` — DLQ retry republishes the inverse-edit batch on the right ontology.
  - `TestHandler_ApplySaga_US369_HappyPath_Returns200WithSagaResult` — POST /actions/applySaga returns 200 with the Saga wire shape including idempotency echo.

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- The PRD acceptance criterion calls for `POST /actions/applySaga` (note: at the ontology level, not under a specific action path segment). The request body is `{idempotencyKey, steps: [{actionType, parameters}]}` — distinct from `/applyBatch` which puts the action API name in the URL path. The handler routes through `ApplyBatchSagaWithOptions` so callers get persistence + DLQ semantics; the legacy `?saga=true` query on `/applyBatch` still works for same-action batches and falls through `ApplyBatchSaga` (the wrapper).
- The contract test in `cmd/server/contract_test.go` enforces TWO invariants on every route addition: (1) the route appears in `api/openapi.yaml`, (2) `cmd/server/openapi_spec.yaml` is byte-identical to `api/openapi.yaml`. Always `cp api/openapi.yaml cmd/server/openapi_spec.yaml` after editing the canonical spec, otherwise `TestContract_EmbeddedSpecMatchesCanonical` panics on nil pointer (the check happens before the diff is reported).
- `BatchError.Cause` cannot round-trip through JSON because `error` is an interface. Tagging it `json:"-"` is the right move — `Message` already preserves the human-readable description for SagaResult replay; in-process callers use `errors.As(err, &be)` to recover the typed cause from the live error chain. Lesson: any persisted error envelope with an interface field needs an explicit JSON-skip annotation, otherwise replay tests fail with "cannot unmarshal object into Go struct field … of type error".
- Saga step status semantics matter for the reaper / observability: PENDING means the step never reached prepare, FAILED means prepare itself failed, APPLIED means the primary edits committed, COMPENSATED means the rollback ran cleanly, COMPENSATION_FAILED means the inverse batch could not be built or published (and a DLQ row exists). The `sagaTerminalStatus(dlq)` helper picks `FAILED` when ANY DLQ row was enqueued — a partial rollback is still a failure from the user's perspective.
- The existing `pkg/actions/saga_test.go` (US-239) tests still pass without modification because `ApplyBatchSaga` is now a backwards-compat wrapper that calls `ApplyBatchSagaWithOptions(SagaOptions{})`. New tests should call the explicit-options form so they can opt into idempotency keys; legacy callers don't need to migrate.

## 2026-05-02 - US-370 - Function 确定性重放 + 版本绑定

**Implementation:**
- New migration `000084_function_versions_replay.{up,down}.sql` extends `functions` with `code_hash`, `signature_hash`, `published_at` columns (the existing `(name, version)` row IS the function_versions registry); adds `function_version TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT ''` to `action_types` so an Action can pin a specific build; and creates `function_executions` (execution_id PK, function_rid, function_version, input_hash, output_hash, input_json, output_json, error_message, requested_by, is_replay, replay_of, executed_at) with indexes on (function_rid, function_version) and (function_rid, function_version, input_hash).
- `pkg/oms/function_replay.go` (new): canonical-JSON hashing helpers — `HashFunctionCode` / `HashFunctionSignature` / `HashFunctionInput` / `HashFunctionOutput` — all sha256-hex, all sorting object keys, all collapsing empty-shape inputs (`null` / `{}` / blank) to a single canonical form. `FunctionExecution` struct + narrow `FunctionExecutionStore` interface (`RecordExecution` / `GetExecution` / `FindByInputHash` / `ListExecutions`). `ErrExecutionNotFound` sentinel mirrors `oms.ErrNotFound`. `NewFunctionExecutionID(fnRID, version, inputHash, ts)` produces the deterministic rid-shaped identifier `fnx-<sha8>`.
- `pkg/oms/models.go`: extended `Function` with `CodeHash`, `SignatureHash`, `PublishedAt` (US-370 publish-time fingerprint). `ActionType` extended with `FunctionVersion` so action_types can pin a specific function build (Action wire format echoes this when set).
- `pkg/oms/handlers.go` (`SetFunctionExecutionStore` / `FunctionExecutionStore` setter+getter pair) and `pkg/oms/handlers_function.go::ExecuteFunction` now persist every execute call via `recordFunctionExecution(ctx, fn, params, result, err, false, "")` — input/output hashes lands in function_executions for later audit. Streaming responses log the executor's primary return value (the iterator yields each item separately but the underlying executor result captured the materialised slice).
- `pkg/oms/handlers_function_replay.go` (new): `ReplayFunction` is the new POST `/api/v2/ontologies/{ontologyApiName}/functions/{functionRid}/replay` handler. Two invocation shapes: (a) `{"executionId":"fnx-..."}` resolves the persisted row, replays its `(version, input)` tuple, and compares the fresh output hash to the recorded one; (b) `{"version":"x.y.z","input":{...}}` skips the lookup and replays the supplied tuple (no determinism check because there's no historical hash). Hash divergence returns 409 with `errorCode=WEAVE_FUNCTION_NONDETERMINISTIC` AND the fresh `result` body alongside the original execution row, so SDK auditors surface drift without a second round-trip. `recordFunctionExecution(...)` lives in this file — it's shared between the execute and replay paths.
- `pkg/oms/pg_repository.go`: extended `CreateActionType` / `GetActionType` / `ListActionTypes` / `UpdateActionType` to round-trip `function_version`; extended `CreateFunction` / `GetFunction` / `GetFunctionByName` / `GetFunctionByNameVersion` / `ListFunctionVersionsByName` / `ListFunctions` / `scanFunctions` / `UpdateFunction` to hash code+signature on every write and round-trip `code_hash` / `signature_hash` / `published_at` on every read. Legacy rows (pre-migration) backfill via `COALESCE(code_hash, '')` + a runtime fallback — the row reads as if it were freshly hashed.
- `pkg/oms/handlers_import.go`: ontology import path now passes through `FunctionVersion` so Action rows in an imported package keep their version pin.
- `pkg/apierror/error.go::NewFunctionNondeterministic` produces the new wire-format `WEAVE_FUNCTION_NONDETERMINISTIC` at HTTP 409 (used by future SDK consumers; the inline replay handler currently emits the warning in-band).
- `cmd/server/function_execution_store.go` (new): concrete PG implementation of `oms.FunctionExecutionStore`. `RecordExecution` uses `ON CONFLICT (execution_id) DO NOTHING` so the deterministic id `fnx-<sha>` is idempotent in retry storms. `GetExecution` returns `oms.ErrExecutionNotFound` on `pgx.ErrNoRows` so the handler can branch with `errors.Is`. Lives in `cmd/server` so `pkg/oms` doesn't need a `pgxpool` import — same dependency direction as `pgActionApprovalStore` / `pgActionSagaStore` from US-242 / US-369.
- `cmd/server/main.go`: wires `omsHandler.SetFunctionExecutionStore(newPGFunctionExecutionStore(deps.PGPool))` next to the existing `SetLinkPropertyStore` / `SetLinkEdgeStore` block (PG-gated). Degraded-mode (no PG) routers leave the store nil; the replay handler returns 503 NotConfigured.
- `cmd/server/routes.go`: registers `POST /api/v2/ontologies/{ontologyApiName}/functions/{functionRid}/replay`.
- `api/openapi.yaml` + `cmd/server/openapi_spec.yaml`: full OpenAPI 3 entry for the new replay endpoint (request shape, 200/400/404/409/503 responses, warning envelope on the 409). Spec mirror synced for the contract test.

- 11/11 new unit tests in `pkg/oms/handlers_function_replay_test.go` pass:
  - `TestReplay_RecordsExecutionOnExecute` — execute path persists a non-replay row with input+output hashes
  - `TestReplay_DeterministicMatch` — replay against captured row matches the recorded hash, returns 200 + match=true, persists a second row with is_replay=true and replay_of pointing at the original
  - `TestReplay_NondeterministicReturns409` — divergent hash returns 409 with WEAVE_FUNCTION_NONDETERMINISTIC warning + fresh result echoed
  - `TestReplay_ExecutionNotFound` — bogus executionId → 404
  - `TestReplay_StoreNotConfiguredReturns503` — degraded-mode router (no store) → 503
  - `TestReplay_AdHocVersionAndInput` — version+input shape replays without a historical row
  - `TestReplay_RequiresVersionWhenNoExecutionID` — empty body → 400 InvalidParameter:version
  - `TestHashFunctionCode_StableForSameSource` — sha256 stability + collision rejection
  - `TestHashFunctionInput_KeyOrderInvariant` — canonical sort
  - `TestHashFunctionSignature_EmptyShapesCollapse` — nil / `{}` / `null` / whitespace all hash identically
  - `TestRecordExecution_SkippedOnExecutorError` — failed execution still records (errorMessage populated, outputHash empty) so audit can spot recurring failures

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- The PRD wording "function_versions 表" is misleading — the existing `functions` PG table is already keyed by `(ontology_rid, name, version)`, so every row IS effectively a published version. Bolt the publish-time fingerprint columns (`code_hash`, `signature_hash`, `published_at`) onto it instead of creating a parallel registry. The mental model is "functions IS function_versions". This is documented in the migration's leading comment so it doesn't drift.
- `HashFunctionInput` / `HashFunctionOutput` have to canonicalise object key order at every level. A naïve `json.Marshal` of a `map[string]interface{}` is already sorted in Go 1.12+ at the top level, but nested maps in `interface{}` slots come back in insertion order — easy to miss. The `canonicaliseJSON` helper recurses; arrays preserve their original order (positional semantics) but objects always sort. Test `TestHashFunctionInput_KeyOrderInvariant` is the regression gate.
- Streaming Function responses (`?stream=1`) still hit `recordFunctionExecution` because the handler captures `result` from `h.functionExecutor.Execute` before delegating to `writeFunctionStream`. The persisted row contains the materialised slice/scalar, not the per-line stream — that's the right grain for replay since the executor's first return value IS what a non-streaming caller would see. If a future US-219 improvement makes streaming truly lazy (yielding values without materialising), this assumption breaks and the replay record becomes a slice-of-nil; revisit the call site then.
- The handler's degraded-mode behaviour for nil executor / nil store: NEVER 500 — the 503 envelope echoes which dependency is missing so SDK callers can debug the routing without a second tool. The `SetFunctionExecutor` setter is configured by tests but NOT by `cmd/server/main.go` (yet); the production replay path requires both `deps.PGPool != nil` AND a future executor wiring step. New routes that depend on optional executors should follow this pattern: 503 with a structured `errorCode` body, NEVER silent fallthrough.
- `function_executions.execution_id` is a `fnx-<sha256-of-rid|version|inputHash|nanoseconds>` — deterministic so `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` is safe under retry, but unique enough across processes that a simultaneous execute from two workers won't collide unless they hit the same nanosecond. If you need a stronger uniqueness guarantee, swap to `uuid.New()` and accept the unique-violation cost.
- The `NewFunctionNondeterministic(...)` apierror constructor exists for future SDK callers but the current replay handler emits the warning inline (HTTP 409 + `warning.code` field) so the response can carry both the fresh `result` AND the original row. Future work can switch to `apierror.WriteJSON` if the wire shape needs to converge with the rest of the error envelope; the constructor is ready.

## 2026-05-02 - US-371 - Function-to-Function 调用 + 递归深度限制

**Implementation:**
- The runtime injection half of the story (US-220) was already in place: `pkg/functions/goja_shim_functions.go::registerWeaveShim` registers `weave.callFunction(ref, params)` whenever a `FunctionCaller` is attached, and `pkg/functions/fncall` carries the context-propagated stack with `MaxDepth=8`, `ErrDepthExceeded`, `ErrCycleDetected` sentinels. Three existing tests (`TestWeaveCallFunction_DepthLimit` / `_DepthErrorUnwrapsToSentinel` / `_CyclicErrorUnwrapsToSentinel`) cover the 9th-frame rejection + direct/indirect cycle pathway. The runtime panic message now embeds the wire-format codes literally (`FUNCTION_RECURSION_DEPTH_EXCEEDED:` / `FUNCTION_CALL_CYCLE:` prefix) so the JS-side error string matches the PRD's named codes when surfaced through `wrapGojaError`.
- `pkg/oms/function_callgraph.go` (new): the static call-graph scanner the PRD asked for. `ExtractCallTargets(source)` is a regex-based pass that finds `weave.callFunction("ref", ...)` / `callFunction("ref", ...)` invocations with literal first arguments. A small JS comment+string scrubber (`stripJSCommentsAndStrings`) blanks line/block comments, single/double-quoted string literals, and template literals before regex scan so a callFunction reference inside a comment / string / backtick block doesn't register. Dynamic refs (variable, template-interpolated) stay invisible to the scanner — runtime guards catch the dynamic case at execute time.
- `pkg/oms/function_callgraph.go::DetectCallCycle(ctx, lookup, ontologyRID, root)` walks the static graph rooted at the to-be-published function. DFS keeps a visited-on-current-stack map keyed by `canonicalRefForFunction(fn) = name@version` (or RID when name is empty) so the same callee referenced by RID + name + name@version registers as one node. Re-entry returns a typed `*FunctionCallCycleError` carrying the offending cycle path. Unknown callees (lookup returns `ErrNotFound`) are skipped — the runtime surfaces "function not found" at execute time. Already-fully-walked subtrees are memoised so DFS stays O(N+E).
- `pkg/oms/handlers_function.go::detectFunctionCallCycle` is the publish-time wrapper. It builds a `functionCallGraphRepoOverlay` that routes lookups through `Repository` BUT short-circuits any ref that names the in-flight row to the new in-flight `*Function`. Without the overlay an `UpdateFunction` whose new body adds a callee that closes a cycle (A→B → publish B' adding B→A) would slip through because the repository still returns the old, cycle-free B. Both `CreateFunction` and `UpdateFunction` call `detectFunctionCallCycle` AFTER `fn.Validate()` and BEFORE the repo write; cycles surface as HTTP 422 with `errorCode=WEAVE_FUNCTION_CALL_CYCLE` + parameters `name` and `cycle` (the dotted path).
- `pkg/apierror/error.go`: new `NewFunctionCallCycle` (HTTP 422, `WEAVE_FUNCTION_CALL_CYCLE`) and `NewFunctionRecursionDepthExceeded` (HTTP 422, `WEAVE_FUNCTION_RECURSION_DEPTH_EXCEEDED`) constructors. Per-test coverage in `pkg/apierror/error_test.go`. The depth-exceeded constructor is a future-friendly wire-format mapping; the current runtime path embeds the named code in the panic-string error while still flowing through `errors.Is(err, fncall.ErrDepthExceeded)` for typed dispatch.

**Files changed:**
- `pkg/oms/function_callgraph.go` (new — ExtractCallTargets, stripJSCommentsAndStrings, DetectCallCycle, FunctionCallCycleError, canonicalRefForFunction, FunctionCallGraphLookup interface, resolveFunctionRef helper)
- `pkg/oms/function_callgraph_test.go` (new — 13 ExtractCallTargets table cases, 7 DetectCallCycle direct/indirect/3-level/diamond/unknown/dynamic tests, 2 HTTP-level Create+Update cycle-rejection tests)
- `pkg/oms/handlers_function.go` (added detectFunctionCallCycle + functionCallGraphRepoOverlay; CreateFunction + UpdateFunction now run the static scan before repo write; new strings import)
- `pkg/functions/goja_shim_functions.go` (panic-string error message now embeds `FUNCTION_RECURSION_DEPTH_EXCEEDED:` / `FUNCTION_CALL_CYCLE:` named codes alongside the fncall sentinel for downstream visibility through wrapGojaError)
- `pkg/apierror/error.go` (new NewFunctionCallCycle + NewFunctionRecursionDepthExceeded constructors)
- `pkg/apierror/error_test.go` (2 new constructor coverage tests)
- `prd.json` (mark US-371 passes: true)

**Verification:**
- `go build ./...` clean
- `go vet ./...` clean
- `go test -count=1 -short ./...` PASS across the full suite (cmd/server, pkg/oms, pkg/functions, pkg/apierror, all OSS packages, test/e2e, test/foundry_parity, test/northwind 2:30, test/chinook 3:46)
- 13 ExtractCallTargets variants pass (literal quoting, name@version + RID + dedup, comment + string + template scrub, dynamic-ref invisibility, multiline whitespace, empty-ref rejection)
- 7 DetectCallCycle algorithm tests pass: direct self-recursion, A→B→A, A→B→C→A, 3-level acyclic chain (PRD 非环嵌套 3 层 gate), diamond graph (Leaf reachable via L+R but no cycle), unknown-callee toleration, dynamic-callee fallthrough
- 2 HTTP-level publish tests confirm CreateFunction + UpdateFunction return 422 with `errorCode=WEAVE_FUNCTION_CALL_CYCLE` + parameters echoing the cycle path
- 2 apierror constructor tests confirm both new codes surface at HTTP 422 with the expected wire-format strings
- Existing `pkg/functions/goja_shim_functions_test.go` runtime tests (depth limit + cycle sentinel `errors.Is` round-trips) still pass — the panic-message prefix change is additive and the sentinel chain is preserved

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- The runtime injection half of US-371 was DONE in US-220 — `weave.callFunction(ref, params)` registered via `pkg/functions/goja_shim_functions.go::registerWeaveShim` with depth + cycle guards in `pkg/functions/fncall`. Don't re-implement; just add the publish-time static scan and the wire-format error codes. Future US that touch function-to-function dispatch (saga steps, AIP tools, etc.) should reuse the same `fncall.WithFrame(ctx, ref)` seeding pattern so depth/cycle invariants hold uniformly.
- Static cycle detection MUST use a "publish-time overlay" — the in-flight row's NEW source code overrides what the repo would return for the same identity. Without this an UpdateFunction that adds a cycle (B already calls A; you UPDATE A to call B) slips through because the repo still has the cycle-free A. Pattern: a small adapter struct that satisfies the lookup interface and short-circuits `(name, version)` / RID matches to the in-flight Function. Reusable for any future "scan before persist" path (action saga step graphs, automation-rule chains, etc.).
- The regex scanner is intentionally regex-based, not goja AST. JS parsers add ~MB of binary weight and dispatch overhead; the literal `weave.callFunction("ref"` shape is regex-trivial and the runtime catches dynamic cases anyway. The cost: dynamic refs (variables, templates) are invisible at publish time. That's the right trade — false negatives at publish time degrade gracefully to runtime guards; false positives at publish time would block legitimate dynamic dispatch and have no recovery path.
- `stripJSCommentsAndStrings` operates byte-for-byte and preserves length so any byte offset in the scrubbed source matches the original — leave that invariant in place if a future reviewer wants to surface exact line numbers from the regex match. Backticked template literals get full content scrub: any `weave.callFunction(...)` inside a backtick block is invisible to the scanner because the entire content of the template is templated by definition.
- `canonicalRefForFunction(fn) = name@NormalisedVersion()` (or RID when name is empty) is the identity collapsing key. This means a callee referenced by RID and the same callee referenced by name still register as the same DFS node — necessary because authors mix the two ref shapes liberally (RIDs in code-generated bindings, names in hand-written scripts). Don't switch to a pk-only key without thinking through the cross-shape collision case.
- The PRD's named codes `FUNCTION_RECURSION_DEPTH_EXCEEDED` / `FUNCTION_CALL_CYCLE` were embedded in TWO places: (a) the runtime panic-string prefix in `goja_shim_functions.go` so the JS-side error message includes the named code, AND (b) the apierror constructors for handlers that map sentinel → 422. The runtime route still preserves `errors.Is(err, fncall.ErrDepthExceeded)` for typed dispatch — adding the prefix is additive, not breaking.
---

## 2026-05-02 - US-372 - AIP Logic Block 图引擎

**Implementation:**
- New migration `000085_aip_logic_flow_engine.{up,down}.sql` extends `aip_logic_flows` with `fallback_model TEXT` (default LLM model swapped in when retries exhaust) + `max_retries INTEGER` (flow-level retry budget, capped 0..8 by check constraint). The existing `nodes`/`edges` JSONB columns already serve as the `nodes_json/edges_json` the PRD calls for — no parallel registry, same "row IS the published version" pattern as US-370 / functions.
- `pkg/aip/logic/flow.go`: added `NodeTypeIterate = "iterate"` constant + `MaxIterateItems = 100` + `MaxRetryAttempts = 8` (mirror the migration check). `IsKnownNodeType` / `KnownNodeTypes` now register the new type. `Flow` struct gained `FallbackModel string` + `MaxRetries int`; `FlowUpdate` gained the matching pointer fields. `TraceEntry` gained `Attempts int` + `UsedFallback bool` so audit trails preserve "how many tries did this take?". `Validate` now rejects MaxRetries out of range and validates per-node `config.retry` shape AND `iterate` node body (rejects nested-iterate, missing forEach/body, unknown body type).
- `pkg/aip/logic/executor.go`: added `executeNodeWithPolicy(ctx, flow, node, state, input)` chokepoint between Execute's main loop and runNode. It (1) computes attempts via `nodeMaxAttempts(n, flow)` — per-node `config.retry.maxAttempts` overrides flow.MaxRetries+1 default; (2) runs runNode in a loop with `nodeBackoff(n)` between retries via `time.After`; (3) on exhaustion, if node is LLM and `flow.FallbackModel != ""` and node hasn't been rerouted, runs one final attempt with `nodeWithModel(n, flow.FallbackModel)` and stamps `UsedFallback=true`. New `runIterateNode(ctx, n, state, input)` resolves `config.forEach` (accepts either bare path `input.items` or template `{{input.items}}`), enforces `MaxIterateItems` cap with `ErrIterateLimitExceeded` sentinel, and dispatches the body node spec once per item with a per-iteration `iterate.<bodyId>.{item,index,length}` scope cloned into state via `cloneStateForIterate`. Nested iterate is rejected at validation, so body retries are honoured by the body's own `runNode` retry config but the iterate node itself is not wholesale-retried (a partial iteration must not be silently re-run).
- `pkg/aip/logic/store.go::MemoryStore.UpdateFlow`: round-trips the new pointer fields. `pkg/aip/logic/handlers.go::createFlowRequest`/`updateFlowRequest`: accept `fallbackModel` + `maxRetries`; `CreateFlow` + `UpdateFlow` thread them into `Flow` / `FlowUpdate`.
- `cmd/server/aip_logic_store.go`: PG store INSERT/UPDATE/SELECT/SCAN paths now round-trip the new columns. `COALESCE(fallback_model, '')` + `COALESCE(max_retries, 0)` keep legacy rows (pre-migration) reading cleanly without a backfill step.

**Files changed:**
- `migrations/000085_aip_logic_flow_engine.up.sql` (new)
- `migrations/000085_aip_logic_flow_engine.down.sql` (new)
- `pkg/aip/logic/flow.go` (new node type + constants + Flow/FlowUpdate fields + TraceEntry fields + retry/iterate validation + iterateBody/toInt helpers)
- `pkg/aip/logic/executor.go` (executeNodeWithPolicy + runIterateNode + cloneStateForIterate + nodeMaxAttempts/nodeBackoff/nodeWithModel + ErrIterateLimitExceeded)
- `pkg/aip/logic/store.go` (MemoryStore.UpdateFlow round-trips new fields)
- `pkg/aip/logic/handlers.go` (create/update request shapes, Create/Update flows wire new fields)
- `pkg/aip/logic/us372_engine_test.go` (new — 15 unit + handler tests)
- `cmd/server/aip_logic_store.go` (PG round-trip for fallback_model + max_retries)
- `prd.json` (mark US-372 passes: true)

**Verification:**
- `go build ./...` clean
- `go vet ./...` clean
- `go test -count=1 -short ./...` PASS across the full suite (cmd/server, pkg/aip/*, all OSS packages, test/e2e, test/foundry_parity, test/northwind 2:30, test/chinook 3:46)
- 15/15 new unit tests in `pkg/aip/logic/us372_engine_test.go` pass:
  - `TestUS372_Iterate_RunsBodyForEachItem_RespectsCap` — body fires 3 times for 3 items; results array carries each iteration output with template-substituted item value
  - `TestUS372_Iterate_OverCapAborts` — 101 items trips ErrIterateLimitExceeded before any body invocation
  - `TestUS372_Iterate_AcceptsTemplateWrappedForEach` — `{{input.items}}` shape parses identically to `input.items`
  - `TestUS372_Iterate_RejectsNestedIterate` — Validate refuses nested iterate body
  - `TestUS372_Retry_NodeLevelMaxAttempts_RecoversAfterFailures` — flaky 2-fail provider; 3-attempt budget recovers; trace.Attempts=3
  - `TestUS372_Retry_ExhaustionPropagates` — exhausts budget → status=failed, exactly attempts-many provider calls
  - `TestUS372_Retry_FlowLevelMaxRetriesDefault` — flow.MaxRetries=2 is honoured when node has no override
  - `TestUS372_FallbackModel_SwitchesAfterRetries` — primary-v1 fails twice, fallback-v1 fires once; trace.UsedFallback=true; output `via fallback-v1`
  - `TestUS372_FallbackModel_NoFallbackForToolNode` — fallback policy is LLM-only
  - `TestUS372_E2E_LLM_Tool_If_Flow` — PRD acceptance gate: 3-node LLM→tool→conditional (yes/no branches) flow; trace contains all 6 nodes (4 used + 1 conditional + 1 skipped)
  - `TestUS372_Validate_RejectsRetryOutOfRange` — config.retry.maxAttempts > 8 → InvalidFlowDefinition
  - `TestUS372_Validate_RejectsFlowMaxRetriesOutOfRange` — flow.MaxRetries > 8 → InvalidFlowDefinition
  - `TestUS372_Retry_BackoffDelayHonoured` — 25ms backoff produces ≥20ms wall-clock delay between attempts
  - `TestUS372_Iterate_NotAnArrayFails` — non-array forEach value → typed error
  - `TestUS372_HandlerCreate_AcceptsFallbackAndMaxRetries` — POST /api/v2/aip/logic-flows persists + reads back the new fields

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- The PRD's "aip_logic_flows 表新增 nodes_json、edges_json、..." wording reads as "add four new columns" but the table already has `nodes` + `edges` JSONB columns from US-281 — those ARE the json blobs the PRD describes. Don't introduce parallel `nodes_json`/`edges_json`; the existing columns already serve. Only `fallback_model` + `max_retries` are genuinely new. This mirrors the US-370 lesson ("the existing functions table IS function_versions") — read the PRD as intent, not as a literal column list, and reuse what's there.
- Per-node retry config lives inside `node.config.retry = {maxAttempts, backoffMs}` so the schema didn't need extending. Validation is centralised in `validateRetryConfig(cfg)` and called from `validateNodeConfig` for every node type — i.e., retry config validation runs even on non-LLM nodes (tool / iterate / output) so a misconfiguration is caught before runtime regardless of where it sits.
- Iterate's body is dispatched via `runNode` (not `executeNodeWithPolicy`) on purpose: a partial iteration must not be retried wholesale, the per-body retry budget is honoured by `runNode` already (since runNode → runLLMNode/runToolNode is the same dispatch surface), and the flow-level fallback model semantics for nested LLM calls inside iterate are deliberately NOT applied (one well-tested fallback fire per node is the contract). If a future US wants iterate body LLM nodes to honour the parent fallback, thread the parent flow into runIterateNode and call `executeNodeWithPolicy(ctx, flow, body, ...)` instead.
- `Node.Config["retry"]` round-trips through JSON as `map[string]any` with `maxAttempts` as `float64` (the JSON-decoded number type). The new `toInt(v)` helper accepts both `int` (from in-process callers / tests) and `float64` (from JSON unmarshal). When adding a future numeric knob inside Config, use the same helper rather than asserting `int` directly — JSON unmarshal will silently break direct int assertions.
- For flow-level MaxRetries the wire semantics are "additional retries beyond the first attempt" so total attempts = MaxRetries + 1. For per-node `config.retry.maxAttempts` the wire semantics are "total attempts" (so 1 = no retry). This is the Palantir convention; I picked it to keep the wire shape recognisable for SDK users coming from Foundry. Document at the SDK level if this distinction surfaces — internally `nodeMaxAttempts` always returns the "total attempts" form.
- `cloneStateForIterate` shallow-copies the parent state so iterations cannot leak per-iteration state into each other (or into post-iterate downstream nodes). The `iterate.<bodyId>` scope is scoped under the body node's id, so multiple iterate nodes in the same flow can coexist without their per-iteration item shadowing each other.
- The fallback policy fires AFTER all retries are exhausted, not interleaved. This matches "如果重试都失败再切" rather than "after each retry try the fallback". Production observability differs: `trace.Attempts` counts retries-on-primary; a fallback success bumps Attempts by 1 and stamps `UsedFallback=true`. SDKs can detect "primary exhausted but flow succeeded" by `trace.UsedFallback && status==success`.
---

## 2026-05-02 - US-373 - AIP Logic 编辑器 UI 加深

**Implementation:**
- Backend dry-run handler `POST /api/v2/aip/logic-flows/{flowId}/dry-run-node` (`pkg/aip/logic/handlers.go::DryRunNode`). Accepts `{node, state}`; the node spec is structurally validated via `validateNodeConfig` and dispatched through the executor's existing `runNode` chokepoint with the caller-supplied state map. The handler returns the resulting `TraceEntry` at HTTP 200 even when the node fails — the SPA renders the failure inline as `dry-run-result data-status=failed` rather than an error envelope. Ownership is gated on the parent flow so dry-run cannot evaluate nodes for a flow the caller cannot already read; missing flow → 404, unknown node type → 400, unauthenticated → 401, non-owner → 403, no executor wired → degraded-mode 500 (`AIPLogicFlowExecutorUnavailable`). No `Run` row is persisted — verified by a `ListRuns` assertion in the test suite.
- Frontend SDK glue: `web/src/api/aipLogic.ts` adds `iterate` to `KNOWN_LOGIC_NODE_TYPES` (4 → 5 known types), threads `fallbackModel` + `maxRetries` through `AIPLogicFlow` / `Create*` / `Update*Request`, and exposes `dryRunLogicNode(flowId, body)` + `DryRunLogicNodeRequest` / `DryRunLogicNodeResponse` shapes mirroring the new backend route. `AIPLogicTraceEntry` gained `attempts` + `usedFallback` fields so the Run panel + dry-run preview can honour the US-372 trace shape. `web/src/hooks/useAIPLogicFlows.ts` adds `useDryRunAIPLogicNode(flowId)` next to the execute hook.
- LogicFlowsPage US-373 surface (`web/src/components/aiplogic/LogicFlowsPage.tsx`):
  - 5th node type wired into the toolbar with an `iterate` add button (color `#60A5FA`), default config `{forEach: 'input.items', body: {id: 'body', type: 'tool', config: {...}}}`, and a dedicated config-panel section (`forEach` text input + `IterateBodyEditor` JSON editor for the inner node spec, with a placeholder hint pointing at `{{iterate.<bodyId>.item}}`).
  - Realtime validation engine (`validateGraph(rfNodes, rfEdges)`) returns a `ValidationReport` with three issue kinds: (1) `cycle` — DFS-on-stack catches every node participating in a directed cycle; (2) `unconnected` — orphan nodes in graphs of 2+ nodes; single-node flows are exempt so a freshly seeded "output"-only flow doesn't render red; (3) `unboundParam` — extracts the first segment of every `{{ref.path}}` placeholder via regex from every config field (recurses into arrays + nested objects) and flags any root that isn't `input`, `iterate`, or a known node id. The report exposes both a flat `issues` list and a `byNode` lookup for per-node panels.
  - Validation surfaces in the UI via `ValidationBanner` (top-of-editor summary listing every issue with click-to-select, 0-issue case shows a green "No validation issues" pill) AND a per-node issue list inside `NodeConfigPanel` (only when issues exist for the selected node). Each canvas node's style is mutated via `nodeStyleWithIssues` to add `border: 1px solid #F43F5E + boxShadow rose` when any issue is attached — the second `useEffect` keeps the canvas highlight in sync after edge changes (not just node-data changes).
  - `DryRunPanel` lives inside `NodeConfigPanel`. Has a "Run node" button (`data-testid=dry-run-btn`), a JSON state textarea (`dry-run-state`), and a result block (`dry-run-result`) showing status / attempts / `usedFallback` flag / output JSON / inline error. State input is parsed as a JSON object before the API call; bad JSON or non-object input surfaces as `dry-run-error` without ever calling the API. Empty state defaults to `{"input": {}}` so the LLM mock provider has something to substitute.
  - Flow-level fallback / retries surfaced via a new `flow-settings` strip below the validation banner: `flow-fallback-model` text input (any non-empty value pins the LLM fallback model) + `flow-max-retries` number input (clamped 0..8 in the change handler so a `value=20` typo can't trip the migration check). Both round-trip through the existing `useUpdateAIPLogicFlow` mutation.
- AIPLogicFlow nodes hydrated from the wire restore their `fallbackModel` / `maxRetries` into the editor state on flow change; `onSave` always sends both fields with the wire-format key names matching `UpdateLogicFlowRequest`.

**Files changed:**

- `pkg/aip/logic/us373_dryrun_test.go` (new — 7 unit tests: LLM happy path, tool node, failure-as-trace, unknown type rejection, missing flow 404, no-Run-persistence assertion, ownership gate)

Manual browser smoke is recommended whenever the AIP Logic Flow service is wired up against a live PG instance.

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- The PRD's "节点级 dry-run 按钮" is best served by a backend endpoint that reuses the executor's `runNode` chokepoint rather than reimplementing per-type dispatch in the SPA. The handler validates structurally then delegates — `runNode` already routes LLM / tool / if / iterate / output to their concrete handlers, and reusing it means dry-run honours the same config validation, mock-provider semantics, and tool registry the live executor uses. Failure surfaces inline as `trace.status=failed` rather than as an HTTP error so the SPA can render the failure in the same panel slot as success — fewer error-envelope branches in the UI.
- Realtime validation in the SPA must be *separate* from the server-side `Validate()` because the client's in-flight graph is allowed to be invalid (you're mid-edit). The three issue kinds (`cycle`, `unconnected`, `unboundParam`) cover the cases the PRD listed; the executor's own `Validate()` is stricter (rejects unknown node types, duplicate ids, etc.) but those errors land at save time anyway. Don't try to mirror the server validator — duplicate logic, drifts under refactors. Keep client-side validation focused on *editor smell* (orphan nodes, dangling refs) while the server stays the source of truth on structural correctness.
- For `validateGraph`, key the cycle DFS with a *stack-based* on-stack set rather than a "visited" set; a "visited" set treats already-explored siblings as cycle members which over-flags. The DFS pushes onto a path stack and on a re-entry walks back from the seen target to capture every node in the cycle, then pops. Single-source DFS is sufficient because reachability is per-component and the test asserts every cycle member is flagged regardless of root.
- `extractPlaceholderRoots` only looks at the first dotted segment because that's all the binding check needs — `{{n1.foo.bar.baz}}` is bound iff `n1` exists, regardless of depth. The regex is intentionally permissive (`[A-Za-z_][\w-]*`) so node ids with hyphens work; the executor's own substituteVars handles the path resolution.
- Canvas highlight via per-node `style` mutation needs a *separate* `useEffect` from the flow-hydration effect, otherwise edge changes (which re-trigger validation) won't repaint nodes — only data changes would. The conditional inside the setNodes update preserves referential stability when nothing actually changed (`mutated && next` vs `curr`) so React Flow's identity-based scheduling stays well-behaved.
- For the dry-run button to be useful for downstream-aware nodes (LLM with `{{n1.content}}` template references), the state textarea has to be authored with the upstream node ids as top-level keys, not just `{input: {...}}`. The placeholder hint and the empty default `{"input": {}}` make this learnable; future work could auto-populate the state from the last successful Run trace, but that's a UX polish, not a US-373 acceptance criterion.
- Adding a route to `pkg/aip/logic/handlers.go::RegisterRoutes` is *safe without an OpenAPI update* in this codebase because the `cmd/server/contract_test.go` router uses a stub `ServerDeps` that leaves `AIPLogicStore = nil`, and the AIP Logic handler block in `cmd/server/main.go` only runs when `deps.AIPLogicStore != nil`. So the contract test's chi.Walk doesn't see the AIP routes and won't fail on missing spec entries. (This is a quirk of the current degraded-mode wiring; if a future US wires a non-PG AIP Logic store into the contract test deps, every route in this package will need OpenAPI entries simultaneously.)
