## 2026-05-03 - US-382 - ObjectSet computeUsage / excludedItems

**Implementation:**
- `pkg/oss/aggregation/response.go`: replaced the legacy
  `ComputeUsage float64` field with a `*ComputeUsage` struct
  `{ScannedRows int64, DurationMs int64, Accuracy string}` and the
  matching JSON shape `computeUsage: {scannedRows, durationMs,
  accuracy}` per the Palantir V2 wire format. Pointer + omitempty so
  pre-stamp responses (e.g. raw merges in tests) still marshal cleanly.
- `pkg/oss/aggregation/engine.go`: added `ExcludedItems []string` to
  `AggregationRequest` and rewrote `AggregateWithQuery` to (a) record
  `startTime := time.Now()` at entry, (b) call new
  `applyExcludedItems(idx, baseQuery, req.ExcludedItems)` BEFORE any
  metric/facet runs, (c) compute `resp.ExcludedItems` from the actual
  intersection (not `len(req.ExcludedItems)` — duplicates and
  out-of-scope PKs contribute zero), (d) stamp
  `resp.ComputeUsage = &ComputeUsage{ScannedRows, DurationMs,
  Accuracy: resp.Accuracy}` after Accuracy is finalised.
  - `applyExcludedItems` deduplicates input, ignores blanks, runs a
    `bleve.NewConjunctionQuery(baseQuery, NewDocIDQuery(cleaned))`
    count search to learn the actual intersection size, then wraps
    `baseQuery` in a Boolean MUST + MUST_NOT so EVERY downstream code
    path (groupBy, sub-aggs, cube/rollup, derived path scopes) sees
    the same exclusion uniformly — no per-call-site forgetting.
  - `countScannedRows(idx, baseQuery)` runs a single `Size=0` search
    for the post-exclusion total. Errors swallow to 0 so the metering
    search never fails the surrounding aggregation (data is already
    produced at that point).
- `pkg/oss/objectset/handler.go`: added `ExcludedItems []string` to
  `AggregateObjectSetRequest` and forwarded it verbatim into the
  engine `AggregationRequest`. The Bleve-facet ObjectSet path
  inherits the engine's exclusion + computeUsage stamp for free.
- `pkg/oss/objectset/handler_aggregate_derived.go`: added
  `applyDerivedExcludedItems(pks, excluded)` mirror for the in-memory
  derived-field path with identical "intersection-count" semantics
  (dedup, blank-tolerant, out-of-scope-tolerant). The derived
  aggregator now (a) pre-filters `result.PrimaryKeys` BEFORE the
  base-field Bleve fetch (saves a multi-doc round-trip when many PKs
  are excluded), (b) populates `resp.ExcludedItems`, and (c) stamps
  `resp.ComputeUsage = &aggregation.ComputeUsage{ScannedRows: int64(len(rows)),
  DurationMs: time.Since(startTime).Milliseconds(), Accuracy:
  resp.Accuracy}`. Same envelope shape as the engine path so SDK
  clients see one wire format regardless of derived-vs-base routing.
- `pkg/oss/handlers_interface.go::mergeAggregationResponses`:
  extended the per-type aggregation merger to accumulate
  `ComputeUsage.ScannedRows + DurationMs` and collapse Accuracy to
  APPROXIMATE if either side is approximate. Without this the
  cross-type interface aggregation surface would have lost
  computeUsage entirely.
- `api/openapi.yaml` + `cmd/server/openapi_spec.yaml` (byte-identical
  per the existing contract test): documented the new computeUsage
  object shape, the new `excludedItems` integer response field, and
  the new `excludedItems[]` request property on both
  `AggregationRequest` and `AggregateObjectSetRequest`.

**Files changed:**

  - aggregation pkg (7):
    `TestAggregate_ExcludedItems_FiltersBeforeCount`,

    `TestAggregate_ComputeUsage_AccuracyMirrorsApproximate`,

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- The wire format change `computeUsage: float64` → `computeUsage:
  {scannedRows, durationMs, accuracy}` is a BREAKING change for any
  client that read the scalar form. The frontend never used it, the
  test fixture `us015_multi_groupby.json` was a doc-only sketch, and
  the only Go assertion was the existing
  `TestAggregate_AccuracyAndComputeUsage`. Future wire changes to
  shared response envelopes should grep `<FieldName>` first to
  enumerate downstream consumers — don't trust the absence of OpenAPI
  drift to mean nothing depends on the old shape.
- `excludedItems` has TWO matching axes that must stay in sync:
  request-side `AggregationRequest.ExcludedItems []string` (input)
  AND response-side `AggregationResponse.ExcludedItems int` (echo of
  what was actually filtered). Reporting `len(req.ExcludedItems)`
  blindly inflates the counter for duplicate / out-of-scope PKs;
  doing a Bleve intersection count is one extra Size=0 search per
  request and is the honest semantics. The same dedup-then-count
  semantics is replicated in `applyDerivedExcludedItems` for the
  in-memory derived path so the wire shape is uniform across both
  routings.
- The Boolean MUST + MUST_NOT exclusion wrap is a SINGLE place where
  `baseQuery` is replaced. Because every downstream path (cube,
  rollup, recursiveGroupBy, runSubAggregations, computeMetrics,
  facet entries) takes its query from the function-scoped
  `baseQuery` variable, the exclusion is uniformly visible without
  per-call-site plumbing. Pattern: when adding a new request-time
  filter that should apply globally to an aggregation tree, rewrite
  `baseQuery` once at the top of `AggregateWithQuery` instead of
  threading the filter into every helper.
- `countScannedRows` deliberately swallows errors to 0 — the
  metering search runs AFTER the response data is already
  populated. Failing the entire aggregation on a metering glitch
  would be worse than reporting a zero-count usage envelope.
  Apply the same "metering side-channel cannot fail the primary
  response" pattern to any future cost / quality fields tacked
  onto the same envelope.
- `*ComputeUsage` (pointer) + `omitempty` is the right shape for an
  optionally-populated envelope. Test code that constructs a raw
  `AggregationResponse{}` (e.g. `mergeAggregationResponses(nil-side,
  …)`) still marshals to a clean JSON without a stale zero-value
  `computeUsage: {scannedRows: 0, …}` polluting the wire. The
  `mergeAggregationResponses` arms guard on `a.ComputeUsage != nil
  || b.ComputeUsage != nil` so a degraded sub-response that lost its
  envelope doesn't force a phantom zero envelope onto the merged
  output.
- The existing OpenAPI contract test
  (`TestContract_EmbeddedSpecMatchesCanonical`) requires
  `cmd/server/openapi_spec.yaml` to be byte-identical to
  `api/openapi.yaml`. Always `cp api/openapi.yaml
  cmd/server/openapi_spec.yaml` after any spec edit; the contract
  test pre-existed and surfaces the drift cleanly with a one-line
  fix instruction.

## 2026-05-03 - US-383 - Ontology Branch 元数据

**Implementation:**
- New migration `000091_ontology_branches_parent_tx.{up,down}.sql` adds two columns to `ontology_branches`: `parent_branch_id TEXT REFERENCES ontology_branches(id) ON DELETE SET NULL` and `base_tx TEXT` (loose-typed; no FK because dataset_transactions only began recording rows in US-379 and pre-US-379 branches have no tx to point at). The status CHECK constraint is widened to accept the PRD's uppercase enum (ACTIVE|MERGED|ABANDONED) alongside the legacy lowercase values (open|merged|closed).
- `pkg/oms/models.go::OntologyBranch`: extended struct with `ParentBranchID string` and `BaseTx string` (json `parentBranchId`, `baseTx`, both `omitempty`). New `NormalizeBranchStatus(s string) string` helper maps the uppercase aliases to canonical lowercase form on store; unknown values pass through unchanged so the upstream CHECK constraint surfaces violations cleanly.
- `pkg/oms/pg_repository.go`: `CreateBranch` / `GetBranch` / `ListBranches` round-trip the new columns (using `*string` scan dest + `nilIfEmpty` for INSERT to keep NULLs clean). `CreateBranch` and `UpdateBranchStatus` route their `Status` argument through `NormalizeBranchStatus` so callers can supply either flavour.
- `pkg/oms/branched_repository.go::NewBranchedRepositoryChain(ctx, base, branchID)`: new chain-builder that walks `ParentBranchID` pointers from leaf to root and stacks `BranchedRepository` decorators outermost-leaf-first. Tolerates a missing leaf (falls through to base) and a `parent_branch_id` that no longer resolves (terminates at the last resolvable ancestor) so a `DELETE`d ancestor cannot turn a working branch read into a 500. Cycle detection: if a parent pointer revisits a branch already in the chain (forbidden by the API but technically schema-permitted), the function silently breaks the loop at the first repeat. Non-`ErrNotFound` errors from `GetBranch` propagate unchanged.
- `pkg/oms/handlers_branch.go::CreateBranch`: `CreateBranchRequest` extended with `ParentBranchID` and `BaseTx` fields. When `parentBranchId` is supplied the handler validates that the parent (a) exists, (b) belongs to the same ontology, (c) is itself open — chaining off a merged/closed branch would silently freeze stale overlays. Failures surface as `ParentBranchNotFound` / `ParentBranchOntologyMismatch` / `ParentBranchNotOpen` 400s.
- `pkg/oms/mock_branches_test.go::CreateBranch` mirror-applies `NormalizeBranchStatus` so the in-memory mock matches PG semantics for both wire flavours.
- `api/openapi.yaml` + `cmd/server/openapi_spec.yaml`: `OntologyBranch` schema gains optional `parentBranchId` and `baseTx` properties (with US-383 prose); the `createBranch` request body schema gains both new optional inputs. The two files are kept byte-identical for the contract test.

**Files changed:**
- `migrations/000091_ontology_branches_parent_tx.up.sql` (new)
- `migrations/000091_ontology_branches_parent_tx.down.sql` (new)
- `pkg/oms/models.go` (OntologyBranch fields + NormalizeBranchStatus helper)
- `pkg/oms/pg_repository.go` (CRUD round-trip + status normalisation)
- `pkg/oms/branched_repository.go` (NewBranchedRepositoryChain + parent-fallback resolver)
- `pkg/oms/handlers_branch.go` (CreateBranchRequest extended; parent validation)
- `pkg/oms/mock_branches_test.go` (mock CreateBranch normalises status)
- `pkg/oms/us383_branch_metadata_test.go` (new — 9 unit tests covering chain builder + normalizer)
- `pkg/oms/us383_handlers_test.go` (new — 4 handler tests for parent validation paths)
- `api/openapi.yaml`, `cmd/server/openapi_spec.yaml`

- `go build ./...` clean
- `go vet ./...` clean

  - `TestNormalizeBranchStatus` — round-trips all 6 alias forms + unknown pass-through

  - `TestNewBranchedRepositoryChain_LeafOverridesParent` — leaf overlay wins on entities both branches touch
  - `TestNewBranchedRepositoryChain_ParentMissingTerminatesGracefully` — parent_branch_id pointing at deleted branch terminates chain cleanly
  - `TestNewBranchedRepositoryChain_CycleSafe` — A↔B parent cycle does not loop forever
  - `TestNewBranchedRepositoryChain_PropagatesGetError` — non-NotFound GetBranch errors surface unchanged
  - `TestCreateBranch_WithParentBranch` — successful parent + baseTx round-trip
  - `TestCreateBranch_RejectsParentNotFound` — missing parent → 400
  - `TestCreateBranch_RejectsParentFromOtherOntology` — cross-ontology parent → 400
  - `TestCreateBranch_RejectsClosedParent` — closed parent → 400
- Pre-existing `seedOntology redeclared` failure under `-tags integration` (noted in US-365 / US-366 progress entries) is unchanged and unrelated to this story.

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- The PRD asked for status enum `ACTIVE|MERGED|ABANDONED` but the existing schema (and ~25 tests) already uses `open|merged|closed`. Solution that preserved the test surface: widen the CHECK constraint to accept BOTH namespaces, add a `NormalizeBranchStatus` helper that maps uppercase → lowercase canonical form on every write path, leave the OpenAPI enum at the canonical form. Future stories that surface uppercase status to UI / SDK should call the helper at the wire boundary; the storage layer is already normalised.
- `NewBranchedRepositoryChain` composes `BranchedRepository` decorators outside-in: the leaf branch wraps the parent's wrap of the base. This means the inside-out evaluation order is "main → parent overlay → leaf overlay" — exactly the fallback semantics the PRD demands without any per-method changes to the existing single-branch decorator. When extending the overlay set with new resolvers (e.g. branch-level Function bodies for US-389), implement them on `BranchedRepository` once and the chain pattern picks them up automatically.
- The PRD names columns `parent_branch` and `base_tx`. The actual schema columns are `parent_branch_id` and `base_tx` — `_id` suffix matches the rest of the codebase (`branch_id`, `ontology_rid`) and avoids ambiguity with the wire-format JSON field `parentBranchId`. `base_tx` matches because it stores the raw `tx-<uuid>` string from `dataset_transactions.tx_id`.
- `parent_branch_id` is `ON DELETE SET NULL`, NOT `CASCADE`. A branch whose parent gets hard-deleted should keep working as a top-level branch (its overlay still applies on top of main); cascading the delete would silently lose the leaf's pending edits. The chain walker tolerates the resulting NULL cleanly.
- `base_tx` does NOT have a FK to `dataset_transactions.tx_id`. The reason: `dataset_transactions` only began recording rows in US-379 (migration 000090); a system that ran on an older release has no tx rows for branches created prior to that. A loose TEXT column lets pre-US-379 branches stay valid and lets future stories tighten the constraint when they own the migration plan.
- Validation on `CreateBranch` rejects parents in non-open status. This is intentional even though the BranchedRepository chain can technically tolerate a closed parent: chaining off a closed branch silently freezes whatever overlays were on the parent at close time, and a future merge of the leaf would propagate stale changes. Enforcing "parent must be open" at create time keeps the data flow sane; if a use-case ever needs read-only inheritance from a closed branch, lift the check.
- The `errBranchRepo` test type embeds `chainRepo` which embeds `inMemoryRepo` which embeds the `Repository` interface — that gives an "infinite stub" for free, so a test only has to override the methods it cares about. The same pattern is used by `noopRepo` / `mockRepo` and is the canonical way to write a focused Repository test fixture without re-implementing 100+ unrelated methods.
- The `OntologyBranch` model has both `ParentBranchID string` (not `*string`) and matching `omitempty` JSON tag. Empty string == "no parent" — same convention used by `Property.SharedPropertyRID` and `ObjectType.ExtendsRID`. Pointer-to-string would force defensive deref / nil checks throughout the chain walker; the empty-string sentinel is simpler and matches the existing pattern.
---

## 2026-05-03 - US-384 - Branch 上的 ActionType / Function 修改

**Implementation:**
- New migration `000092_action_types_function_branch.{up,down}.sql` adds `branch_id TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'main'` to BOTH `action_types` and `functions`. The legacy `(ontology_rid, api_name)` UNIQUE on `action_types` is dropped and replaced with `(ontology_rid, api_name, branch_id)`; same widening for `functions` from `(ontology_rid, name, version)` → `(ontology_rid, name, version, branch_id)`. Two index helpers (`action_types_branch_idx`, `functions_branch_idx`) keep the branch-aware lookup cheap.
- `pkg/oms/branch_scope.go` (new): `DefaultBranch = "main"`, plus `WithBranchScope(ctx, branch)` / `BranchScopeFromContext(ctx)` (defaults to `DefaultBranch` when empty), plus `NormalizeBranchID(s)` helper that substitutes empty → DefaultBranch for write-side stamping. The pattern mirrors `pkg/oss/objectset.WithBranchScope` from US-381 — separate `branchScopeKey{}` so the two namespaces don't cross-contaminate.
- `pkg/oms/models.go`: extended `ActionType` and `Function` with `BranchID string` (json `branchId`, `omitempty`). Empty input normalises to `"main"` at write time so legacy callers that don't populate the field keep landing on the canonical trunk. Pre-existing wire helpers (`ToWireJSON` / `ToFullMetadataJSON`) intentionally do NOT surface BranchID — the field is plumbing for branch routing, not user-facing metadata; `json.Marshal(at)` still includes it for SDK round-trips because of the struct tag.
- `pkg/oms/pg_repository.go`:
  - `CreateActionType` / `CreateFunction` stamp `branch_id` from the model field via `NormalizeBranchID` and write it back so the caller sees the resolved value.
  - `GetActionType` (by RID) round-trips `branch_id` so callers reading by RID still know which branch the row is on.
  - `GetActionTypeByAPIName` reads `BranchScopeFromContext(ctx)` and resolves with `WHERE branch_id IN (current, 'main') ORDER BY (CASE WHEN branch_id = current THEN 0 ELSE 1 END) LIMIT 1`. Branch-scoped lookup picks the branch row when present; otherwise falls back to main. Default branch lookup naturally matches only the main row because the CASE collapses.
  - `ListActionTypes` uses `DISTINCT ON (api_name)` with the same CASE-based ORDER BY to keep one row per ApiName, preferring the branch overlay when present.
  - `GetFunction` round-trips branch_id; `GetFunctionByName` and `GetFunctionByNameVersion` and `ListFunctionVersionsByName` and `ListFunctions` all read the branch from context, filter `branch_id IN (current, 'main')`, then post-filter via `preferBranchFunctions` / `preferBranchFunctionsByName` Go helpers that suppress the main row whenever the branch publishes its own copy at the same `(name, version)`. This lets a branch override `compute@2.0.0` while still inheriting the trunk's `compute@1.0.0` version line.
  - `scanFunctions` widened to `Scan(..., &fn.BranchID)` — single chokepoint so all the function read paths inherit the column without per-call-site plumbing.
- `pkg/oms/cached_repository.go`: `GetActionTypeByAPIName` and `ListActionTypes` cache keys now include `BranchScopeFromContext(ctx)` so a branch-aware lookup never returns the main row from a sibling request that warmed the cache without a branch.
- `pkg/oms/handlers_function.go::ExecuteFunction`: stamps `?branch=` onto `r.Context()` via `WithBranchScope` BEFORE `resolveFunctionRef`. Propagated via `r.WithContext(...)` so every downstream `r.Context()` call (recordFunctionExecution, execCtx) inherits the branch scope.
- `pkg/actions/handlers.go`: new `withBranchScope(r) *http.Request` helper that wraps `r` with the request's `?branch=` stamped via `oms.WithBranchScope`. Wired into `Apply`, `ApplyBatch`, `ApplyWithOverrides` so the executor's `resolveActionType` (which falls through to `ListActionTypes`) sees the branch overlay.
- `api/openapi.yaml` + `cmd/server/openapi_spec.yaml`: `ActionType` and `Function` schemas gain optional `branchId` property with US-384 prose. Files kept byte-identical for the contract test.

**Files changed:**
- `migrations/000092_action_types_function_branch.up.sql` (new)
- `migrations/000092_action_types_function_branch.down.sql` (new)
- `pkg/oms/branch_scope.go` (new)
- `pkg/oms/models.go` (ActionType.BranchID + Function.BranchID)
- `pkg/oms/pg_repository.go` (CRUD round-trip + branch-aware reads + preferBranchFunctions* helpers)
- `pkg/oms/cached_repository.go` (cache keys include branch scope)
- `pkg/oms/handlers_function.go` (ExecuteFunction stamps ?branch onto ctx)
- `pkg/actions/handlers.go` (withBranchScope shim wraps Apply/ApplyBatch/ApplyWithOverrides)
- `pkg/oms/us384_branch_dispatch_test.go` (new — 11 unit tests covering helpers + JSON serialisation)
- `pkg/oms/us384_pg_branch_test.go` (new — 6 integration tests for branch isolation + main fallback)
- `api/openapi.yaml`, `cmd/server/openapi_spec.yaml`
- `prd.json` (mark US-384 passes: true)

**Verification:**
- `go build ./...` clean
- `go vet ./...` clean
- `go test ./...` PASS across 70+ packages, including `pkg/oms` (full suite), `pkg/actions`, `pkg/oss/...`, `cmd/server` (contract tests including embedded-spec parity), `test/e2e`, `test/foundry_parity`, `test/northwind`, `test/chinook`
- 11 new unit tests pass: `TestNormalizeBranchID`, `TestBranchScopeFromContext_DefaultsToMain`, `TestWithBranchScope_RoundTrips`, `TestWithBranchScope_EmptyAndMainAreNoOps`, `TestPreferBranchFunctions_SuppressesMainDuplicate`, `TestPreferBranchFunctions_NoOpForMainScope`, `TestPreferBranchFunctions_NoBranchOverride_KeepsMain`, `TestPreferBranchFunctionsByName_KeyedOnNameAndVersion`, `TestActionType_BranchIDJSONOmitemptyOnDefault`, `TestActionType_BranchIDJSONIncludedWhenSet`, `TestFunction_BranchIDJSONIncludedWhenSet`
- 6 integration tests authored (need live PG to execute, compile-checked via `go test -tags integration -run NEVERMATCH ./pkg/oms/`): branch+main coexist for ActionType, branch fallback to main for ActionType, ListActionTypes branch overlay, branch+main coexist for Function, branch fallback to main for Function, ListFunctionVersionsByName prefers branch on semver collision
- Pre-existing `seedOntology redeclared` failure under `-tags integration` (noted in US-365 / US-366 / US-383 progress) is unchanged — `seedOntologyForUS384` deliberately uses a different name so the new file does not add a fresh conflict.

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- For "same key on different branches" semantics, the pattern is: (1) widen UNIQUE constraint to include `branch_id`, (2) default the column to `'main'` so legacy rows are still valid, (3) thread branch via context + `BranchScopeFromContext`, (4) lookups use `WHERE branch_id IN (current, 'main') ORDER BY (CASE WHEN branch_id=current THEN 0 ELSE 1 END) LIMIT 1` so the branch row wins when present, otherwise main is the fallback. This gives "branch overlay with main fallback" without per-method-signature plumbing — caller sets ctx once, every read in scope inherits.
- The function read path is more nuanced than ActionType because of semver: a branch can publish v2 while main has both v1 and v2. The Go-side `preferBranchFunctions(in, branch)` helper post-filters the SELECT result, suppressing main rows ONLY for `(name, version)` keys that the branch overlay covers. This way branches inherit unmodified versions of the trunk's history while shadowing the ones they republish. A branch publishing its own v2 does NOT hide main's v1 — that would be wrong, since the branch hasn't said anything about v1.
- Cache key composition: when adding branch awareness to a method that's wrapped by `CachedRepository`, the cache key MUST include `BranchScopeFromContext(ctx)`. The straightforward pattern is `BranchScopeFromContext(ctx) + "|" + existingKey`. Forgetting this means a warm-cache miss on the branch lookup returns the main row from an earlier unbranded request — silently wrong, no error, hard to debug. Done for `actionTypeAPI` and `actionTypeList` here; future methods that join the branch-aware club need the same treatment.
- The HTTP-handler shim `withBranchScope(r)` in `pkg/actions/handlers.go` returns a NEW `*http.Request` with the stamped context (rather than mutating `r.Context()` indirectly). This is the canonical Go way to propagate context through handler chains: every downstream `r.Context()` call sees the same stamped value without a separate `ctx` variable to forget about. The `oms.WithBranchScope` no-op for empty/main keeps the wrapper free of unnecessary allocations on the legacy path.
- The legacy `?branch=` overlay (admin handlers writing to `branch_changes`) and the new US-384 direct-row mechanism (writing to `action_types` / `functions` with `branch_id` stamped) coexist intentionally. The overlay is the "branch as patch" model used by interactive PR-style flows (BranchedRepository decorator); US-384 is the "branch as named version" model used by the action/function dispatch path. A future US can converge them if needed; for now they target different surfaces and both are valid.
- The `ON DELETE` policy on `action_types.branch_id` is intentionally absent — there's no FK to `ontology_branches(id)` because the branch column is also used as a free-form namespace (any string the system's branch-scope context wants to use). If a future US needs FK enforcement, add it as a separate constraint that drops gracefully when the branch is removed (`ON DELETE CASCADE` would silently drop a branch's published actions, which is probably the wrong policy for an audit-trail system; `ON DELETE SET DEFAULT 'main'` would silently merge the rows into trunk, also wrong). Leave the column free-form unless a clear policy is decided.
- `DISTINCT ON (api_name)` requires the leading column of the `ORDER BY` to match the DISTINCT ON column. Then secondary ordering picks the winner for each distinct group. This is the cleanest SQL way to express "give me the branch row if present, else main, one row per apiName" without a window function or a self-join.
- Branch direct-merge with explicit conflict resolution (US-385): `pkg/oms/handlers_branch_merge.go` adds two new endpoints — `POST /branches/{branchId}/diff` returning the categorised AnnotatedDiffEntry buckets (added/modified/deleted) plus an inline AnnotatedMergeConflict list, and `POST /branches/{branchId}/merge` that consumes `conflictResolution: { "<entityType>:<apiName>": "use-branch"|"use-main" }` and applies the branch changes accordingly. The conflict detector reuses `detectConflicts` from `handlers_proposal.go` so the rebase / proposal / direct-merge paths share one source of truth. The conflictResolution key shape is "<entityType>:<apiName>" (entityType is already lowercase by storage convention — objectType / actionType / linkType / property — so no case-folding is applied to keep the key strictly equal to what the diff endpoint emits). apiName is sourced from `apiNameFromState`, which probes a `{ apiName string }` shape against the JSON payload (works for ObjectType / Property / LinkType / ActionType because they all expose `apiName` at the top level of their wire shape). For DELETED changes apiName is read from `BeforeState`; for ADDED/MODIFIED from `AfterState` with `BeforeState` as a fallback. The merge handler scans every conflict against the resolution map BEFORE writing any rows: if any conflict lacks a resolution it returns 409 MERGE_CONFLICT with both `conflicts` (full annotated list) and `unresolved` (the subset missing a key); the branch stays open and main stays untouched. When every conflict is resolved, "use-branch" entries flow through `applyAdd/applyModify/applyDelete` exactly like the proposal merge path, and "use-main" entries are skipped — `appliedCount` / `skippedCount` in the response let the caller verify the split. `IncrementOntologyVersion` only fires when at least one change was actually applied (a "skip everything" merge still flips the branch to merged but doesn't bump main's version, since nothing changed). Pattern: when adding a new conflict-aware mutation surface, derive the conflict identity from the entity payload (apiName) rather than the random EntityRID — apiName is human-readable and survives serialisation round-trips, while EntityRID can be rebuilt by replays / packaging tools and would force the UI to show opaque RIDs in the resolution UX.
---

## 2026-05-03 - US-385 - Branch 合并冲突检测

**Implementation:**
- `pkg/oms/handlers_branch_merge.go` (new): two handlers + helpers.
  - `PostBranchDiff` (`POST /api/v2/ontologies/{ontologyApiName}/branches/{branchId}/diff`) returns a `BranchDiffPostResponse` with separate `added` / `modified` / `deleted` arrays of `AnnotatedDiffEntry` plus an `AnnotatedMergeConflict` list and a `hasConflicts` flag.
  - `MergeBranch` (`POST /api/v2/ontologies/{ontologyApiName}/branches/{branchId}/merge`) consumes `MergeBranchRequest{ ConflictResolution map[string]string }` where the key is `"<entityType>:<apiName>"` and the value is `"use-branch"` or `"use-main"`. Applies branch changes, returns `MergeBranchResponse{ Branch, AppliedCount, SkippedCount }`.
- `apiNameFromChange` / `apiNameFromState` extract entity apiName from the `BeforeState` / `AfterState` JSON. All four branch-tracked entity types (ObjectType / Property / LinkType / ActionType) expose `apiName` at the top of their wire shape, so a single 4-line JSON probe works for every kind.
- `resolutionKey(entityType, apiName)` composes `"<entityType>:<apiName>"` — the conflict identity used both in the wire shape (as `resolutionKey` field on each annotated entry) and as the lookup key the merge handler expects in `conflictResolution`.
- The merge flow runs `detectConflicts` (existing helper from `handlers_proposal.go`) once, then scans the conflict set against `req.ConflictResolution`. If any conflict lacks a resolution → 409 `MERGE_CONFLICT` with `{conflicts, unresolved}` payload, branch stays open, main unchanged. If every conflict is resolved → `applyAdd` / `applyModify` / `applyDelete` for "use-branch" entries (and non-conflicting changes), skip for "use-main", increment ontology version once if anything was actually applied, then `UpdateBranchStatus(branchId, "merged")`.
- Invalid resolution values (anything other than `use-branch` or `use-main`) return 400 `InvalidConflictResolution` BEFORE the conflict detection runs — surfaces malformed bodies cleanly without rolling through PG.
- `cmd/server/routes.go` (US-113/US-116 branch group): added two new lines wiring `omsHandler.PostBranchDiff` and `omsHandler.MergeBranch`. The legacy `GET /diff` and proposal-based `POST /proposals/{id}/merge` paths stay untouched — they remain the source of truth for read-only diff inspection and for review-gated merges respectively.
- `api/openapi.yaml` + `cmd/server/openapi_spec.yaml`: added schemas `AnnotatedDiffEntry`, `AnnotatedMergeConflict`, `BranchDiffPostResponse`, `MergeBranchRequest`, `MergeBranchResponse`; documented the two new POST routes (200/400/404/409 surfaces). Files kept byte-identical for the contract test.

**Files changed:**
- `pkg/oms/handlers_branch_merge.go` (new — 2 handlers + 5 wire types + apiNameFrom* / resolutionKey helpers)
- `pkg/oms/handlers_branch_merge_us385_test.go` (new — 11 unit tests covering both endpoints)
- `cmd/server/routes.go` (POST diff + POST merge route registrations)
- `api/openapi.yaml`
- `cmd/server/openapi_spec.yaml`
- `prd.json` (mark US-385 passes: true)

**Verification:**
- `go build ./...` clean
- `go vet ./...` clean
- `go test ./...` PASS across the full suite (~70 packages including pkg/oms, cmd/server contract tests, test/e2e, test/foundry_parity, test/northwind, test/chinook)
- 11 new unit tests pass:
  - `TestPostBranchDiff_CategorisesEntries_NoConflicts` — 1 ADDED + 1 MODIFIED + 1 DELETED bucket out
  - `TestPostBranchDiff_FlagsConflicts_WhenMainModifiedSameApiName` — surfaces apiName + resolutionKey on the conflict
  - `TestPostBranchDiff_BranchNotFound` — 404
  - `TestMergeBranch_NoConflicts_AppliesAllChanges` — both rows applied, branch transitioned to merged
  - `TestMergeBranch_Conflict_NoResolution_Returns409` — 409 + branch stays open + main untouched
  - `TestMergeBranch_ConflictResolution_UseBranch_AppliesBranchState` — branch state wins on resolved conflict
  - `TestMergeBranch_ConflictResolution_UseMain_SkipsBranchChange` — main retained, branch flipped to merged
  - `TestMergeBranch_PartialConflictResolution_Returns409` — partial resolution still 409s; nothing partially applied
  - `TestMergeBranch_BranchNotOpen_Returns409` — merged/closed branches reject
  - `TestMergeBranch_BranchNotFound_Returns404` — 404
  - `TestMergeBranch_InvalidResolutionValue_Returns400` — 400 on `keep-mine` etc.

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- The PRD literally specifies `conflictResolution: { apiName: 'use-branch'|'use-main' }`, but a bare `apiName` is ambiguous across entityTypes (an `objectType` named "Order" vs an `actionType` named "Order"). The implementation widens the key shape to `<entityType>:<apiName>` and surfaces it as `resolutionKey` on every diff entry / conflict so the SPA and SDK don't have to reconstruct it themselves. Wire-format JSON keys are case-sensitive, so the key never gets case-folded — entityType is already lowercase by storage convention.
- `detectConflicts` is the shared chokepoint for rebase / proposal merge / direct merge — ALL three should keep using it. When extending what counts as a conflict (e.g. semantic equality on JSON fields the user can't possibly have changed), update `detectConflicts` once instead of duplicating the predicate per handler.
- The merge handler builds the *full* annotated conflict list once, then partitions it into `unresolved` only at response time. Returning both lists in the 409 payload lets the SPA render "here's everything that needs your attention, here's what you still owe a decision on" without a follow-up round-trip.
- `IncrementOntologyVersion` only fires when at least one change was actually applied. A "use-main everywhere" merge still flips the branch to `merged` (the branch is done from the user's perspective) but does NOT bump main's version, because main's content is unchanged. Avoiding the version bump keeps `OntologyVersion` aligned with the actual state of main, which downstream caches / snapshot labels use as a freshness indicator.
- `apiNameFromState` is an obvious target for "what if ObjectType wraps apiName one layer deeper?" anxiety, but the existing wire helpers (`Property`, `LinkType`, `ActionType`, `ObjectType`) all unconditionally have `apiName` at the top level of `json.Marshal(entity)`, and the branch_changes table stores exactly that shape. If a future refactor nests apiName, the probe needs to grow accordingly — but until then the 4-liner is sufficient and avoids importing any of the 4 entity types into the helper module.
- The diff handler returns `branch` as part of the response so the SPA can render the branch metadata header (name, status, baseVersion) without a separate `GET /branches/{id}` round-trip. Cheap on the server, useful on the client.
- New OpenAPI routes need entries in BOTH `api/openapi.yaml` AND `cmd/server/openapi_spec.yaml`. The contract test (`TestContract_AllRoutesDocumented` + `TestContract_EmbeddedSpecMatchesCanonical`) walks the chi router and the embedded spec; missing either file flags a regression. The convention is `cp api/openapi.yaml cmd/server/openapi_spec.yaml` after the canonical edit.

## 2026-05-03 - US-386 - Branch UI 切换器与浏览

**Implementation:**
- `web/src/stores/branchStore.ts` (new): Zustand store with `localStorage` persistence under key `weave-active-branch`. Holds `selections: Record<ontologyApiName, branchId>` so a user can pin different branches per ontology. `setBranch` normalises blank / `'main'` input to the default sentinel and DELETES the entry rather than persisting `'main'`, so absence == default. `activeBranchFor(ontologyApiName)` is the synchronous read used by non-React modules (the API client).
- `web/src/api/client.ts`: introduced `extractOntologyApiName(path)` (regex `/^\/api\/(?:v2|admin)\/ontologies\/([^/?#]+)/`) and `withActiveBranch(path)`. The latter is invoked once before `authedFetch` in `request()` and rewrites the URL to inject `?branch=<active>` whenever (a) the path targets an ontology surface, (b) the store has a non-default branch for that ontology, and (c) the caller hasn't already supplied an explicit `?branch=`. Non-ontology surfaces (`/api/auth/login`, `/api/v2/notifications`, `/health`) are left untouched. Existing query params and url fragments are preserved.
- `web/src/api/types.ts`: added `OntologyBranch` interface mirroring `pkg/oms/models.go::OntologyBranch` (id, ontologyRid, name, baseVersion, parentBranchId?, baseTx?, status, createdBy, createdAt, updatedAt).
- `web/src/api/ontologies.ts`: added `listBranches(ontologyApiName)` calling `GET /api/v2/ontologies/{name}/branches` (matches the existing `pkg/oms.OMSHandler.ListBranches` route which already filters to status=open).
- `web/src/components/layout/BranchPicker.tsx` (new): dropdown trigger displaying the active branch name (`main` by default) + a menu of `main` plus the open branches returned by the API. Selecting a branch calls `setBranch(ontologyApiName, branchId)` and `queryClient.invalidateQueries()` so every cached view refetches under the new branch context. The picker is hidden when no ontology is active (Dashboard / Admin). Dropdown closes on outside click; `aria-haspopup="menu"` + `aria-expanded` for accessibility, plus `data-testid` hooks for tests.
- `web/src/components/layout/Topbar.tsx`: read the active ontology via `useParams().ontology ?? useOntologyStore().selectedOntology` (same fallback chain `Shell.tsx` already uses) and mount `<BranchPicker ontologyApiName={activeOntology} />` to the left of the theme/language/notifications cluster.
- `web/src/i18n/resources/{en,zh-CN}.ts`: new `branch.{label, default, loading}` keys.
- `web/src/api/__tests__/client_branch.test.ts` (new): 11 unit tests covering `extractOntologyApiName` (4 cases), `withActiveBranch` (8 cases incl. ontology vs non-ontology paths, default vs non-default branch, query-param preservation, explicit `?branch=` override, per-ontology isolation, admin path inheritance, clearBranch revert), and 2 e2e msw tests through `request()`.
- `web/src/stores/__tests__/branchStore.test.ts` (new): 7 unit tests covering null/undefined handling, set/clear semantics, blank-string normalisation, per-ontology isolation.
- `web/src/components/layout/__tests__/BranchPicker.test.tsx` (new): 5 React-Testing-Library tests covering null-ontology hide, default trigger label, branch listing, selection persistence, and the "select main clears the entry" behaviour.

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- Per-ontology branch state (rather than a single global "active branch") matches the backend's per-ontology `ontology_branches` table. A user often wants to keep ontology A on main while exploring branch X on ontology B. Same convention as the existing `useOntologyStore.recentlyViewed` (per-RID, not global).
- The right injection point for branch scope is the `request()` boundary in `web/src/api/client.ts`, NOT individual API helpers. A path-regex match (`/^\/api\/(?:v2|admin)\/ontologies\/([^/?#]+)/`) reliably captures every ontology-rooted surface (~120 endpoints) without per-helper plumbing. The same pattern can be reused for other request-time scopes (tenant, environment).
- The "explicit `?branch=` already on the path" override is intentional — it lets diagnostic tools (cross-branch comparison, admin overrides) bypass the store without mutating global state. Without this clause a developer trying `?branch=other` directly would silently get clobbered to whatever the picker had selected.
- DEFAULT_BRANCH writes deliberately DELETE the store entry rather than persist `'main'`. This keeps `localStorage` minimal (no per-ontology garbage on initial visits) and means absence canonically maps to "default branch". The branchStore tests assert this contract explicitly so future refactors can't silently start persisting `'main'` and break the cache-invalidation logic that depends on `selections[name] === undefined → no ?branch= injected`.
- `queryClient.invalidateQueries()` on branch selection is mandatory — otherwise stale `react-query` cache hits would keep rendering the previous branch's data after the dropdown changes, and the user has to reload the page to see any difference. The cost is a refetch of in-flight queries; cheap on a dev workstation, acceptable in prod since branch switching is a rare interactive action.
- Frontend tests that touch `useBranchStore` MUST reset state in `beforeEach` (`useBranchStore.setState({ selections: {} })`) — Zustand keeps a singleton across tests in the same vitest worker, so a leak from one test would silently change the URL rewrite verdict in the next.
- `vite build` is a more permissive transpiler than `tsc -b` (esbuild vs official tsc). When `tsc -b` flags errors but `vite build` succeeds and tests pass, verify the errors are pre-existing on main via `git stash && tsc -b && stash pop` before treating them as a regression. Three pre-existing errors live in this repo today (ObjectSetComposer test fixture + 2× Buffer type missing in objectSetUrl.ts) — they are not blockers for new work.
- The Topbar's `useParams().ontology ?? useOntologyStore().selectedOntology` fallback chain mirrors what `Shell.tsx` already does — keep them in sync. If a new route uses a different URL parameter for ontology, both Shell and BranchPicker need updating.
---

## 2026-05-03 - US-387 - Branch Reconcile UI

**Implementation:**
- `web/src/components/explorer/BranchReconcilePage.tsx` (new): the PR-style two-pane reconcile surface. Header carries breadcrumbs (`ontology / branches / <id> / reconcile`), branch status pill, change tally (added/modified/deleted/conflicts), a link to the legacy raw-diff page, and the merge submit button. Body is split into a `Conflicts` section (each row: change badge + entityType badge + apiName + side-by-side `mainState` vs `branchState` JSON view + inline radio group `use-main` / `use-branch`) and an `Other changes` section (collapsible cards using the existing `before` / `after` schema). Submit calls `mergeBranch` and either navigates back to `/explorer/<ontology>` on 200 or replaces the conflict list with `serverConflicts` from the 409 body so the user sees freshly-detected conflicts without a re-fetch round trip.
- `web/src/api/types.ts`: new wire types mirroring `pkg/oms/handlers_branch_merge.go` — `AnnotatedDiffEntry`, `AnnotatedMergeConflict`, `BranchDiffPostResponse`, `MergeBranchRequest`, `MergeBranchResponse`, `MergeConflictBody`, plus the `ConflictResolutionChoice = 'use-branch' | 'use-main'` literal union.
- `web/src/api/ontologies.ts`: new `postBranchDiff(ontology, branchId)` and `mergeBranch(ontology, branchId, body)` helpers. The merge helper bypasses the standard `request()` path because the 409 body is non-standard (`{errorCode: "MERGE_CONFLICT", conflicts, unresolved}` rather than the normal `ApiError` envelope) — it builds the URL via `withActiveBranch`, calls `authedFetch` directly, and on a 409 throws the new `MergeBranchConflictError` carrying the typed conflict + unresolved arrays. Other failures still surface as `ApiRequestError`.
- `web/src/hooks/useBranches.ts`: added `useBranchReconcileDiff` (POST cache key `['branchDiffPost', ontology, branchId]`) and `useMergeBranch` (mutation that invalidates `branchDiffPost`, `branchDiff`, and `branches` query keys on success).
- `web/src/App.tsx`: registered the new route `explorer/:ontology/branches/:branch/reconcile` next to the existing `…/diff` route.
- `web/src/components/explorer/BranchDiffPage.tsx`: split the heading row into a flex container so a new `Open Reconcile UI` link sits to the right of the existing title. This is the discoverability bridge from the read-only diff to the new reconcile surface.
- `web/src/i18n/resources/{en,zh-CN}.ts`: new `branchReconcile.*` namespace with 26 keys covering crumbs, badges, button labels, success/error toasts, and empty states. Both trees stay shape-equivalent (verified via `node scripts/extract-i18n.mjs`).

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- The merge endpoint's 409 body is the project's first non-`ApiError`-shaped error response. The standard `request()` helper would discard the `conflicts` / `unresolved` arrays because it parses errors into `ApiRequestError` (which only carries `errorCode` / `errorName` / `parameters`). Solution: bypass `request()` for the merge call and read the JSON body directly, branching on `status === 409 && parsed.errorCode === 'MERGE_CONFLICT'` to throw a typed `MergeBranchConflictError` carrying the structured payload. Other failures still flow through `ApiRequestError` so call sites that don't care about the conflict shape behave identically. Pattern: when an endpoint returns a structured error envelope that doesn't match the standard `ApiError` shape, define a typed `Error` subclass per endpoint and short-circuit the response parsing for it; don't try to widen `ApiRequestError` because that would force every consumer to handle the new fields.
- React-i18next `t()` returns the raw key (e.g. `"branchReconcile.merging"`) when the i18n instance is not initialised in tests. The existing test setup (`web/src/test/setup.ts`) does NOT bootstrap i18n, so RTL tests must NOT assert on translated strings — assert on `data-testid` markers, props (`disabled`), or the dynamic data values that flow through the component (e.g. branch ids, `apiName` strings). The BranchPicker test follows the same convention and is the canonical reference. If a story genuinely needs translated copy under test, set up an `i18next.init({lng:'en', resources})` in a per-test wrapper rather than mutating the shared setup file.
- For test data attributes that include colons (e.g. `data-testid="reconcile-conflict-${resolutionKey}"` where the key is `actionType:updateOrder`), `screen.getByTestId('reconcile-conflict-actionType:updateOrder')` works directly — RTL's testid lookup uses string equality, not CSS selector parsing, so colons are not interpreted. This is the right shape because `resolutionKey` is the conflict's stable identity (`<entityType>:<apiName>`) and matching it 1:1 in the testid lets E2E tests select an exact conflict row without index gymnastics.
- The reconcile page deliberately re-renders the conflict list using `serverConflicts` (set from the 409 body) rather than re-fetching the diff. This is faster (no extra round trip), keeps the user's existing radio selections intact for the still-conflicting subset, and matches the backend's behaviour of computing conflicts on every merge call (so a re-fetch of `postBranchDiff` would return the same list anyway). The `useMergeBranch` mutation also invalidates the `branchDiffPost` query key on the *successful* path so a navigation-back later reflects the merged state.
- The `mergeBranch` helper in `ontologies.ts` re-exports the static `withActiveBranch` and `ApiRequestError` from `./client` rather than dynamic-importing them. Dynamic imports inside an async handler work but make TypeScript inference and test mocking harder. Static imports at the top of `ontologies.ts` are the canonical pattern and match what every other API helper in this file does.
- Backend `MergeBranch` rejects branches with `status != "open"` (409 BranchNotOpen) BEFORE inspecting the body, so a closed-branch user hitting "Merge" surfaces as an `ApiRequestError` with `errorCode: "BranchNotOpen"`. The page also pre-disables the merge button when `branch.status !== 'open'` so the user gets immediate visual feedback instead of relying on the round-trip 409. Keep both guards: client-side disable for UX, server-side reject for correctness.
- The "Open Reconcile UI" link from `BranchDiffPage` is the discoverability bridge — without it users would have no way to find the new surface unless they typed the URL. Adding the link in the header (rather than the body) keeps it visible regardless of how many diff entries the page renders. When introducing a new sibling page that supersedes an existing read-only surface, mirror this pattern: keep the legacy page operational, add a link from its header to the new page, and note both in the route table.

## 2026-05-03 - US-388 - Dataset Transaction Chain (rollback)

**Implementation:**
- New migration `000093_dataset_transaction_rollback.{up,down}.sql` adds the rollback audit overlay to `dataset_transactions`: nullable `rolled_back_at TIMESTAMPTZ` + `rolled_back_to_tx_id TEXT`, plus a partial index `idx_dataset_transactions_rolled_back ON (ontology_api_name, rolled_back_at) WHERE rolled_back_at IS NOT NULL` so a "show me undone txs" lookup stays cheap. Loose TEXT (no FK) on `rolled_back_to_tx_id` because the rollback bookkeeping row legitimately self-points to its own TxID and an FK would create an awkward "constraint deferred to commit" pattern.
- `pkg/oms/dataset_transaction.go`:
  - `DatasetTransaction` gains `RolledBackAt time.Time` + `RolledBackToTxID string` (both `omitempty`). Zero-value time means "not rolled back" — matches the SQL NULL semantics without an extra `*time.Time` indirection.
  - New `AffectedKey{ObjectTypeRID, PrimaryKey}` struct surfaces the per-PK replay set the rollback handler needs.
  - `DatasetTransactionStore` interface widened with `ListAfterCommittedAt(ctx, ontologyAPIName, after)` (oldest-first chain entries strictly newer than `after`) and `MarkRolledBack(ctx, txID, rolledBackToTxID, rolledBackAt)` (idempotent overwrite).
- `pkg/oms/pg_repository_dataset_transaction.go`:
  - All five existing read methods (`GetDatasetTransaction`, `LatestForOntology`, `ListByOntology`) round-trip the new `rolled_back_at` / `rolled_back_to_tx_id` columns via `*time.Time` / `*string` scan dests — same pattern as `parent_tx_id` / `user_id`.
  - New `ListAfterCommittedAt` selects rows with `committed_at > $2` ordered ASC for chronological replay; rolled-back rows are still surfaced (caller's choice whether to skip).
  - New `ListAffectedKeysSince` joins `object_history` to `object_types` so the affected-keys lookup is scoped by `ontology_rid`. `DISTINCT (object_type_rid, primary_key)` so a PK touched by N batches surfaces once.
  - New `MarkRolledBack` rejects an empty `txID` / zero `rolledBackAt` up front, then `UPDATE` with `nilIfEmpty(rolledBackToTxID)` so a self-pointing bookkeeping row is still expressible via a non-empty string. Returns `ErrNotFound` on zero rows-affected.
- `cmd/server/dataset_rollback_handler.go` (new):
  - `datasetTransactionWriter` interface composes `datasetTransactionLister` (the existing read-only contract from `dataset_transaction_handler.go`) with `RecordDatasetTransaction` + `ListAfterCommittedAt` + `MarkRolledBack`. Same shape pattern as `oms.DatasetTransactionStore` minus the funnel consumer's recorder overlap so a degraded-mode test router can plug a fake.
  - `datasetRollbackOntologyRepo` is the OMS subset (`GetOntology` + `GetObjectType`) used for URL → ontology resolution AND per-PK replay's ObjectType lookup.
  - `datasetRollbackAffectedStore` (`ListAffectedKeysSince`) and `datasetRollbackSnapshot` (`SnapshotObjectsAt`) are the two replay-side hooks; nil any of them and the rollback degrades to a metadata-only audit overlay.
  - `indexWriter` is the `*index.Manager` subset (`IndexDocument`/`DeleteDocument`) the replay path mutates.
  - `CreateTransaction` (`POST /api/v2/datasets/{rid}/transactions`): looks up ontology → reads chain head via `LatestForOntology` → mints a `tx-<32-hex>` id via `crypto/rand` (16 bytes = 128 bits) → records with `parent_tx_id = chain head`. Optional body `{userId?, editsCount?}` with `DisallowUnknownFields`. Returns `201` JSON of the freshly stamped row. Empty chain → `parentTxId` blank.
  - `Rollback` (`POST /api/v2/datasets/{rid}/rollback?to=tx-...`): the AC chokepoint. (1) validate `?to=` → starts with `tx-` (`InvalidRollbackTarget` 400 if not), (2) lookup ontology + target tx, reject `RollbackTargetWrongOntology` cross-ontology, `RollbackTargetNotFound` 404 missing, (3) `ListAfterCommittedAt` newer txs, (4) replay each `(objectTypeRID, primaryKey)` against `SnapshotObjectsAt(targetTx.CommittedAt)` — restored if snapshot row exists, deleted from the live Bleve index otherwise, (5) `MarkRolledBack` each newer tx, (6) record a fresh "rollback" tx whose `parent_tx_id = target.TxID` AND `rolled_back_to_tx_id = target.TxID` (self-pointer audit) so the chain head moves coherently.
  - Replay degrades cleanly: nil affected/history/index → `RestoredObjects=0, DeletedObjects=0` but the audit overlay still flips and the bookkeeping row still lands.
- `cmd/server/main.go`:
  - `ServerDeps` gains `DatasetAffectedStore datasetRollbackAffectedStore` for the affected-keys lookup. Wired off the uncached `*PGRepository` in the PG bootstrap (the `ListAffectedKeysSince` method joins `object_history`, not on the cached metadata Repository).
  - Routes `POST /api/v2/datasets/{rid}/transactions` + `POST /api/v2/datasets/{rid}/rollback` mounted unconditionally so OpenAPI / contract tests always discover them. The `datasetTxWriter` is acquired via type assertion on `deps.DatasetTransactionStore` (the same `*PGRepository`) so a degraded-mode router that left it unset still gets `DatasetRollbackUnavailable` 400 cleanly.
- `api/openapi.yaml` + `cmd/server/openapi_spec.yaml`: documented both new POST routes (`createDatasetTransaction` + `rollbackDataset`) including the `?to=` query, `RollbackTargetNotFound` / `RollbackTargetWrongOntology` / `DatasetRollbackUnavailable` error envelopes, and the response wire shape (`rolledBackTxIds`, `restoredObjects`, `deletedObjects`, `newTransaction`, `targetTx`). Files kept byte-identical for the contract test.

**Files changed:**
- `migrations/000093_dataset_transaction_rollback.up.sql` (new)
- `migrations/000093_dataset_transaction_rollback.down.sql` (new)
- `pkg/oms/dataset_transaction.go` (RolledBackAt/RolledBackToTxID + AffectedKey + 2 store-interface methods)

**Verification:**
- `go build ./...` clean
- `go vet ./...` clean
- `go test ./...` PASS across the full ~70-package surface (cached on second run; uncached cmd/server completes in 2.6s)
- `go test ./cmd/server/ -run "TestRollback|TestCreateTransaction|TestNewCheckpointTxID" -v` — 12/12 PASS

  - `TestCreateTransaction_GenesisHasEmptyParent` — empty chain → blank ParentTxID

  - `TestRollback_InvalidTargetPrefix` — non-`tx-` target → `InvalidRollbackTarget` 400
  - `TestRollback_FullReplay_RestoresAndDeletes` — AC end-to-end: t1 write → t2 modify+create → rollback to t1 restores pk=A from snapshot, deletes pk=B (post-target create), marks tx-2 + tx-3 rolled back, records bookkeeping row at chain head pointing at tx-1
  - `TestRollback_DegradedNoReplayInfra` — nil affected/history/index → metadata-only rollback (counts=0, audit overlay flipped, bookkeeping row stamped)
  - `TestRollback_NoNewerTxs_NoOp` — target IS chain head → empty RolledBackTxIDs but bookkeeping row still recorded

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- The acceptance criterion has THREE parts that look like one feature: (a) `dataset_transactions` table with `parent_tx_id` (already built in US-379, migration 000090), (b) `POST /transactions` for explicit checkpoints, (c) `POST /rollback?to=tx-...` for the actual time-travel effect. The first part is reused as-is; the second is an empty-edits checkpoint stamper; the third is the surgical part. Don't conflate them — the rollback is the only one that touches user-visible state, the checkpoint is metadata-only.
- The rollback's "write isolation" semantics (PRD: "写入按 tx 隔离") translate to: every `POST /rollback` records a fresh bookkeeping tx whose `parent_tx_id = target.TxID`, NOT the prior chain head. This intentionally branches the chain visually — a future `?asOf=tx-...` read past the rollback resolves correctly because the rollback's CommittedAt > target's CommittedAt and `committed_at`-ordered scans still find the right snapshot. The audit overlay (`rolled_back_at` / `rolled_back_to_tx_id` on the abandoned txs) lets a future history view render the "deleted" branch with strikethrough rather than dropping it.
- Self-pointer convention: the bookkeeping row sets `rolled_back_to_tx_id = target.TxID` (NOT its own TxID). This makes the audit semantics clean — every row with a non-NULL `rolled_back_to_tx_id` either (a) was rolled back (its own state is stale, points at the rollback target), or (b) IS a rollback bookkeeping row (its state is canonical, points at the rollback target). The schema doesn't disambiguate the two cases — the join with `parent_tx_id` makes (b) detectable (rollback rows have parent = the same target).
- Replay degrades, marks don't. The handler returns 200 even with nil affected/history/index — the chain audit overlay always flips, but `RestoredObjects` / `DeletedObjects` stay 0. This matches the PRD's "rollback is a metadata-first operation" semantics: a system without a replay infrastructure (e.g. a fresh Bleve cluster being rebuilt) should still be able to flip the chain. Conversely, a `MarkRolledBack` failure DOES abort the handler — a half-marked chain is genuinely incoherent and would surface as a stale `?asOf=tx-...` read returning the rolled-back state.
- The replay walks `affectedStore` keys, NOT `ListAfterCommittedAt`'s tx list. The two views differ on purpose: the tx list is the AUDIT axis ("which txs to mark"); the affected-keys list is the OBJECT axis ("which docs to replay"). A single tx can touch hundreds of PKs; conversely a long sequence of compensating edits can re-touch the same PK across N txs but the replay only needs the latest snapshot at target's CommittedAt — one re-index per affected (objectType, pk) is sufficient.
- Bucketing affected keys by `ObjectTypeRID` BEFORE iterating cuts the snapshot fetches from O(affected_keys) to O(distinct_object_types). For a typical rollback that touches N rows of M ObjectTypes (N >> M), this is the difference between minutes of round-trips and milliseconds. Done by `byOT := make(map[string][]string)` in `replayObjects`.
- `crypto/rand` for the tx id (16 bytes = 128 bits) is overkill for a single-machine deployment but matches the rest of the project's RID minting and avoids the time-precision ambiguity of `tx-20260503T...`. The fallback to a time-based id when `crypto/rand` errors is a defensive hedge that keeps the handler making forward progress on platforms where `getrandom(2)` is mis-configured.
- The `datasetTransactionWriter` interface composes `datasetTransactionLister` rather than re-declaring the read methods. This means a future story that adds a new read method to the lister (e.g. `ListByUser` for an audit drill-down) automatically picks it up in the writer surface and no per-method ceremony is needed in the rollback handler. Same composition pattern as `oms.DatasetTransactionStore` extending a hypothetical reader interface.
- `nilIfEmpty(rolledBackToTxID)` in the `MarkRolledBack` UPDATE is the canonical PG nullability shim: empty Go string → SQL NULL, non-empty string → TEXT value. Used throughout the repo for `parent_tx_id` / `user_id` / etc. — keep using it for any new nullable column to avoid the "stored empty string vs NULL" ambiguity that breaks `WHERE col IS NULL` queries.
- The cmd/server `historySnapshotStore` interface (in `history_snapshot_provider.go`) and the new `datasetRollbackSnapshot` interface (in `dataset_rollback_handler.go`) have IDENTICAL method shapes — both expect `SnapshotObjectsAt(ctx, objectTypeRID, asOf) ([]oms.LatestObjectState, error)`. They're declared independently so each consumer can stub its own minimal subset; `*PGRepository` satisfies both. Don't try to merge them into one shared interface — the duplication is intentional and keeps test fakes narrow.
- The `recordingIndex` test fake in `dataset_rollback_handler_test.go` captures Index/Delete calls into nested maps keyed by scoped key + PK so tests can assert exactly what the rollback wrote. This is the canonical pattern for asserting on "what side effects landed on the index manager" — same shape used by other index-touching tests in the repo. The `index.ScopedKey(ontologyAPIName, objectTypeAPIName)` formatter is the single source of truth for the `{ontology}__{objectType}` key shape — never hardcode the separator.
---

## 2026-05-03 - US-389 - Branch on Function (Goja)

**Implementation:**
- `pkg/oms/handlers_function.go::CreateFunctionRequest` gains an optional `BranchID string \`json:"branchId,omitempty"\`` body field. The publish handler reads the branch in this priority order: `?branch=` query (authoritative, mirrors the read-side `WithBranchScope` stamping convention from US-384) → `req.BranchID` (legacy/SDK fallback) → `NormalizeBranchID("") → "main"`. The resolved branch is stamped onto `fn.BranchID` BEFORE `fn.Validate()` so a future schema constraint on the field surfaces cleanly, and BEFORE `detectFunctionCallCycle` so the cycle scan walks the branch's overlay (a branch publish that adds A→B should resolve B against the branch first, falling back to main).
- `cycleCtx := WithBranchScope(r.Context(), branchID)` is the chokepoint for branch-aware cycle detection. The repository overlay (`functionCallGraphRepoOverlay`) honours the ctx because the underlying `repo.GetFunctionByName` already pulls branch from `BranchScopeFromContext` (US-384). No changes were needed to the call-graph machinery itself — wiring the ctx is sufficient.
- 409 conflict envelope on `ErrDuplicate` now carries `branchId` alongside `name` and `version` so SDK callers can render "function 'compute' v1.0.0 already exists on branch 'feature-x'" instead of the ambiguous "already exists in this ontology". Required because the (ontology, name, version, branch_id) UNIQUE constraint widened in US-384 means duplicates are now branch-scoped, and a clear error message is the difference between a 5-second fix and a 5-minute hunt.
- `pkg/oms/mock_functions_test.go::CreateFunction` uniqueness key now includes `BranchID` so the in-memory mock matches the PG UNIQUE constraint shape. `GetFunctionByName`, `GetFunctionByNameVersion`, `ListFunctions`, and `ListFunctionVersionsByName` are now branch-aware — they consult `BranchScopeFromContext(ctx)` and route to branch-specific rows when present, falling back to main otherwise. New `mockPreferBranchFunctions` / `mockPreferBranchFunctionsByName` mirror the `pkg/oms.preferBranchFunctions` / `preferBranchFunctionsByName` helpers so the in-memory test harness reproduces the production overlay/inheritance semantics. This is what lets handler-level unit tests verify branch routing through the full request → handler → repo chain.
- `api/openapi.yaml` / `cmd/server/openapi_spec.yaml`: documented the new `?branch=` query parameter and `branchId` body field on the `createFunction` operation; updated the version description to note the new (ontology, name, version, branchId) UNIQUE shape. Files kept byte-identical for the contract test.

- `pkg/oms/handlers_function.go` (CreateFunctionRequest.BranchID + branch resolution + cycleCtx + branchId on 409 envelope)

- 10 new unit tests pass via `go test ./pkg/oms/ -run US389 -v`:
  - `TestCreateFunction_DefaultsToMainBranch_US389` — empty publish lands BranchID="main"
  - `TestCreateFunction_AcceptsBranchQueryParameter_US389` — `?branch=feature-x` writes BranchID="feature-x"
  - `TestCreateFunction_AcceptsBranchBodyField_US389` — `branchId` in body works for SDKs that can't easily mutate URL
  - `TestCreateFunction_QueryParamWinsOverBody_US389` — query takes precedence over conflicting body field
  - `TestCreateFunction_BranchAndMainCoexistAtSameSemver_US389` — main v1 + feature-x v1 land as 2 distinct rows (PRD AC chokepoint)
  - `TestCreateFunction_DuplicateOnSameBranch_Returns409_US389` — within-branch (name, version) collisions still 409
  - `TestCreateFunction_DuplicateAcrossBranches_DoesNotConflict_US389` — main + feature-x + feature-y all coexist with same name@version
  - `TestGetFunction_BranchScopeRoutesToBranchVersion_US389` — `WithBranchScope("feature-x")` reads the branch row, default scope reads main
  - `TestListFunctions_BranchScopeMergesMainAndBranch_US389` — branch sees main fnA v1 (inherited) + branch fnA v2 (overlay) + branch fnB v1 (new)
  - `TestCreateFunction_DuplicateConflictResponseCarriesBranchId_US389` — 409 envelope `parameters.branchId` set so callers know which branch hit the dup
- `diff api/openapi.yaml cmd/server/openapi_spec.yaml` — empty (contract test gate clean)

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- US-384 already shipped the persistence + read-side branch dispatch for Functions (column, UNIQUE constraint widening, `BranchScopeFromContext`-aware repo methods, ExecuteFunction stamping `?branch=` onto the request context). US-389 closed the publish-side gap: until this story, the `BranchID` field on the wire model was effectively read-only because `CreateFunction` never populated it from the request, so every newly-published row landed on main regardless. The fix was small-scoped — request struct + handler resolution + cycle ctx + 409 envelope — exactly because the storage/read story was already done. Pattern: when a feature splits into "branch-aware reads" and "branch-aware writes" stories, expect the writes story to be the much smaller one if reads landed first.
- The `?branch=` query vs `branchId` body precedence rule is "query wins" because the read path uses query exclusively (`r.URL.Query().Get("branch")` → `WithBranchScope`). Honouring both inputs with query taking precedence keeps the wire shape symmetric across publish and read while still letting SDK builders that can't easily mutate the URL pass the branch in the body. Don't surface this ambiguity as a 400 — that would break SDKs that do both for belt-and-braces and is a footgun for no real benefit.
- `WithBranchScope(r.Context(), branchID)` MUST wrap the cycle-detection call site even though the ctx is not threaded into the persistence call below it. Reason: `detectFunctionCallCycle` walks the call graph through `repo.GetFunctionByName` and `GetFunctionByNameVersion`, both of which read `BranchScopeFromContext(ctx)`. Without the wrap, a branch publish whose new code calls `weave.callFunction("helper")` would resolve `helper` against main instead of the branch's overlay, and a cycle that exists on the branch overlay (A→B → branch publish B' → B→A) could slip through validation. The persistence call below it doesn't need the same ctx because the row's own `BranchID` field is the source of truth at INSERT time — but the cycle scan reads OTHER rows from the repo and those reads need the scope.
- The 409 envelope `parameters.branchId` is the canonical disambiguator for "which branch did the duplicate hit" — with US-384's widened UNIQUE shape, the same name@version pair can legitimately exist on N branches and the bare `name`+`version` from the legacy envelope is no longer enough to identify the conflict. Pattern: any error response whose root cause depends on a column that branch-overlays widened MUST surface that column in `parameters` so SDKs/UIs can render unambiguous messages.
- Test mockRepo branch-awareness pattern: when the production read path consults `BranchScopeFromContext(ctx)` AND filters with a SQL `WHERE branch_id IN (current, 'main')` AND post-filters with `preferBranch*`, the in-memory mock must reproduce ALL THREE behaviours to give handler tests fidelity. The `mockPreferBranchFunctions` helper in `pkg/oms/mock_functions_test.go` mirrors `pkg/oms/pg_repository.go::preferBranchFunctions` with identical semantics — when adding new branch-aware repo read methods, expect to add the corresponding mock helper too. Without this, handler tests would silently miss branch-specific routing bugs (the in-memory mock would always return both main and branch rows, masking the production "branch row wins" predicate).
- The legacy `CreateFunction` test in `handlers_function_test.go::TestCreateFunction` continues to pass unchanged after the request struct widening because `BranchID` is `omitempty` on the wire and the resolution path normalises the empty string to `DefaultBranch`. New optional fields on existing publish APIs should always default-normalise so legacy callers don't need a code change.
- The repository's `UpdateFunction` was deliberately NOT widened to honour a branch override on the body. The branch is set at create time and immutable thereafter — to "modify a function on a branch" the user creates a NEW row with the same `(name, version)` on the target branch (the standard publish flow). This matches how branch-aware ActionType writes work in US-384 and avoids the "cross-branch UPDATE" footgun that would land a branch-tagged row pointing at main's underlying RID.

## 2026-05-03 - US-390 - PITR 时间点恢复

**Implementation:**
- `internal/cliclient/client.go`: added `DatasetTransaction`, `CreateDatasetTransactionRequest`, `PITRRollbackResponse`, `DatasetHistoryResponse` wire types mirroring `pkg/oms.DatasetTransaction` and the US-388 rollback handler shapes. Three new methods: `CreateDatasetTransaction(ctx, datasetRID, *req)` POSTs `/api/v2/datasets/{rid}/transactions`, `RollbackDataset(ctx, datasetRID, targetTxID)` POSTs `/api/v2/datasets/{rid}/rollback?to=<tx>` with `url.QueryEscape` on the target, `DatasetHistory(ctx, datasetRID)` GETs `/api/v2/datasets/{rid}/history`. The cliclient layer is intentionally permissive about target-id validation (no client-side prefix check) — the server is the source of truth and surfaces `InvalidRollbackTarget` cleanly. The CLI command layer enforces the prefix as a UX-side guardrail.
- `cmd/weave-cli/cmd_pitr.go` (new): `runPitr` dispatches `restore` / `history`. `runPitrRestore` parses `--dataset` + `--to-tx` (both required) and an optional `--json` flag, validates the `tx-` prefix client-side using `oms.DatasetTransactionIDPrefix` (avoiding a redundant server round-trip on obvious typos), invokes `c.RollbackDataset`, and renders a human summary via `printRollbackSummary` (rolled-back tx ids + restored/deleted counts + new chain head) or raw JSON. `runPitrHistory` lists the per-ontology transaction chain in tabwriter format (TX ID / PARENT / COMMITTED AT / EDITS / ROLLED BACK marker), which is the canonical "pick a target before restoring" workflow.
- `cmd/weave-cli/main.go`: registered `pitr` in the dispatch switch + usage banner.
- `cmd/weave-cli/cmd_pitr_test.go` (new — 7 unit tests): covers happy-path POST shape with rolled-back tx ids in stdout, JSON output mode, missing `--dataset` and `--to-tx` flags reporting usage, target-prefix client-side rejection, server-error propagation through exit code + stderr (`RollbackTargetNotFound` envelope), unknown subcommand handling, and the `pitr history` listing.
- `internal/cliclient/client_test.go` (4 new tests): `TestRollbackDataset` verifies the URL shape + response decode, `TestRollbackDatasetSurfacesAPIError` verifies `*APIError` round-trip on 404, `TestCreateDatasetTransactionStampsBody` verifies the optional body is posted, `TestDatasetHistoryParsesTransactions` verifies the history-endpoint shape.
- `cmd/server/pitr_integration_test.go` (new — 2 integration tests): `TestPITR_ThreeWritesRestoreToSecondDropsThirdWrite` is the AC chokepoint — seeds tx-1 / tx-2 / tx-3, builds the real `dataset_rollback_handler` with the existing `fakeDatasetTxWriter` / `fakeAffectedStore` / `fakeHistorySnapshot` / `recordingIndex` test fakes (re-used from `dataset_rollback_handler_test.go`), wraps it in `httptest.Server`, drives the rollback through `cliclient.RollbackDataset`, and asserts every consequence (pk=A re-indexed to t2 state {price:200}, pk=C deleted, pk=B left alone, tx-3 marked rolled-back, tx-2 NOT marked, bookkeeping row's parent points at tx-2). `TestPITR_HistoryThenRestoreFlow` exercises the canonical operator workflow: `c.DatasetHistory` to list, then pick the oldest tx, then `c.RollbackDataset`. Both tests use the cliclient → real handler flow so the wire shape is exercised end-to-end.
- New helper `newCombinedDatasetRouter(historyH, rollbackR)` mounts the GET history route alongside the existing rollback POST router on a single chi router — the production `cmd/server/main.go` mounts these on the same `api` subrouter (lines 990 / 1012-1013) so the test composition mirrors production.

**Files changed:**

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- US-388 already shipped the rollback HTTP surface (POST /api/v2/datasets/{rid}/rollback?to=tx-...). US-390's job is to add a CLI binding + the explicit AC-level "3 writes → restore to 2nd → 3rd write disappears" integration test that exercises the *whole flow* end-to-end through the cliclient. The two stories split cleanly: US-388 = handler + per-store fakes per-handler tests; US-390 = client + CLI command + an integration test that drives the handler via HTTP. Pattern: when a CLI story sits on top of an already-shipped HTTP story, the CLI tests should cover (a) URL/body shape, (b) flag parsing, (c) error propagation; the integration test that proves "the feature works" should drive through the cliclient against a real handler instance.
- The `cmd/weave-cli/` test package and the `cmd/server/` test package both target `package main`, so neither can `import` the other. The integration test landed in `cmd/server/` because it needs the real `dataset_rollback_handler` + the existing `fakeDatasetTxWriter` / `fakeAffectedStore` / `fakeHistorySnapshot` / `recordingIndex` fakes from `dataset_rollback_handler_test.go`. Reusing those fakes (rather than duplicating them in `cmd/weave-cli/`) keeps the test surface narrow and avoids drift between the two suites' notion of "what counts as a fake history snapshot". The cliclient is the bridge: importable from either side.
- `url.QueryEscape` on the rollback target is the canonical safe shape — even though the current target id pattern (`tx-<32-hex>`) doesn't need escaping, a future tx-id format that includes path-reserved characters should not silently break the URL. Don't string-concatenate query params; always go through `net/url`.
- The CLI's client-side `tx-` prefix check is a UX guardrail, NOT a security boundary — the server validates again with the canonical `oms.DatasetTransactionIDPrefix` constant (US-388's `InvalidRollbackTarget` 400). The CLI uses the same `pkg/oms` constant rather than a string literal so the two checks stay in sync if the prefix ever changes. Pattern: when both the client and server validate the same shape, share the constant via `pkg/<domain>` rather than duplicating string literals — duplicates drift silently.
- `chi.NewRouter().Mount("/", subRouter)` is the canonical way to compose two pre-existing chi routers into one. The `Mount` doesn't strip the prefix, so the inner router's full URL pattern is preserved. This let `newCombinedDatasetRouter` add the GET history route alongside the entire `newDatasetRollbackRouter` without re-declaring the rollback-specific URL pattern.
- The integration test deliberately sets pk=B as "last-touched at t2" → NOT in the affected-keys set → replay leaves pk=B alone. This is the correct behaviour because pk=B's last write IS already the t2 state, so re-indexing it would be wasted work. The fake store's affected-keys map mirrors what the production `ListAffectedKeysSince(ontology, t2)` SQL would return: only PKs touched by transactions strictly after t2.
- The cliclient response decoder accepts the server's `time.Time` JSON encoding (`"2026-05-03T00:00:00Z"`) without any custom `UnmarshalJSON` because `time.Time` round-trips RFC3339 by default. If a future endpoint emits `time.Duration` or another type that doesn't round-trip cleanly, add a per-type `UnmarshalJSON` rather than touching the decoder.
- The CLI's restore summary intentionally does NOT echo the target tx (`Rollback to tx-1: ...` already names it), but DOES echo the new chain head and parent. This mirrors what an operator typically wants in the terminal: "what did I undo, what's the new state". Anything richer (full diff of restored fields, etc.) belongs in `--json` output for piping into jq.

## 2026-05-03 - US-391 - App 容器与 Layout DSL

**Implementation:**
- New migration `000094_apps_app_versions.{up,down}.sql` adds two tables: `apps` (rid TEXT PK, name TEXT, owner_id TEXT, layout_json JSONB, version INT ≥1, created_at, updated_at; UNIQUE on (owner_id, name)) and `app_versions` (composite PK (app_rid, version) with `app_rid REFERENCES apps(rid) ON DELETE CASCADE`, name + layout_json snapshot, created_by, created_at). The version-DESC index on `app_versions(app_rid, version DESC)` keeps the rollback UI's "newest first" listing index-only.
- New package `pkg/apps` with the canonical Workshop-lite App Editor surface:
  - `pkg/apps/layout.go`: Layout DSL types + `ValidateLayout(json.RawMessage) error`. Three node types — `row` (children []Node, must be cols, sum widths ≤12), `col` (width 1..12 strict integer, single child), `component` (componentType non-empty string + opaque props). Validator uses `json.Decoder` with `UseNumber` so 6.5 widths are rejected even before the range check. Strict decode rejects trailing JSON noise. `MaxLayoutDepth = 32` recursion cap so a pathological client can't blow the Go stack. Errors carry a JSON path (`children[0].child.componentType`) so the SPA editor can highlight the offending node, plus an `ErrInvalidLayout` sentinel for `errors.Is` at the store/handler boundary.
  - `pkg/apps/model.go`: `App{RID, Name, OwnerID, LayoutJSON, Version, CreatedAt, UpdatedAt}` + `AppVersion{AppRID, Version, Name, LayoutJSON, CreatedAt, CreatedBy}` + `Update{Name, LayoutJSON}` partial-update DTO. `MaxNameLength=128` + `ValidateName` mirror the dashboards pattern.
  - `pkg/apps/store.go`: narrow `Store` interface (Create / Get / List / Update / Delete / ListVersions / GetVersion). `MemoryStore` implementation for tests + degraded-mode deployments. Every `Create` stamps Version=1 and records the initial snapshot; every `Update` increments Version and appends a fresh snapshot — even a no-op DTO bumps Version because the contract is "every Update is auditable history". Validation runs before any state mutation so a bad layout cannot leave the live row half-mutated. ErrNotFound is owner-scoped (cross-owner read = NotFound regardless of existence).
  - `pkg/apps/handlers.go`: 7 chi routes — list / create / get / update / delete / list versions / get version. Auth-required; `nil store` falls through to a 500 `AppsUnavailable` envelope so degraded routers can keep their /api/v2 prefix mounted. Layout-validation failures surface as 400 `InvalidAppLayout` with the path field copied from the underlying `*LayoutError`. Conflicts → 409 `AppNameConflict`; missing → 404 `AppNotFound`; bad version path segment → 400 `InvalidAppVersion`. Update PUT re-validates name + layout BEFORE forwarding to the store so a malformed request is rejected at the handler layer.
- `cmd/server/apps_store.go` (new): concrete `pgAppsStore` implementing `apps.Store` against the `apps` + `app_versions` tables. Create + Update wrap the live-row mutation AND the `app_versions` insert in one transaction so the two surfaces never drift; Update uses `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` on the live row to make the version bump race-free across concurrent writers. PG unique-violation (SQLSTATE 23505) on (owner_id, name) maps to `apps.ErrNameConflict`. Layout JSONB writes go through `layoutForWrite` so a nil RawMessage lands as `'{}'` not the literal string `'null'`.
- `cmd/server/main.go`:
  - New import `github.com/liyang/weave/pkg/apps`.
  - `ServerDeps` gains `AppsStore apps.Store` (US-391 docstring) — nil in degraded mode.
  - Route mount: `if deps.AppsStore != nil { apps.NewHandler(deps.AppsStore).RegisterRoutes(api) }` next to the existing `dashboards` mount.
  - PG bootstrap: `deps.AppsStore = newPGAppsStore(pool)` immediately after the dashboards wiring + a `[apps] store wired` log line.
- The /api/v2/apps/* routes are intentionally NOT in `api/openapi.yaml` because the contract test router leaves `deps.AppsStore` nil — same pattern as dashboards. When future stories (US-392+) flesh out the SPA App Editor surface and wire AppsStore in the contract router, both files (`api/openapi.yaml` + `cmd/server/openapi_spec.yaml`) will need entries for these 7 routes; until then the contract tests stay green.

**Files changed:**
- `migrations/000094_apps_app_versions.up.sql` (new)
- `migrations/000094_apps_app_versions.down.sql` (new)
- `pkg/apps/layout.go` (new — Layout DSL + ValidateLayout + MaxLayoutDepth/MaxColumns + ErrInvalidLayout sentinel)
- `pkg/apps/model.go` (new — App / AppVersion / Update + ValidateName)

- `go build ./...` clean

- `go test ./...` all green across the full ~80-package surface
