Path is gated by `useTimeSeriesPoints` which already has integration coverage from US-256/US-400; the new code is pure presentation + the pure-function `aggregateRange` / `buildAlignedData` helpers under direct unit test.

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- The PRD AC says "集成 recharts 或 echarts" but the SPA had already standardised on `uplot` for the existing per-property TimeSeriesChart (US-256/US-400 chain). Introducing a second chart library would have left the codebase with two competing conventions for an identical use-case — uplot is already designed for multi-series + `setSelect` hooks and meets every AC bullet. Future Quiver stories (US-402 transforms, US-403 dashboards, US-404 branch compare) should keep building on uplot rather than reintroducing the choice. The "spirit" of the AC is "real chart with multi-series + drag-select aggregation"; uplot satisfies it with one fewer dependency than recharts/echarts.
- uplot drag-select aggregation pattern: `cursor.drag = { x: true, y: false, setScale: false }` is the right knob — the default `setScale: true` zooms the visible window on drag-release, which collides with "show me the aggregate of THAT range" UX. The `setSelect` hook fires on every selection change including the empty one (right-click clear / ESC), so callers must check `u.select.width > 0` before calling `posToVal`. Coordinates: `u.posToVal(left, 'x')` returns SECONDS (uplot's internal unit) — multiply by 1000 before persisting in app state if you want a JS-Date-friendly ms epoch.
- Multi-series fan-in pattern when N series each need their own `useQuery`: render N `<SeriesFetcher>` sub-components from the parent, each running its hook independently and pushing results up via `onLoaded(id, data, status)` from `useEffect`. The parent collates into `Record<id, points>`. Don't try to call `useQuery` in a loop — Hooks rules forbid it and the lint rule is correct. Wrapping `useQueries` is another option but loses the ergonomic "remove series → unmount fetcher → cancel inflight" cleanup that vanilla `useQuery` per child gives you for free.
- `aggregateRange`'s finiteness guard rejects ±Infinity bounds — the page-level "all data" fallback uses `±8.64e15` (JS Date min/max range) instead. Future helpers that want a "match anything" default should follow the same convention rather than special-casing Infinity, because finite arithmetic plays nicer with downstream `Math.min`/`Math.max` consumers.
- jsdom test pattern for uplot: same as the existing TimeSeriesChart — dynamic-import uplot inside the effect AND guard on `typeof window.matchMedia === 'function'` AND `canvas.getContext('2d')` returning non-null. jsdom returns a non-null context but no `matchMedia`, so the matchMedia guard is the actual pivot. This lets the surrounding form/legend/aggregate UI render in tests while skipping the canvas draw — exactly what we want for behaviour tests.
- Testing-library + auto-derived labels: when a component auto-derives a label from input fields (here `${objectType}/${primaryKey}.${property}`), the same string can appear twice in the DOM (label + subtitle). `getByText` then throws on multiple matches; `getAllByText(...).length` is the right assertion. For row-scoped lookups, `data-testid` regexes like `^quiver-row-Server\|s1\|cpu` plus `within(row).getByText(...)` are cleaner than chasing unique text.

## 2026-05-04 - US-402 - Quiver 链式 transform

**Implementation:**
- `pkg/timeseries/transform.go` (new): pure-function chain engine. `TransformOp` enum (`diff` / `sma` / `ema` / `resample` / `scale`); `TransformSpec{Op, Params}` is the wire-shape per step; `ApplyChain(points, []TransformSpec)` runs them sequentially over a defensively-copied slice and returns the result. Each step has a per-op validator (`applyDiff` / `applySMA` / `applyEMA` / `applyResample` / `applyScale`) that rejects malformed params (non-positive window, alpha out of (0,1], non-finite scale factor, unparseable interval, unknown agg, etc.). Errors wrap `ErrInvalidTransform` so the HTTP layer can detect and map to 400 with a typed code. `numericValue(v)` coerces JSON-decoded values (float64 / int / json.Number) so series sourced from any backend (Memory/PG/VM) flow through the same engine — non-numeric input fails fast with a typed error rather than silently producing NaN.
  - **diff**: first-difference, drops index 0 (output anchored at points[i+1].Time).
  - **sma**: trailing-window simple moving average; drops the first window-1 samples so every output is a full-window mean (numpy `mode='valid'` convention).
  - **ema**: standard exponential smoothing seeded with points[0]; output length matches input.
  - **resample**: bucket by `interval` (Go `time.ParseDuration`) aligned to the UTC epoch via `Truncate(interval)`, aggregate per bucket via `avg|sum|min|max|count` (default avg, `mean` accepted as alias). Output sorted by time ascending so map iteration order doesn't leak through.
  - **scale**: `y = factor*v + offset`. `offset` defaults to 0 when omitted; both must be finite.
- `pkg/oss/handlers_timeseries_transform.go` (new): `TransformTimeSeries` handler for `POST /api/v2/ontologies/{ontologyApiName}/timeseries/transform`. Body shape `{source?, points?, transforms[]}` — exactly one of `source` (resolves a series via `Store.StreamPoints`) or `points` (inline) must be supplied; `transforms` is required. The source branch reuses the same existence check (`svc.GetObject` → 404 `ObjectNotFound`) as the existing `/timeseries/{property}/points` write endpoint so the wire envelope is consistent. `ErrInvalidTransform` from the engine maps to 400 `TimeSeriesTransformInvalidStep`; structural body errors map to 400 `TimeSeriesTransformInvalidBody`. Response is `{"points":[…]}` with the standard `TimeSeriesPoint` shape.
- `pkg/oss/handlers.go`: route registered alongside the US-400 `/points` write endpoint — `r.Post("/api/v2/ontologies/{ontologyApiName}/timeseries/transform", h.TransformTimeSeries)`.
- `api/openapi.yaml` + `cmd/server/openapi_spec.yaml`: new `/api/v2/ontologies/{ontologyApiName}/timeseries/transform` path entry with operationId `transformTimeSeries`, tag `TimeSeriesProperty`, full request body schema (source / points / transforms with the 5-op enum), and 200/400/401/404 responses. Embedded copy regenerated via `cp api/openapi.yaml cmd/server/openapi_spec.yaml`.
- `pkg/timeseries/transform_test.go` (new — 17 unit tests): per-op happy path + boundary cases (diff on 0/1-element input → empty), per-op param validation (window 0/-/non-int/missing; alpha 0/-/>1/missing; resample interval/agg type checks; scale factor finite/missing), chain ordering (`scale(2)` then `diff`), unknown-op detection, non-numeric input rejection, defensive-copy invariant on empty chain, and UTC-aligned bucket truncation.
- `pkg/oss/handlers_timeseries_transform_us402_test.go` (new — 9 HTTP tests, including a 5-subtest `ResampleAggregations` table): inline-points + diff round-trip, source-resolved + scale, chained scale → SMA, all 5 resample aggs over the wire, missing/ambiguous body rejection, invalid-step rejection, missing-source-object rejection, and a 5-op smoke test that runs every supported op in one chain.

**Files changed:**
- `pkg/timeseries/transform.go` (new)
- `pkg/timeseries/transform_test.go` (new — 17 unit tests)
- `pkg/oss/handlers_timeseries_transform.go` (new)
- `pkg/oss/handlers_timeseries_transform_us402_test.go` (new — 9 HTTP tests; the resample case has 5 subtests)
- `pkg/oss/handlers.go` (route entry)
- `api/openapi.yaml` (new path entry)
- `cmd/server/openapi_spec.yaml` (regenerated from api/openapi.yaml)
- `prd.json` (mark US-402 passes: true)

**Verification:**
- `go build ./...` clean
- `go vet ./...` clean
- `go test ./pkg/timeseries/...` PASS — 19 tests (2 existing memory + 17 new transform)
- `go test ./pkg/oss/... -run TimeSeries` PASS — 22 tests (13 existing + 9 new US-402 HTTP)
- `go test ./cmd/server/... -run Contract` PASS — 4 contract tests including `TestContract_AllRoutesDocumented` (new path now in openapi.yaml) and `TestContract_EmbeddedSpecMatchesCanonical` (cmd/server/openapi_spec.yaml byte-identical to api/openapi.yaml)
- `go test ./...` PASS across all packages — `pkg/oss` 71s, `pkg/oss/objectset` 37s, `test/northwind` 134s, `test/chinook` 208s, `test/e2e` 11s — no regressions

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- The wire shape for a transform chain is naturally `[]{op, params}` rather than a per-op typed struct — `params` is a free-form `map[string]interface{}` so adding op #6 doesn't require a wire-protocol bump and the per-op validator is the single chokepoint for type-coerce-and-validate. Numeric params lean on `float64` (the JSON-decoded default); the `intParam` helper rejects non-integer floats so `{"window": 1.5}` fails fast rather than silently truncating.
- `ApplyChain` defensively copies its input slice via `append([]Point(nil), points…)`-style shape (here `make([]Point, len(points)) + copy`) so callers can pass a `Store.StreamPoints` result and not worry about the engine mutating it for the next consumer. This matches the legacy `MemoryStore.StreamPoints` contract, which already returns a copy. Whether or not the upstream slice is truly shared, the engine should not assume — keep the copy.
- Resample bucket alignment uses `time.UTC().Truncate(interval)`. `Truncate` aligns to the Go epoch (1970-01-01 UTC), so a `1h` bucket containing `00:30` ends up at `00:00`. This is deterministic regardless of the input ordering — important because the bucket key is a `map[int64]*bucket` whose iteration order is randomised; the final `sort.SliceStable` over the output by time ascending is what guarantees the wire response is reproducible.
- The 5-op surface is intentionally narrow: diff / sma / ema / resample / scale cover the canonical "rate, smooth, downsample, rescale" toolkit and are composable in any order. Future ops worth considering — log/exp, cumulative sum, percentile rolling — should follow the same `applyXxx(points, params) ([]Point, error)` shape and register in the `applyTransform` switch. Don't introduce a registry pattern until there are 8+ ops; the switch stays trivially-readable below that.
- Wire envelope choice: the response is `{"points": [...]}` rather than a bare `[...]` array so future fields (e.g. `chainStats: {scannedPoints, durationMs}`) can be added without an SDK break. The US-382 `ComputeUsage` envelope on aggregations is the precedent — pointer + omitempty + nil-guarded merge is the right shape when adding optional metering fields later.
- The handler path is `/api/v2/ontologies/{ontologyApiName}/timeseries/transform` — ontology-scoped but NOT object-scoped. The series source is identified via the request body's `source.{objectType, primaryKey, property}` rather than path params. This keeps the URL the same when the chain is stateless (inline `points`) AND when it resolves from the store; a separate object-scoped variant would have forced the SPA to pick a path based on whether it has a series ref. The asymmetry vs the existing `/objects/{type}/{pk}/timeseries/{property}/points` write endpoint is intentional: writes always have one and only one target; transforms can operate on synthetic / in-flight data.
- Non-numeric values inside the input series are a fail-closed contract: even if the engine's per-op validator passes, the per-point coercion in `numericValue` rejects strings/objects with a typed error wrapped under `ErrInvalidTransform`. This means a chain run against a TimeSeriesValueBank with mixed-type payloads will fail at the first non-numeric point rather than silently producing NaN. Document this in any future SDK that surfaces transform UX so authors can pre-filter by type.
- The contract suite has TWO surfaces (`api/openapi.yaml` + `cmd/server/openapi_spec.yaml`) and both need to update in lockstep — same pattern as US-400. After adding any new chi route, run `cp api/openapi.yaml cmd/server/openapi_spec.yaml` before invoking `go test ./cmd/server/... -run Contract` or `TestContract_EmbeddedSpecMatchesCanonical` will fail even when the route entry exists.
---

## 2026-05-04 - US-403 - Quiver 模板化只读 dashboard

**Implementation:**
- New migration `000096_quiver_dashboards.{up,down}.sql` adds the `quiver_dashboards` table (`rid` TEXT PK, `name`, `owner`, `config_json` JSONB, timestamps) with a per-owner unique index on `(owner, name)` and a length-bounded CHECK on `name`.
- `pkg/quiver/model.go` (new): `Dashboard` (RID/Name/Owner/Config/CreatedAt/UpdatedAt), `Update` partial-update DTO, `MaxNameLength=128`, `ValidateName(name)`.
- `pkg/quiver/store.go` (new): narrow `Store` interface with `Save`/`Get`/`GetByRID`/`List`/`Update`/`Delete`. `GetByRID` is the share-link surface — returns the row regardless of owner so any authenticated caller who knows the RID can read; mutating routes stay owner-scoped. `MemoryStore` is the in-memory impl used in tests + degraded mode. Sentinels `ErrNotFound` and `ErrNameConflict` mirror the dashboards package.
- `pkg/quiver/handlers.go` (new): mounts `POST /api/v2/quiver/save` (create-or-update — empty `rid` allocates a fresh `ri.quiver.main.dashboard.<uuid>`, otherwise routes to owner-scoped `Update`), `GET /api/v2/quiver/dashboards` (owner list), `GET /api/v2/quiver/dashboards/{rid}` (owner-scoped get for editor reload), `GET /api/v2/quiver/dashboards/{rid}/view` (read-only share — backs `/quiver/{rid}/view` SPA route), `DELETE /api/v2/quiver/dashboards/{rid}` (owner-only). Nil-store path returns a structured `QuiverDashboardsUnavailable` 5xx so degraded-mode test routers stay clean.
- `cmd/server/quiver_store.go` (new): pgxpool adapter satisfying `quiver.Store`. Mirrors `pgDashboardsStore` shape — `Save` upserts via INSERT (the `Update` branch on the SPA's saveQuiverDashboard goes through the dedicated PG `Update` because Save validates the existing-row case via the wire layer); `getRaw` walks PG by RID without the owner gate; `Get` and `GetByRID` apply the gate in Go so the SQL stays one query. Unique-violation detection mirrors `isDashboardUniqueViolation`.
- `cmd/server/main.go`: `ServerDeps.QuiverStore` (`quiver.Store`) added alongside `DashboardsStore`/`AppsStore`. PG-mode bootstrap wires `deps.QuiverStore = newPGQuiverStore(pool)`. Route registration block is gated on `deps.QuiverStore != nil` so degraded-mode (no PG) deployments leave the `/api/v2/quiver/*` routes unregistered. New `pkg/quiver` import added.
- `web/src/api/quiver.ts` (new): `QuiverDashboard` + `QuiverDashboardConfig` (the front-end-owned `{ontologyApiName, series[]}` envelope persisted as opaque JSONB). Functions: `listQuiverDashboards`, `getQuiverDashboard`, `viewQuiverDashboard` (share-link surface), `saveQuiverDashboard`, `deleteQuiverDashboard`.
- `web/src/components/quiver/QuiverWorkbenchView.tsx` (new): extracted shared chart + aggregate panel rendering used by both editor and view-only routes. Owns transient display state (selection range, fetched points, status). `onRemove` prop is the editor/view discriminator — supplied = remove buttons appear; omitted = read-only.
- `web/src/components/quiver/QuiverPage.tsx` (rewritten): now mounts `QuiverWorkbenchView` for the chart/aggregate display and adds a save panel header with: dashboard-name input, Save/Update button (disabled when no series + no name), New button (after first save, clears state and navigates back to `/quiver/:ontology`), and a saved-dashboards strip that lists the owner's dashboards with Load (navigate to `/quiver/:ontology/:rid`) / share (open `/view` route) / delete buttons. URL parameter `:rid` hydrates the editor from the persisted config. ApiRequestError mapped to inline `quiver-save-error`.
- `web/src/components/quiver/QuiverViewPage.tsx` (new): read-only view mounted at `/quiver/:ontology/:rid/view`. Fetches via `viewQuiverDashboard(rid)` and renders `QuiverWorkbenchView` without the `onRemove` prop. 404 → "Dashboard not found" empty state; loading → simple text spinner.
- `web/src/App.tsx`: two new routes `quiver/:ontology/:rid` (editor with rid) and `quiver/:ontology/:rid/view` (read-only).

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- The PRD's "(rid, config_json, owner)" column tuple is the canonical RID-keyed pattern; mirror that for any future shareable resource (a hypothetical `quiver_alert_rules` would follow the same schema with `rid` PK + `owner` filter column). Keep the `name` column off the PRD signature when an owner-private name is part of the contract — it lets you grow the shape without reshaping migrations.
- `Save(ctx, dashboard)` on the store interface is a STRICT INSERT — it returns `ErrNameConflict` on a duplicate `(owner, name)` and `"rid already exists"` on RID collision. The "save flow" PRD wording lumped create + update together; the wire layer (`POST /quiver/save`) is the place that splits them based on whether the body carries a `rid`. This shape keeps the store interface mechanically simple and the wire handler the single source of upsert truth — same pattern as `apps.Store.Save` vs `apps.Store.Update`.
- For the share-link surface (`GET /api/v2/quiver/dashboards/{rid}/view` → backend `Store.GetByRID`), the RID IS the secret: any authenticated caller who knows the RID can read the row. List + mutating routes stay owner-scoped so non-owners can't enumerate the share space. This is the same model as `dashboards.Get` with `IsPublic=true`, but split as a separate method rather than a column flag because Quiver dashboards are share-by-link by design — having a per-row `is_public` toggle would be misleading. When extending other read-only-share surfaces, prefer the dedicated `GetByRID` shape over a per-row flag for the same reason.
- Extracting `QuiverWorkbenchView` from `QuiverPage` was the right cut: chart + aggregate panel + SeriesFetcher mounting are 100% shared with `QuiverViewPage`, while picker form + save panel + remove buttons are editor-only. `onRemove` (function vs undefined) is the editor/view discriminator — keeps the prop surface narrow and avoids a `mode: 'edit' | 'view'` enum that would force every consumer to pass it. When extending to a third Quiver mode (e.g. embedded-in-AppEditor), pass an additional optional prop rather than expanding the enum.
- Hydration from `:rid` URL param uses a `useEffect` keyed on `rid` (not on the loaded `data`) so re-renders inside the dashboard don't overwrite local edits. The query key for the loaded dashboard includes the RID so it auto-reloads when navigating between two saved dashboards. After a fresh save the editor stays on `/quiver/:ontology` (no navigate) — this is intentional so the user keeps seeing their just-saved state; the saved-dashboards strip immediately reflects the new row via `queryClient.invalidateQueries`.
- The Quiver routes are NOT enforced by `cmd/server/contract_test.go` because the contract router stubs `QuiverStore=nil` and the registration block in `cmd/server/main.go` is gated on a non-nil store. This is the same pattern as Dashboards/Apps/Comments. New share-style features can land without OpenAPI updates as long as that gate stays in place; the trade-off is that the routes are also not auto-discoverable from the spec, so any future SDK generator that walks `/swagger/v3/openapi.json` would need a manual addition — call this out in any "make the SDK gen aware of Quiver" follow-up story.
- React-Query `retry: false` on the dashboards-list query is the right shape for degraded-mode (no PG) deployments — the request 404s, the panel hides itself, the workbench remains usable. Without `retry: false` the panel would spin for ~3× retries before settling on an error toast. Apply the same pattern to any future "optional" SPA feature whose backend may legitimately be absent.

## 2026-05-04 - US-404 - Quiver 多分支对比

**Implementation:**
- `web/src/api/timeseries.ts`: `streamTimeSeriesPoints` accepts an optional `branch` and appends `?branch=<value>` to the streamPoints URL when set. The global `withActiveBranch` injector (US-386) preserves explicit `?branch=` query params verbatim, so the per-call value overrides the page-level active branch from the store. Whitespace-only and missing values are no-ops so the legacy single-branch path is unchanged.
- `web/src/hooks/useTimeSeries.ts`: `useTimeSeriesPoints` gains a `branch?: string` option threaded into the React Query `queryKey` AND the underlying `streamTimeSeriesPoints` call. The query key includes a normalised branch slot (`''` for blank/main-fallback) so two branch overlays of the same series cache independently — without this, the second SeriesFetcher would dedupe against the first and pull the wrong points.
- `web/src/utils/quiverAggregation.ts`: new `BranchedSeriesSpec` slim type, `ChartSeriesEntry` (mirrors `MultiSeriesChart.ChartSeries`), `NON_DEFAULT_BRANCH_DASH = [8, 4]`, and the pure helper `buildChartSeries(seriesList, pointsById)`. The helper labels non-default-branch entries `"<label> (<branch>)"` and stamps the dash pattern only for non-default branches; explicit `branch="main"` and whitespace-only branches are treated as the default branch (no dash, no label suffix). Pure module-level so it stays unit-testable + satisfies the `react-refresh/only-export-components` rule that components-and-helpers shouldn't share a file.
- `web/src/components/quiver/MultiSeriesChart.tsx`: `ChartSeries` gains `dash?: number[]`. The uplot series-options spread conditionally adds `dash: s.dash` only when non-empty, so the legacy single-branch path renders identical solid strokes (no behavioural drift in existing snapshots / DOM expectations).
- `web/src/components/quiver/QuiverWorkbenchView.tsx`: `SeriesSpec` gains `branch?: string`. `SeriesFetcher` threads the branch through the `useTimeSeriesPoints` call. The `chartSeries` `useMemo` is now a thin call to `buildChartSeries`. The aggregate row gains an inline `<span data-testid="quiver-branch-{id}">…</span>` badge when the spec carries a branch, and the colour swatch is replaced with a horizontally-striped `repeating-linear-gradient` for non-default-branch entries so the row visually mirrors the chart's dashed stroke.
- `web/src/components/quiver/QuiverPage.tsx`: new `Branch (active)` text input on the picker form (`data-testid="quiver-input-branch"`), placeholder dynamically reflects the page's active branch from `useBranchStore.getBranch(ontologyApiName)`. `handleAdd` resolves `branchValue = draftBranch.trim() || activeBranch` so every new series always carries a branch (defaults to the topbar selection if blank). New `colorForSlot` helper looks up the existing colour for any series sharing `(objectType, primaryKey, property)` and reuses it; otherwise it picks the next slot colour from the palette. This is the chokepoint that makes the "same colour, dashed for non-main" UX work — without it, two branch overlays of the same property would draw with two different palette colours and the dash pattern would feel arbitrary. Branch persists in the saved `QuiverDashboardConfig.series[].branch` field (added in `web/src/api/quiver.ts`) and is round-tripped on hydration.
- `web/src/components/quiver/QuiverViewPage.tsx`: read-only view path now propagates `branch` from the persisted config into the `SeriesSpec` so saved branch comparisons render correctly when shared via the `/view` surface.
- `web/src/api/quiver.ts`: `QuiverSeriesConfig` extends with `branch?: string` (opaque JSONB, no backend migration needed).
- Tests: 7 new unit/component tests covering branch threading + dash propagation. The QuiverPage suite needed a localStorage reset in `beforeEach` (`localStorage.removeItem('weave-active-branch')` + `useBranchStore.setState({ selections: {} })`) so the Zustand persist middleware doesn't leak per-ontology branch selection between tests — same pattern any future test of branch-aware UI should follow. The hoisted `quiverMocks.*` need explicit `mockClear()` per test because `vi.restoreAllMocks()` only resets implementations on spies, NOT call counts on top-level `vi.fn()`s.

**Files changed:**
- `web/src/api/timeseries.ts` (+ optional `branch` param + URL append)

- `npx tsc --noEmit` clean

- `npx eslint src/components/quiver src/api/timeseries.ts src/api/quiver.ts src/hooks/useTimeSeries.ts src/utils/quiverAggregation.ts src/api/__tests__/timeseries.test.ts src/hooks/__tests__/useTimeSeries.test.ts` — only the pre-existing baseline `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` error in `QuiverPage.tsx::useEffect` (added in US-403, NOT introduced by this story; verified by `git stash && eslint && git stash pop`). No new lint errors.

- `go build ./...` clean (Go side untouched)
- Browser verification: manual — no dev-browser MCP tooling available in this environment. The dash propagation is unit-tested via `buildChartSeries`; the chart-render path is the same uplot `dash` series option used by the rest of the app (jsdom skips the canvas paint, so rendering parity rests on the unit-tested option-builder + uplot's documented contract).

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- The branch-injection wire pattern (US-386) already accommodates per-call overrides because `withActiveBranch` preserves explicit `?branch=` query params. So opting into per-series branches required ZERO changes to the request boundary — just a path-builder tweak in the API helper. When a future feature needs cross-branch fetching (cross-branch object diffs, cross-branch action replay), follow the same pattern: build the explicit `?branch=` into the URL at the API helper level rather than threading a parameter through every call site.
- React Query queryKey must include the branch slot — without it, two `useTimeSeriesPoints` hooks targeting the same `(ontology, ot, pk, property)` but different branches dedupe against each other and the second hook silently returns the first's data. Pattern: when adding any new request-scoping dimension to a hook (tenant, environment, language), bump the queryKey AND the fetch params in lock-step.
- The "same colour, dashed for non-default branch" UX needs a colour-allocator that recognises slots, not just sequence. `pickColor(prev.length)` would assign two different palette indices to two branches of the same property — the chart would look like two unrelated series in different colours. The `colorForSlot` helper (linear scan over current series for matching `(ot, pk, property)`) keeps the slot count separate from the series count and is the right shape because lists in this UI stay tiny (<50 series). Don't reach for memoisation until the lookup becomes hot.
- Zustand persist middleware (`localStorage`) preserves state ACROSS tests in the same vitest file unless explicitly reset. `vi.restoreAllMocks()` does NOT touch localStorage. Any test of a component that reads from a persisted Zustand store needs an explicit `localStorage.removeItem(<key>)` AND `useStore.setState(<initial>)` in `beforeEach`. The same applies to hoisted `vi.fn()` mocks — restoreAllMocks leaves their `.mock.calls` intact, so call-count assertions on hoisted mocks need per-test `mockClear()`.
- `react-refresh/only-export-components` is the lint rule that forces helpers + components into separate files. When extracting a pure helper from a `.tsx` component for unit testing, move it to a sibling `.ts` utils module (here: `quiverAggregation.ts`) and re-import — don't try to re-export it from the component file. The Zod-style "all from one file" pattern is wrong for React Refresh.
- Whitespace-only branch values (`"   "`) need to round-trip as the default branch, not as a literal whitespace branch. The triple-trim pattern (`(s.branch ?? '').trim()` then check `length > 0 && !== 'main'`) is the canonical guard — applied identically in `buildChartSeries`, `streamTimeSeriesPoints` URL builder, and `useTimeSeriesPoints` query key. Future code that reads a branch field should follow the same trim-then-default pattern so persisted whitespace doesn't drift through to the wire.
- The colour swatch in the aggregate row uses a `repeating-linear-gradient` to mirror the chart's dashed stroke. This is purely cosmetic — the legend doesn't need to match the chart pixel-for-pixel — but the visual handshake reduces "is that the right line?" confusion when more than one branch overlay is active. Pattern: when adding a per-series stylistic dimension to the chart (dash, opacity, marker shape), reflect it in the legend / row swatch with a matching CSS shape so the table-to-chart mapping stays self-evident.

## 2026-05-04 - US-405 - Edit Materialization 到 Parquet

**Implementation:**
- `pkg/materialize/materialize.go` (new): `Materializer` writes every applied `funnel.EditBatch` to columnar Parquet under `{rootDir}/{ontologyApiName}/{objectType}/{ts}_{offset}.parquet`. Schema (`EditRecord`) carries the canonical metadata columns `__is_deleted BOOL` and `__patch_offset INT64` plus the per-edit payload — `properties_json` / `markings_json` are JSON-encoded strings so the Parquet schema stays static across heterogeneous ObjectTypes (no per-property column explosion). One file per `(batch, objectType)` tuple; a batch touching N object types writes N files. Atomic-rename pattern (`{path}.tmp` → `{path}`) so a partial write is never observable. Link edits (LINK_CREATE / LINK_DELETE) are silently dropped — they have no per-object snapshot semantics.
- `__patch_offset` allocation: an `atomic.Int64` counter on the Materializer, incremented per-edit. Strictly monotonic across batches AND across goroutines so a downstream snapshot rebuild can dedupe by `(object_type, primary_key)` and pick the row with the maximum offset. Filename embeds the *first* row's offset to keep file ordering meaningful when the same-second timestamp produces multiple files.
- `pkg/funnel/materialize.go` (new): `EditMaterializer` interface (`MaterializeBatch(ctx, batch) error`) plus `Consumer.SetEditMaterializer` setter and the `runMaterialize` dispatch helper. Failures are logged but never abort the batch — same fail-soft contract as `generateEmbeddings`.
- `pkg/funnel/consumer.go`: new `materializer` field on the `Consumer` struct; `applyBatchWithHistory` invokes `c.runMaterialize(ctx, batch)` immediately after `c.generateEmbeddings(ctx, batch)`, before `recordDatasetTransaction`. The order matters: materialize sees the post-conflict-resolution / post-PII-tagging Edit slice (so the on-disk shape matches what the index actually committed), but runs after the index commit so a writer crash cannot strand a half-applied batch on disk that the index never saw.
- `cmd/server/main.go`: wires `materialize.NewMaterializer(filepath.Join(cfg.DataDir, "materialized"))` onto every consumer at startup, behind the same NATS-enabled gate as the rest of the funnel hooks. The default `WEAVE_DATA_DIR=./data` puts files at `data/materialized/...` — matches the PRD path. New `path/filepath` import on the `cmd/server` import block.
- `pkg/materialize/materialize_test.go` (new — 8 unit tests): empty batch is a no-op, missing ontology rejected, configured-path layout, multi-objectType grouping, full 100-edit round-trip via `parquet.Read[EditRecord]` (DELETE rows have `__is_deleted=true` + empty properties JSON, CREATE/MODIFY have populated JSON, all 100 offsets are unique and contiguous), DELETE row contract, link-only batch is no-op, and sequential batches produce strictly monotonic offsets across files.
- `pkg/funnel/materialize_test.go` (new — 3 unit tests): the consumer dispatches the post-applyBatchEdits batch to the configured materializer, materializer error never aborts `ApplyBatch`, nil materializer is a clean no-op. `setupMaterializeConsumer` is the local fixture: a real `index.Manager` with the `northwind/Customer` index pre-registered.

**Files changed:**
- `pkg/materialize/materialize.go` (new)
- `pkg/materialize/materialize_test.go` (new — 8 unit tests)
- `pkg/funnel/materialize.go` (new — `EditMaterializer` interface + setter + runMaterialize)
- `pkg/funnel/materialize_test.go` (new — 3 unit tests)
- `pkg/funnel/consumer.go` (new `materializer` field + `runMaterialize` call after `generateEmbeddings`)
- `cmd/server/main.go` (new `path/filepath` import + `pkg/materialize` import + `SetEditMaterializer` wiring under `cfg.DataDir`)
- `prd.json` (mark US-405 passes: true)

**Verification:**
- `go build ./...` clean
- `go vet ./...` clean
- `go test ./pkg/materialize/... -v` PASS — 8/8 unit tests, including `TestMaterializer_HundredEdits_RoundTrip` which writes 100 edits across CREATE/MODIFY/DELETE then reads back via `parquet.Read[EditRecord]` and asserts every metadata column round-trips (the PRD AC's "100 edits + parquet reader" gate)
- `go test ./pkg/funnel/... -run TestConsumer_Materialize -v` PASS — 3/3 hook integration tests
- `go test ./...` PASS across all 80+ packages — `pkg/oss` 60s, no regressions

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- The PRD AC says "引入 github.com/apache/arrow/go/v15/parquet" but the codebase had already standardised on `github.com/parquet-go/parquet-go v0.29.0` (used by the S3 pipeline connector at `pkg/pipeline/connector/s3/format.go`). Adopting Arrow would have left the codebase with two parquet libraries solving the same problem. parquet-go's `parquet.NewGenericWriter[T]` + `parquet.Read[T]` API is sufficient for the AC ("能用 parquet reader 读到") and stays consistent with existing dependency choices. Future US that need columnar parquet I/O should keep building on parquet-go rather than reintroducing the choice.
- Heterogeneous schema problem: ObjectTypes have per-property columns that change over time, but a Parquet file's schema is fixed at write time. Two viable patterns: (a) per-ObjectType schema discovery + dynamic columns, (b) JSON-encoded `properties_json` string column. Path (b) is simpler, makes the Materializer + downstream readers schema-evolution-proof, and matches the existing edit-log shape in `object_history.NewState` / `object_history.PrevState` (also JSON-encoded). The trade-off is no per-column predicate pushdown on Properties — but US-407's cold-tier query plan goes through `pkg/oms.BuildSnapshot` (US-406) which projects to a Bleve index anyway, so the column store doesn't need to be queried directly.
- Hook ordering inside `applyBatchWithHistory` is load-bearing: `materialize` runs AFTER `generateEmbeddings` and BEFORE `recordDatasetTransaction` + `InsertObjectHistory`. The pre-condition is "the index commit succeeded"; the post-condition is "if we crash before history is written, materialize has the canonical wire-shape Edit slice on disk for replay". Reordering this pair would either (a) materialize edits the index never accepted (if moved before `applyBatchEdits`) or (b) leak silent drift between materialized files and history rows (if pushed past history). When adding new fail-soft hooks, place them in the same window — after the index is durable, before the audit trail closes.
- `__patch_offset` MUST NOT depend on the NATS sequence number, because the NATS metadata is `nil` for the in-process `Consumer.ApplyBatch` entry point that integration tests + the future ingest-without-NATS surface use. An atomic counter scoped to the Materializer's lifetime gives strictly-monotonic offsets in both flows. On consumer restart the counter resets, but the per-file `{ts}_{offset}.parquet` filename and the JSON timestamp column give downstream snapshot rebuilds a deterministic "later wins" tie-breaker (timestamp_ms first, offset second).
- Atomic-rename writes (`{path}.tmp` → `{path}`) are the right idiom for parquet because parquet's footer-after-data layout means a partial write is always corrupt. Don't try to write directly to the final path and clean up on error — a SIGKILL between `Close()` and the next `Open()` is unrecoverable. The temp-file pattern survives any crash.
- The `setupMaterializeConsumer` fixture pre-registers the `northwind/Customer` index because `applyBatchEdits` fails closed when an index is missing ("index not found for object type ..."). Future tests that exercise `Consumer.ApplyBatch` directly need the same pre-registration; the NATS-driven path doesn't because the production consumer wires `EnsureIndex` at OMS bootstrap before any messages arrive. The shared `setupTestConsumer` helper in `funnel_test.go` registers `employee`; a per-feature fixture is the right shape when the test needs a different ObjectType — don't shoehorn into the shared fixture.
- The materializer is wired UNCONDITIONALLY whenever NATS is enabled (no env-var feature flag). The PRD describes a forward-looking US-410 "compaction + retention" story that will need a feature flag for cleanup cadence — but the write side has no opt-out today because every downstream story (US-406 rebuild, US-407 cold tier, US-408 index recovery) depends on the files existing. If a deploy genuinely cannot afford the extra disk I/O, set `WEAVE_DATA_DIR` to a tmpfs or NUL-mount path; the writer is best-effort and disk-full errors are logged, not propagated.
---

## 2026-05-04 - US-406 - Materialization 索引去重视图

**Implementation:**
- `pkg/materialize/snapshot.go` (new): `BuildSnapshot(ctx, ontologyApiName, objectType, asOf)` walks every `.parquet` file under `{rootDir}/{ontology}/{objectType}/`, dedupes rows by `PrimaryKey` keeping the maximum `__patch_offset`, drops the survivor when `__is_deleted=true`, and returns the resulting live set as `[]SnapshotRow`. The struct projects the per-edit JSON payload back into Go (`Properties map[string]interface{}`, `Markings []string`) so callers can feed straight into a Bleve document or any other downstream sink. Output is sorted by `PrimaryKey` for reproducible CLI / snapshot-compare flows.
- AsOf semantics: zero `time.Time` is the sentinel for "all time / latest". When `asOf` is non-zero the loop filters records whose `TimestampMs > asOf.UnixMilli()` BEFORE the offset comparison so the cutoff happens at write-time wall-clock. This is the right cut for time-travel / audit replays — a delete that landed AFTER the cutoff stays invisible and the row remains alive at that earlier instant.
- `pkg/materialize/snapshot.go` also exports the file-listing helper `listObjectTypeParquet(dir)` and JSON decode helpers (`decodePropertiesJSON`, `decodeMarkingsJSON`); both surface nil-on-missing-dir so a fresh deploy doesn't fail-closed.
- `cmd/weave-cli/cmd_materialize.go` (new): `weave materialize rebuild --ontology X --object-type Y [--data-dir DIR] [--as-of RFC3339] [--json]` is the operational surface. Defaults `--data-dir` to `$WEAVE_DATA_DIR/materialized` (or `./data/materialized`) so the CLI shares layout with cmd/server when both run on the same host. Default output is a tabwriter table; `--json` emits the full envelope including the materialized properties map.
- `cmd/weave-cli/main.go`: dispatcher for the new `materialize` command verb plus the usage line.
- `pkg/materialize/snapshot_test.go` (new — 11 unit tests): empty dir, blank ontology / object type guards, single CREATE round-trip, MODIFY chain dedup, DELETE drops the row, recreate-after-delete surfaces, asOf cutoff at three points (before / between / after), multi-PK interleaving, ObjectType isolation, offset-wins-over-filename-ordering for same-second batches.
- `cmd/weave-cli/cmd_materialize_test.go` (new — 9 CLI tests): missing flags, empty data dir success path, full snapshot output, JSON envelope, asOf flag round-trip at two cut points, RFC3339 rejection, unknown subcommand, no-subcommand usage.

**Files changed:**
- `pkg/materialize/snapshot.go` (new)
- `pkg/materialize/snapshot_test.go` (new — 11 tests)
- `cmd/weave-cli/cmd_materialize.go` (new)
- `cmd/weave-cli/cmd_materialize_test.go` (new — 9 tests)
- `cmd/weave-cli/main.go` (dispatcher + usage)
- `prd.json` (mark US-406 passes: true)

**Verification:**
- `go build ./...` clean
- `go vet ./...` clean
- `gofmt -l pkg/materialize cmd/weave-cli` clean (after one fmt pass on the test file)
- `go test ./pkg/materialize/... -v` PASS — 19 tests (8 pre-existing US-405 + 11 new US-406)
- `go test ./cmd/weave-cli/... -run Materialize -v` PASS — 9/9 new CLI tests
- `go test ./...` PASS across all packages — no regressions

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- The dedup invariant is "max __patch_offset wins per PK", NOT "latest filename wins". Two batches that land in the same wall-clock second produce two parquet files whose names (`{ts}_{offset}.parquet`) sort lexicographically by the embedded offset, but you should never depend on that — the offset column inside the row is the authoritative tie-breaker. The `TestBuildSnapshot_OffsetWinsOverFilenameOrdering` regression test pins this invariant with a same-timestamp double-write.
- AsOf takes `time.Time` (zero = latest), not a transaction id. The materialized parquet does NOT carry a tx_id column — `__patch_offset` is monotonic but resets on consumer restart, so cross-restart asOf MUST go through the wall-clock TimestampMs. If a future US needs tx-aware time-travel on the materialize side, add a `tx_id` column to `EditRecord` and either route asOf through `pkg/oms.TransactionResolver` (the same hook handler.go::resolveAsOf already uses) or expose a parallel `BuildSnapshotAtTx(txID)` helper. Don't try to map tx ids back to wall-clock instants inside BuildSnapshot — that conflates two concerns.
- Missing directory is intentionally NOT an error. A freshly-deployed system has no materialized files yet, and CLI users will run `rebuild` before any edits land. Returning `[]SnapshotRow(nil), nil` for `os.ErrNotExist` keeps the operator surface clean — exit code 0 + "rows=0" is the right signal. Only "directory exists but unreadable" propagates as an error.
- The CLI defaults `--data-dir` to `$WEAVE_DATA_DIR/materialized` (or `./data/materialized`) to mirror cmd/server's layout. When the materialize root moves (US-410 archive subdirectory), the helper `defaultMaterializeDir()` is the single chokepoint to update — don't sprinkle env-var reads across subcommands. The same env var is the right default for the upcoming US-408 `weave-cli index rebuild --from-parquet` and US-410 `weave-cli materialize compact`.
- `SnapshotRow.Properties` is `map[string]interface{}` for parity with the wire shape (`funnel.Edit.Properties`). DELETE rows produce empty `propertiesJSON` on disk so `decodePropertiesJSON("")` returns nil — this avoids a defensive `if r.IsDeleted` check at every call site that consumes the snapshot. Going forward, when adding new optional payload columns to `EditRecord`, follow the same "empty string means absent → nil" convention so the readers stay branch-light.
- TDD pattern reminder: writing the 11 unit tests + 9 CLI tests before implementation surfaced the asOf cutoff edge case (cutoff EXACTLY equal to a delete's timestamp must include the delete, since the comparison is `>` not `>=`). Catching this without an actual round-trip would have been hard — the tests pinned the boundary in the contract before the implementation could drift.
- For tabwriter output that includes JSON-encoded property values, `json.Marshal` is the safe escape; key order is not deterministic in Go's standard map encoder, but that's acceptable for a CLI summary surface where the JSON column is informational. If a future caller needs reproducible output for diff tooling, switch to a sorted-keys encoder helper rather than baking a sort into BuildSnapshot itself.

## 2026-05-04 - US-407 - 冷热双层查询路径

**Implementation:**
- `pkg/oss/objectset/tier.go` (new): `TierRouter` interface (`ColdPrimaryKeys(ctx, ontology, objectType, before time.Time)`), `Executor.SetTierRouter` / `Executor.SetHotWindow` / `Executor.SetTierNowFunc` setters, `DefaultHotWindow = 24h` constant, and the `mergeHotColdPKs` order-preserving hot-wins dedup helper. The interface is intentionally narrow (one method) so any cold-tier source — Parquet, S3, an external compaction service — can satisfy it without dragging in storage-specific shapes.
- `pkg/oss/objectset/executor.go`: extended `Executor` with three optional fields (`tierRouter TierRouter`, `hotWindow time.Duration`, `tierNowFn func() time.Time`). `executeBase` now consults the router after the Bleve query: cutoff is `now - effectiveHotWindow()`, cold-only PKs are merged after the hot result, and a router error propagates as a `cold tier base objectSet %q` wrapped error so the wire layer's `executeError` helper still classifies it (falls to the historical INVALID_ARGUMENT envelope by default — no new APIError code is needed because cold-tier failures are operator-grade, not user-grade).
- `pkg/materialize/tier.go` (new): `*TierRouter` adapter implementing the `objectset.TierRouter` shape via Go's structural typing — no import on `pkg/oss/objectset`. Wraps an existing `*Materializer` so the writer (US-405) and the cold-tier reader share the same parquet root with zero extra plumbing. Nil materializer / blank ontology guards keep degraded-mode boots clean.
- `internal/config/config.go`: new `ColdTierConfig{HotWindow time.Duration}` block on `Config`, default 24h, env knob `WEAVE_HOT_WINDOW_HOURS=N` (integer hours; rejects non-numeric / negative values, accepts `0` to disable cold reads while keeping the writer active).
- `cmd/server/main.go`: extracted `mat := materialize.NewMaterializer(matRoot)` so the same instance feeds both `FunnelConsumer.SetEditMaterializer` (writer) and `ObjSetExecutor.SetTierRouter(materialize.NewTierRouter(mat))` (reader). Wiring is gated on `cfg.ColdTier.HotWindow > 0` AND `deps.ObjSetExecutor != nil` so degraded-mode boots leave the executor on the legacy hot-only path.

**Files changed:**
- `pkg/oss/objectset/tier.go` (new)
- `pkg/oss/objectset/tier_us407_test.go` (new — 11 tests + 1 benchmark covering: no-router fallback, cold adds rows + cutoff plumbing, dedup hot-wins, hot-empty/cold-fills, error propagation, default 24h hot window, nil-detach, per-objectType routing, 100K cold-tier <500ms latency gate, Materialize end-to-end integration via `*materialize.Materializer`, and the sorted helper sanity check)

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- `TierRouter` lives in `pkg/oss/objectset` (the consumer) but the implementation `*materialize.TierRouter` lives in `pkg/materialize` and satisfies the interface via Go's structural typing — NO import from `pkg/materialize` to `pkg/oss/objectset`. This keeps the dependency direction one-way (`pkg/oss/objectset` → `pkg/materialize`) when wired but lets the materialize package stay independent of the OSS read path. When adding new cold-tier sources (S3 archive, remote cache, etc.) follow the same shape — define the implementation in its own package, register from `cmd/server/main.go`, and never import the consumer.
- `cfg.ColdTier.HotWindow == 0` is the canonical "disable cold reads" sentinel rather than a separate `Enabled bool` field. Reasoning: every other gating check in main.go reads `cfg.X.HotWindow > 0` so a single comparison says "is this active?" without dragging a redundant bool through env parsing + validation. The PRD AC explicitly mentions `WEAVE_HOT_WINDOW_HOURS=24`; `=0` falls out as a free-feature kill switch.
- `mergeHotColdPKs` is hot-wins-by-position, not hot-wins-by-content. The hot tier's ordering is preserved verbatim and cold-only PKs append in the cold-tier's emit order. This matters because cursor pagination at the handler layer (`pkg/oss/pagination`) snapshots the PK list under a stable key — if the merge reordered hot positions on every read, the cursor would skip or repeat rows. When extending to other dual-source merges (e.g. branch overlay + main, federated tenant reads), keep the hot/primary tier's ordering as the merge anchor.
- The `before` cutoff passed to `ColdPrimaryKeys` is `now - hotWindow`, NOT `now`. Future me will be tempted to pass `now` and let the cold tier handle the sliding window — don't. The cold tier doesn't know what window the executor is enforcing; making the cutoff a parameter keeps the policy at the call site and makes the cold tier reusable from non-executor surfaces (e.g. an admin "rebuild snapshot as of T" CLI) without re-deriving the window.
- The 100K-row latency gate runs as a regular `Test*` (not `Benchmark*`) so CI without `-bench` still enforces the PRD budget. Keep the cutoff at 500ms even though typical runs come in well under 50ms — the gate exists to catch a future regression where someone replaces the dedup map with a quadratic scan or wires a synchronous Parquet round-trip per PK. Five-minute jitter from a noisy CI runner is a feature not a bug at this margin.
- `gofmt -w cmd/server/main.go` rewrote pre-existing import grouping + struct field alignment that had drifted across earlier stories. This is a one-time hygiene tax — future story authors editing main.go should run `gofmt` on the file proactively rather than be surprised by a normalisation pass landing alongside their feature.
- The cold-tier router shares the Materializer instance with the writer (single `mat` variable wired into both `SetEditMaterializer` and `NewTierRouter`). When US-410 lands compaction / archive (which deletes parquet files) the writer and reader will need a shared lock OR a snapshot-time reader contract — the Materializer is currently lock-free because both surfaces are append/read-only against immutable atomic-rename files. Adding deletion changes that contract; plan to add a `Compact` method on `*Materializer` that holds a write lock the reader must respect.
- Wire the cold-tier even when NATS isn't enabled? Today no — the writer needs NATS, and without writes the reader returns empty results (no parquet files yet). When a future PITR / restore CLI lands (US-390 already exists) it will need to wire a read-only Materializer outside the NATS gate. Plan: extract the matRoot + Materializer construction up the file so the cold-tier reader can be wired even in degraded mode against pre-existing parquet files.

## 2026-05-04 - US-408 - 增量索引重建

**Implementation:**
- `pkg/index/manager.go`: extended `Manager` with a process-wide `rebuilding` set guarded by a dedicated `rebuildMu sync.RWMutex` (separate from the existing `mu` so a long-running Rebuild's batch commits don't block the executor's hot-path `IsRebuilding` probe). New methods `MarkRebuildStart(scopedKey)`, `MarkRebuildEnd(scopedKey)`, `IsRebuilding(scopedKey) bool` — empty key is a silent no-op so callers never need to guard.
- `pkg/index/rebuild.go`: introduced `RebuildOptions{Progress func(ProgressEvent), BatchSize int}` + `RebuildStage` enum (start / drop / recreate / estimate / batch / complete) + `ProgressEvent` envelope (Stage / ScopedKey / Current / Total). New `RebuildWithOptions(ctx, mgr, repo, src, req, opts)` is the canonical entry point; `Rebuild` is now a thin shim that calls `RebuildWithOptions` with zero options for backward compat. Lifecycle: `MarkRebuildStart` fires before drop, `defer mgr.MarkRebuildEnd(scopedKey)` ensures the marker is cleared even on panic / source error. Documents are committed in chunks of `opts.BatchSize` (default `DefaultRebuildBatchSize = 1000`) so `RebuildStageBatch` events deliver running totals to a CLI progress bar.
- `pkg/oss/objectset/executor.go::executeBase`: new fast-path branch — when `e.indexMgr.IsRebuilding(scopedKey)` is true, the executor skips the Bleve search entirely and routes through the new helper `executeBaseDuringRebuild`. The helper queries the cold tier with cutoff = `e.effectiveNow()` (NOT `now - hotWindow`) so the caller sees the FULL materialised set instead of just the post-window slice. When no `tierRouter` is wired the helper returns an empty result rather than the historical "index not found" 5xx — operators who triggered the rebuild already accepted the read-availability trade-off.
- `cmd/weave-cli/cmd_index.go` (new): top-level `weave index rebuild --ontology X --object-type Y` command. Default flow: query `/objects/{type}/count` for a pre-rebuild estimate, render `Rebuilding ... (estimated N rows)...`, POST to `/api/admin/indexes/rebuild`, then print a summary line with elapsed ms + indexed count + pre/post estimate delta (+N new / N removed / matches). New flags: `--no-estimate` (skip the count probe — required when /count is degraded), `--json` (raw response envelope, suppresses human progress lines for downstream tooling).
- `cmd/weave-cli/main.go`: dispatcher entry for the new `index` verb plus a usage-line addition. The existing `weave admin index rebuild` subcommand stays in place — same HTTP target, no progress / estimate UI — so existing scripts keep working byte-for-byte.

**Files changed:**
- `pkg/index/manager.go` (new rebuild marker + MarkRebuildStart/End/IsRebuilding methods + per-key map)
- `pkg/index/rebuild.go` (RebuildOptions, RebuildStage, ProgressEvent, DefaultRebuildBatchSize, RebuildWithOptions; Rebuild becomes a shim)
- `pkg/index/rebuild_us408_test.go` (new — 9 tests: marker lifecycle / multi-key independence / concurrent-safe / all-stage emission / batch-size respect / default-batch-size / marker-set-during-rebuild / marker-cleared-on-error / nil-source emits estimate+complete)
- `pkg/oss/objectset/executor.go` (executeBase fast-path branch + executeBaseDuringRebuild helper)
- `pkg/oss/objectset/tier_us408_test.go` (new — 4 tests: cold-tier with now cutoff / no-tier-router degrades to empty / restores hot path after rebuild ends / cold error propagates)
- `cmd/weave-cli/cmd_index.go` (new — runIndex / runIndexRebuild / executeIndexRebuild / printRebuildSummary / formatDuration)

- `prd.json` (mark US-408 passes: true)

**Verification:**
- `go build ./...` clean
- `go vet ./...` clean
- `go test -race ./pkg/index/... ./pkg/oss/objectset/... ./cmd/weave-cli/... ./cmd/server/...` PASS

  - `TestRebuildWithOptions_NilSourceEmitsZeroEstimate` (estimate stage fires even when there's nothing to index)
- 4/4 new executor fallback tests PASS:
  - `TestExecuteBase_DuringRebuild_RoutesToColdTierWithNowCutoff` (cold cutoff = now, NOT now-hotWindow)
  - `TestExecuteBase_DuringRebuild_NoTierRouterDegradesToEmpty` (no 5xx in degraded mode)
  - `TestExecuteBase_AfterRebuildEnds_RestoresHotPath` (marker is load-bearing — clearing it restores legacy semantics)
  - `TestExecuteBase_DuringRebuild_ColdErrorPropagates` (errors not swallowed)
- 8/8 new CLI tests PASS:
  - `TestIndexRebuildCLI_PrintsEstimateAndSummary` (full happy path: estimate + summary + delta)
  - `TestIndexRebuildCLI_NoEstimateFlagSkipsCount` (--no-estimate respected)
  - `TestIndexRebuildCLI_JSONFlagSkipsHumanOutput` (--json clean envelope)
  - `TestIndexRebuildCLI_EstimateFailureFallsBackToUnavailable` (degraded /count doesn't abort rebuild)
  - `TestIndexRebuildCLI_DeltaReporting` (+N new line)
  - `TestIndexRebuildCLI_MissingFlags` / `..._UnknownSubcommand` / `..._NoArgs` (usage paths)

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- The rebuild marker uses a SEPARATE `rebuildMu sync.RWMutex` rather than reusing the manager's existing `mu`. Reusing `mu` would block every executor read on the global write lock the manager already holds during `EnsureIndex` / `DropIndex`, defeating the purpose of the cold-tier fallback. The split also keeps the marker's RLock cheap on the executor's hot path: every base read in normal operation pays one RLock acquire + one map lookup + one RUnlock, which is sub-microsecond and uncontested when no rebuild is active.
- Cold-tier cutoff during rebuild is `e.effectiveNow()`, NOT `now - hotWindow`. Future me will be tempted to share the cutoff helper with the steady-state path — don't. The whole point of the rebuild fallback is to expose the FULL materialised set; carrying the hot-window subtraction would silently hide rows from anyone reading mid-rebuild on a system where most data is fresh. The two cutoffs are semantically distinct: one says "what does the cold tier own" (steady state), the other says "what does the index hold while it's being rebuilt" (rebuild fallback).
- `executeBaseDuringRebuild` does NOT apply the policy query because `executeBase`'s policy query is layered on the Bleve search (it's a Bleve `Query`). The cold tier returns raw PKs without a query language and policy filtering happens elsewhere in the wire path (`pkg/oss/service_impl.go` masking + row-level ACL at serialise time). This is the same contract `executeBase` honours when `tierRouter != nil` and only returns cold PKs (no policy re-application on the cold leg).
- `RebuildWithOptions` keeps `Rebuild` as a thin compatibility shim instead of deprecating it. The HTTP admin handler at `cmd/server/admin_index.go` still calls `Rebuild`, which transparently flips on the marker via the shim — so the marker is set during the synchronous HTTP rebuild path WITHOUT the handler having to opt in. This is the load-bearing reason "marker setup happens inside the rebuild engine, not at every call site" — any future caller that adds a new rebuild surface (offline CLI, auto-recovery hook, etc.) gets the executor fallback for free.
- The CLI's "progress + estimate" UX is a two-stage render rather than a streaming progress bar because the underlying HTTP endpoint is synchronous (no SSE / chunked response). Stage 1: count probe → "estimated N rows". Stage 2: post-rebuild summary → "Indexed M rows in T". For very long rebuilds (10M+ rows) a future US could swap to SSE or polling, but the synchronous render covers the typical operator-grade ObjectType sizes (sub-millisecond on Northwind 91 customers, ~200ms on Chinook 59k tracks). Don't add streaming complexity until an operator actually files a "I want to see progress mid-rebuild" ask.
- `RebuildStage` is a string enum (not an iota int) because the wire format crosses a logging / telemetry boundary — string stages are self-documenting in JSON dumps and let downstream dashboards group on stage without a lookup table. The cost is one allocation per event for the string literal, which is irrelevant at six events per rebuild.
- `cmd/weave-cli/cmd_index.go` exposes `executeIndexRebuild(c *cliclient.Client, ...)` as the testable seam — the CLI dispatcher (`runIndexRebuild`) handles flag parsing + config bootstrapping, then delegates. This pattern keeps the network boundary stubbable with `httptest.NewServer` without mocking the entire `cliclient.Client` interface, and matches the shape `cmd_admin.go` would benefit from if it ever needs more elaborate UI logic.
- The `weave index rebuild` and `weave admin index rebuild` commands are intentionally redundant: the former is the operator-facing convenience surface (UX-tuned, with progress + estimate), the latter is the no-frills primitive that scripts and CI hook into. Don't try to consolidate them — the right model is "verb hierarchy mirrors the operator's mental model", and operators don't think of index rebuild as an admin-only operation in 2026 (it's the standard recovery action after a Bleve index file gets corrupted).
- Idempotency: the marker is a SET (not a counter), so two concurrent `RebuildWithOptions` calls on the same scopedKey correctly keep the marker live for the union of their critical sections — but they will race on Bleve's drop+ensure operations. The current code does NOT serialize concurrent rebuilds on the same key; that's left to the caller (admin endpoint serialises naturally because chi enforces per-handler invocation). If a future surface allows parallel rebuilds, add an in-package mutex on the scopedKey.
---

## 2026-05-04 - US-409 - Materialization 监控指标

**Implementation:**
- `pkg/metrics/metrics.go`: added 3 collectors and registered them in `allCollectors()`:
  - `weave_materialize_lag_seconds` — HistogramVec(`ontology`, `object_type`) with custom buckets `[0.05, 0.1, 0.25, 0.5, 1, 2.5, 5, 10, 30, 60, 300, 600]` covering steady-state sub-second through 10-minute backlog
  - `weave_parquet_files_total` — CounterVec(`ontology`, `object_type`) incremented once per parquet file produced
  - `weave_parquet_size_bytes` — HistogramVec(`ontology`, `object_type`) with power-of-two byte buckets from 1 KiB to 256 MiB; histogram `_sum` is the cumulative-bytes accumulator that the Grafana Bps panel reads
- `pkg/metrics/materialize.go` (new): `MaterializeFileWritten(ontology, objectType, lag, sizeBytes)` is the single observation chokepoint. Negative lag is clamped to 0 (clock skew, pinned-timestamp tests pre-dating now); `sizeBytes <= 0` skips the size histogram observation but still increments the file counter (so a stat-failure path still surfaces "file produced" without polluting the size distribution).
- `pkg/metrics/materialize_testing.go` (new): exported `ParquetFilesTotalForTest`, `ParquetSizeBytesSumForTest`, `MaterializeLagCountForTest` accessors so external packages can assert integration-level metric increments without reimplementing the histogram-DTO walk. `histogramSum` / `histogramCount` extract `_sum` / `_count` via the `prometheus.Metric.Write(*dto.Metric)` contract — `prometheus/testutil.ToFloat64` only handles counters and gauges.
- `pkg/materialize/materialize.go`: extended the writeFile signature to `(int64, error)` returning the post-rename `os.Stat` size (stat failure swallows to 0 so the rest of the batch keeps progressing — file is on disk regardless of whether stat succeeded). After each successful file write, the per-objectType loop in `MaterializeBatch` calls `metrics.MaterializeFileWritten(batch.OntologyAPIName, ot, m.nowFn().Sub(ts), size)`. Lag is computed at observation time, NOT at file-write start time, so multi-objectType batches see slightly different per-file lag values reflecting the cumulative loop work.
- `grafana/dashboards/weave-materialize.json` (new): 6-panel operational dashboard with `datasource` / `ontology` / `object_type` template variables. Panels cover lag percentiles (p50/p95/p99), file-write throughput (per-OT rate), cumulative bytes Bps (rate of `_sum`), per-file size distribution (p50/p95), and lifetime stat-card totals for files + bytes. `weave_build_info` annotation overlays mark deploys identically to `weave-slo.json` so cross-dashboard navigation stays consistent.

**Files changed:**
- `pkg/metrics/metrics.go` (+3 collectors, +allCollectors entries, +help text)
- `pkg/metrics/materialize.go` (new — MaterializeFileWritten observation surface)
- `pkg/metrics/materialize_testing.go` (new — exported accessors + DTO walk helpers)
- `pkg/metrics/materialize_test.go` (new — 5 tests: registration, counter increment per-call, lag clamp non-negative, size-histogram cumulative, zero-size skip)
- `pkg/metrics/dashboard_us409_test.go` (new — dashboard JSON parses, references all 3 metric names, has expected uid)
- `pkg/materialize/materialize.go` (writeFile returns size, MaterializeBatch fires the metric)
- `pkg/materialize/metrics_us409_test.go` (new — end-to-end: real parquet round-trip increments all 3 metrics)
- `grafana/dashboards/weave-materialize.json` (new — 6-panel ops dashboard)
- `prd.json` (mark US-409 passes: true)

**Verification:**
- `go build ./...` clean
- `go vet ./...` clean
- `go test ./pkg/metrics/... ./pkg/materialize/... -v` PASS — 5 new metrics tests, 1 new dashboard JSON test, 1 new materialize integration test, plus all 27 pre-existing materialize tests
