**Learnings for future iterations:**
- For per-file metric observations, fire AT the natural completion boundary (after `os.Rename` returns), NOT before. The file isn't observable to readers until rename succeeds, so a metric that fires earlier would over-report on partial-write failure paths. The trade-off is that the `os.Stat` on the renamed path can fail for legitimate reasons (FUSE filesystems with unusual semantics) — swallow that to 0 and let the file counter still increment, because "file landed on disk" is the load-bearing signal and "file is N bytes" is the cosmetic-but-useful signal.
- `_sum` on a Prometheus histogram IS a cumulative counter for free. The Grafana panel "Bps" computes `rate(weave_parquet_size_bytes_sum[5m])` and gets a per-second byte throughput without needing a separate counter. This is the right shape for any per-event "size of thing N" telemetry — don't add a parallel `*_bytes_total` counter, just observe the size into the histogram and let downstream queries pull `_sum` as the cumulative accumulator. Two corollaries: (a) the histogram `_count` is redundant with any sibling `*_total` files counter (we kept both because a future rename of the histogram should not require a dashboard-side migration of the file-count panel), (b) if you ever delete the histogram, deal with the `_sum` consumers BEFORE the deletion — Grafana will silently render zero where it used to render bytes/sec.
- The `MaterializeFileWritten` helper takes `time.Duration` for lag rather than `time.Time` start/end so callers can compose lag from any clock source — wall clock for the steady-state path, `nowFn()` for tests with pinned timestamps, an upstream NATS-stamped publish time for a future "publish-to-disk" lag flavour. Pushing the math to the call site keeps the helper unit-test-clean and avoids a "which clock are we comparing against" footgun in the metric's HELP text.
- Custom histogram buckets need explicit thought: `prometheus.DefBuckets` (5ms..10s exponential) is wrong for materialization lag because steady state is seconds and SLO breaches are minutes. The 12-bucket curve `[0.05..600]` covers both regimes with reasonable per-decade resolution. For byte sizes, power-of-two buckets `[1 KiB..256 MiB]` are the natural shape because parquet file sizes scale with EditBatch fan-out and operators reason in dyadic terms (KiB / MiB / GiB). Don't reach for `prometheus.LinearBuckets` here — log spacing is the right shape for both.
- `prometheus/testutil.ToFloat64` only handles counters and gauges. To extract `_sum` / `_count` from a histogram in test code, walk the DTO via `prometheus.Metric.Write(*dto.Metric)` (the `pb.Histogram.GetSampleSum()` / `GetSampleCount()` getters). Wrap this in package-local helpers (`histogramSum` / `histogramCount`) so call sites stay one-line. Pattern: when adding a new non-counter/non-gauge metric type that needs test-time aggregate access, add the DTO walker to the same package as a zero-cost helper rather than re-implementing it per consumer.
- The dashboard JSON is checked into the repo at `grafana/dashboards/weave-materialize.json` and validated only by a JSON-parse + metric-name-substring sanity test — full PromQL or grafana-schema validation would require either a Grafana process or a heavy schema-ingestion library. The current "parses + references the right metrics" guard is sufficient to catch the most common breakages (typo'd metric name, accidental partial-rewrite). If a future US needs deeper validation, the right approach is to spin up grafana-render or grafonnet during CI, not to invent a Go-side schema replica.
- Wiring point note: the metric calls live INSIDE `MaterializeBatch` rather than in the funnel `Consumer.runMaterialize` dispatcher. Reasoning: the funnel hook has a single `MaterializeBatch(ctx, batch) error` interface and the per-objectType + per-file fan-out happens entirely inside the materializer — if metrics were emitted at the dispatcher level they would have batch-level granularity (wrong) instead of per-file granularity (right). When adding observability to other plugin-style hooks, fire metrics from the IMPLEMENTATION not the DISPATCHER for the same reason.
- The `ForTest` accessor naming (`ParquetFilesTotalForTest`, etc.) is intentional public-but-test-only nomenclature. Naming them with the suffix signals to future readers that the entry point is for assertion plumbing only — don't use them in production code paths or wire-format response shapes. This is the same convention as `prometheus.NewRegistry` (test-only) vs `prometheus.DefaultRegisterer` (production).
---

## 2026-05-04 - US-410 - Parquet 压缩与清理策略

**Implementation:**
- New `pkg/materialize/retention.go` houses `Retainer` + `RetentionConfig` + `RunOnce` / `RunLoop`. `RunOnce` walks every `{rootDir}/{ontology}/{objectType}/` leaf and runs three phases in order: compact → archive → delete. Phases are independent (no shared mutable state), so a partial filesystem failure in one phase doesn't poison the others.
- Compaction: groups parquet files by their UTC date prefix (extracted from filename via the new `parseFileTimestamp` helper). Strict-before-today filter so today's still-being-written files are never touched. For each date group with ≥2 files, reads every row, sorts by `__patch_offset` ascending, writes one merged file `YYYYMMDDT000000_compact_NNNNNNNNNNNNNNNNNNNN.parquet` via tmp+rename, then removes the originals (skip-if-equal-target guard for the rare same-day re-compaction case).
- Archive: files older than `ArchiveAfter` (default 7d) move into a per-ObjectType `archive/` subdirectory via `os.Rename`. Because `pkg/materialize/snapshot.go::listObjectTypeParquet` skips subdirectories, archived files become invisible to `BuildSnapshot` AND `TierRouter.ColdPrimaryKeys` immediately — that IS the operational semantic.
- Hard delete: sweeps both the active dir and `archive/` for files older than `DeleteAfter` (default 30d derived from `WEAVE_PARQUET_RETENTION_DAYS`). `DeleteAfter <= 0` disables the sweep entirely so operators with regulatory retention can keep files forever.
- New env knob `WEAVE_PARQUET_RETENTION_DAYS` (default 30). Negative or non-numeric values fail loudly during `config.Load()`. Compact interval (24h) and archive threshold (7d) are PRD-fixed but exposed on `RetentionConfig` for future env promotion without breaking compat.
- `cmd/server/main.go` spawns a `RunLoop` goroutine right after `NewMaterializer`, using `ctx` for cancellation and a log-only error sink — failures never abort the server.

**Files changed:**
- `pkg/materialize/retention.go` (new — `Retainer`, `RetentionConfig`, `RetentionStats`, `RunOnce`, `RunLoop`, `parseFileTimestamp`)
- `pkg/materialize/retention_test.go` (new — 12 tests covering compact / archive / delete / context / RunLoop / parser)
- `internal/config/config.go` (`ParquetRetentionConfig` field, defaults, `WEAVE_PARQUET_RETENTION_DAYS` parsing)
- `internal/config/config_test.go` (5 new tests: defaults / override / zero / negative / non-numeric)
- `cmd/server/main.go` (wire `Retainer.RunLoop` after `NewMaterializer`)
- `prd.json` (US-410 passes: true)

**Verification:**
- `go build ./...` clean
- `go vet ./...` clean
- `go test ./pkg/materialize/... ./internal/config/...` PASS — 17 new tests in materialize (12 retention + 5 pre-existing) + 5 new config tests, all pre-existing tests unchanged
- `go test ./...` PASS across all 80+ packages including e2e / northwind / chinook / foundry_parity, no regressions
- `gofmt -l` clean

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- `parseFileTimestamp` parses the underscore-prefixed YYYYMMDDTHHMMSS produced by the Materializer's `writeFile`. It also accepts the compaction output `YYYYMMDDT000000_compact_NNNNNNNNNNNNNNNNNNNN.parquet` because the first underscore still terminates a valid time prefix. Files that don't match (e.g. operator-dropped backups) are silently skipped — the retainer is defensive, not greedy. When extending the filename layout in the future, keep the YYYYMMDDTHHMMSS prefix invariant or update this helper in lockstep.
- Compaction is grouped by DATE not by hour. The reasoning: the writer can produce many small files per day (one per EditBatch), but operators want at most one compacted file per calendar day per ObjectType. Hourly compaction would produce 24 files/day per ObjectType which defeats the purpose. The natural unit is the day boundary because (a) it aligns with operator mental models, (b) it bounds the read amplification of `BuildSnapshot` to N_DAYS files instead of N_BATCHES files.
- The "skip today" rule is load-bearing: the writer atomically renames `tmp` → final, so a file present in the directory IS complete, but new files keep arriving throughout the day. If we compacted today's files, the next batch would land alongside an in-progress merged file and the next compaction sweep would have to merge them all again. Strict-before-today bounds the compaction work per file to "at most once".
- The compaction output's `_compact_` infix is purely informative — readers (snapshot rebuild, tier router, future audit replay) dedupe by `__patch_offset` and don't care about names. So the merge is safe to run multiple times against the same date group: each run produces a new file, deletes the old originals (including the previous compact), and the dedup invariant survives because the offsets are identical. This is why `mergeGroup`'s "skip-if-equal-target" guard is the only special case needed — a future re-compact-of-compact path Just Works.
- Archive moves are observable to readers IMMEDIATELY because `listObjectTypeParquet` skips subdirectories. This is the right OPS semantic but it means the archive sweep is more impactful than the name suggests — moving a file IS a "hide from queries" action. If a future US needs a "dim but still queryable" tier, that would need a separate flag column or directory naming scheme rather than reusing `archive/`.
- The `Retainer` is intentionally STATELESS. There's no in-memory record of "files I've already considered". Every `RunOnce` rebuilds from filesystem state. The trade-off: rescanning the directory tree every tick is wasteful at scale, but the tick is daily, the directory layout is partitioned by ObjectType, and the alternative (a persisted state file) introduces a consistency boundary that could disagree with the filesystem. Stateless wins until proven otherwise.
- The `time.Duration(int) * 24 * time.Hour` conversion for `RetentionDays` lives in `cmd/server/main.go` rather than in `config.go` because the retention package's API takes a Duration. Keeping the unit conversion at the wiring layer (not deep in config) means a future US that wants to introduce `WEAVE_PARQUET_RETENTION_HOURS` (e.g. for tests) can wire it without changing the package interface.
- `gofmt` aggressively rewrites struct field comment alignment when fields are added — when extending a config struct, expect `gofmt -w` to be required. The CI typecheck step runs `gofmt -l`, so the PR will fail without the rewrite.
---

## 2026-05-04 - US-411 - Ontology 包导出格式（.weavepkg）

**Implementation:**
- New `pkg/weavepkg` package: pure-Go .weavepkg builder with no `pkg/oms` dependency. `Manifest` struct (name / version / author / license / description / minWeaveVersion / dependencies / generatedAt / contents) marshals to manifest.json. `BuildInput` aggregates the four content axes (Ontology blob, Actions, Functions, Migrations) the AC enumerates; `Build(io.Writer, BuildInput)` is the single chokepoint that produces the deflate-compressed ZIP. File-name constants (`ManifestFilename` / `OntologyFilename` / `ActionsDir` / `FunctionsDir` / `MigrationsDir`) live on the package so future readers (US-412 install, US-413 marketplace UI, US-454 marketplace browse) reference the layout symbolically rather than re-typing string literals.
- Layout invariants the builder enforces: (a) Manifest.Name and Manifest.Version are required; empty strings reject before any byte hits disk. (b) Ontology body is required (it carries the entire `OntologyExport` envelope from `pkg/oms`). (c) Action APIName uniqueness is checked up front — duplicate names map to a single zip entry path so we fail loudly instead of silently last-write-wins. (d) Migration filenames must be plain basenames; `path.Clean(name) != name` OR any `..` substring rejects, so `weave pkg install` (US-412) extracting into a target dir can never escape it. (e) Function name collisions disambiguate by `<name>@<version>.js` so multi-version coexistence (US-217) survives an export round-trip.
- Manifest stamps `GeneratedAt = time.Now().UTC()` when zero, so callers don't have to thread a clock; tests pin it for byte-stable assertions. `Manifest.Contents` (Ontology / Actions / Functions / Migrations file lists, sorted lexically) is populated by Build itself — callers never set it.
- New CLI verb `weave pkg export --ontology X -o X.weavepkg [...]` in `cmd/weave-cli/cmd_pkg.go`. Flags cover every manifest field plus `--migrations-dir` (defaults to `./migrations`, missing-dir tolerated for non-repo working dirs) and `--dependencies` (comma-separated `name@version` pairs, fails 2 on bad parse). Output uses tmp+rename so a partial write is never observable. Stdout summary line: `Wrote <path> (N actions, M functions, K migrations)`.
- New `cliclient.Client.ExportOntology(ctx, ontology)` calls the existing `GET /api/v2/ontologies/{name}/export` endpoint that returns the full `OntologyExport` envelope (ontology + objectTypes + linkTypes + actionTypes + interfaces + sharedProperties + valueTypes + typeGroups + functions + queryTypes). The CLI marshals this map verbatim into `ontology.json` and slices `actionTypes` / `functions` into per-entry files via `extractActions` / `extractFunctions`. Both extractors sort their output for deterministic ordering inside the archive.
- `loadMigrations(dir)` reads every `*.sql` under dir non-recursively in lexical order. Non-`.sql` siblings (READMEs, .keep files) are silently skipped — the migrations dir mixes documentation and SQL today and a hard rejection would force operators to clean their dir before exporting.
- `cmd/weave-cli/main.go` dispatcher gains a `pkg` verb plus a usage line.

**Files changed:**
- `pkg/weavepkg/manifest.go` (new — Manifest / Dependency / Contents + filename constants + package doc)
- `pkg/weavepkg/builder.go` (new — BuildInput, ActionEntry, FunctionEntry, MigrationEntry, Build, validate, write helpers)
- `pkg/weavepkg/builder_test.go` (new — 11 unit tests: empty-name reject, empty-version reject, empty-ontology reject, full manifest round-trip, one-file-per-action, one-file-per-function, function name collision via @version suffix, migrations layout, path-traversal reject, duplicate action reject, deterministic GeneratedAt)
- `cmd/weave-cli/cmd_pkg.go` (new — runPkg / runPkgExport / buildExportInput / extractActions / extractFunctions / loadMigrations / writePackageFile / parseDependencies)
- `cmd/weave-cli/cmd_pkg_test.go` (new — 9 CLI tests: missing --ontology / missing -o / unknown subcommand / no subcommand usage / valid weavepkg full round-trip with stub server + filesystem migrations / empty migrations dir / bad dependency spec / server error propagation / pkg name defaults to ontology)
- `cmd/weave-cli/main.go` (dispatcher entry for `pkg` + usage line)
- `internal/cliclient/client.go` (new `ExportOntology(ctx, ontology) (map[string]any, error)` method calling `GET /api/v2/ontologies/{name}/export`)
- `prd.json` (mark US-411 passes: true)

**Verification:**
- `go build ./...` clean
- `go vet ./...` clean
- `gofmt -l pkg/weavepkg cmd/weave-cli internal/cliclient` clean
- `go test ./pkg/weavepkg/... -v` PASS — 11/11 tests
- `go test ./cmd/weave-cli/... -v -run Pkg` PASS — 9/9 new CLI tests
- `go test ./...` PASS across all 80+ packages, no regressions

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- The existing `GET /api/v2/ontologies/{name}/export` endpoint already returns the full `OntologyExport` envelope (every entity collection — including Functions with their JS source), so the CLI doesn't need a fan-out across multiple HTTP calls. When future package-related stories (US-412 install, US-414 example packages, US-454 marketplace) need server-side ontology data, reach for this single endpoint rather than chasing individual list APIs.
- The package builder is intentionally split from the CLI fetcher: `pkg/weavepkg.Build` takes pre-fetched data and writes a ZIP; the CLI layer does HTTP + filesystem reads then calls Build. This split keeps the builder unit-testable without an HTTP harness AND makes US-412 (`weave pkg install`) trivially symmetric — install will be a `pkg/weavepkg.Read(io.Reader) (BuildInput, error)` that reverses the write path, then the CLI does the HTTP POSTs to apply edits. Don't be tempted to fold the HTTP boundary into the builder package.
- Path-traversal protection is THE security-relevant invariant in package builders. `validateMigrationFilename` rejects anything that isn't a plain basename — `..`, embedded slashes, absolute paths, or `path.Clean` mutations. The installer (US-412) MUST mirror this on the read side or a hostile package can write arbitrary files via `tar`-style escape. Keep the validator centralised in the builder so the read path can call the same helper rather than re-deriving the rules.
- Function name + version collision is a real concern in Weave because US-217 made multi-version coexistence first-class. The builder's `<name>@<version>.js` fallback stays inside the same `functions/` directory so naive globs (`functions/*.js`) still find both versions; the installer needs to parse the `@version` suffix when present and round-trip the version field on the wire. When extending to other multi-version entities (saga steps with versions, automation rules with revisions) follow the same suffix-on-collision-only pattern instead of always emitting `<name>@<version>` filenames.
- `path.Clean` rejects different shapes than you'd expect: `"./foo.sql"` is cleaned to `"foo.sql"` (different from input → reject), but `"foo.sql"` round-trips identically (accept). The combination of `clean != name`, `ContainsAny(name, "/\\")`, and `Contains(name, "..")` covers every common path-traversal payload AND tolerates plain basenames; weakening any one check creates a hole. The test `TestBuild_RejectsUnsafeMigrationFilenames` pins this contract.
- The CLI's `--migrations-dir` flag defaults to `./migrations` (matching the canonical repo layout) but tolerates a missing directory silently. Reasoning: the CLI is often run from `~/Downloads` or a fresh checkout where the migrations dir simply doesn't exist; failing hard would make the no-frills `weave pkg export --ontology X -o X.weavepkg` invocation fail for users who legitimately don't want to ship SQL. To opt out explicitly pass `--migrations-dir=""`. Future flag additions should follow this "missing path = silent skip, present-but-malformed = loud fail" shape.
- The cliclient's `do(ctx, ...)` translates 5xx into `*APIError` with `StatusCode` and `RawBody` populated. The CLI surface should wrap these with `fmt.Errorf("fetch X: %w", err)` so the user sees both the operation context AND the upstream error envelope. Don't reach for `errors.As` to special-case 404 / 500 unless the UX needs different exit codes — generic propagation is enough for a single-line operator-grade error message.
- `Manifest.Contents` is populated by the Build function itself (NOT by the caller) so callers can't drift the manifest's file inventory from the actual archive contents. This is the right pattern for any "what's in this archive?" sidecar — derive at write time, never trust the caller's claim. The same shape will help US-412 install: it can verify each path in `manifest.contents` is also a real zip entry as a defense-in-depth check on top of path-traversal rejection.
---

## 2026-05-04 - US-412 - Ontology 包导入与依赖解析

**Implementation:**
- `pkg/weavepkg/reader.go` (new): symmetric counterpart of `Build` — `Read(io.ReaderAt, size)` and `ReadBytes([]byte)` parse a .weavepkg archive into a `Package{Manifest, Ontology, Actions, Functions, Migrations}` struct. Mirrors every BuildInput axis. Defence-in-depth path traversal validation runs on the read side too: `validateMigrationFilename` is called from `readMigrationEntry` so a hostile archive bypassing the writer can't escape the migrations dir at install time. The duplicate-function disambiguation suffix (`<name>@<version>.js`) is reversed via `splitNameVersion` so versioned coexistence round-trips cleanly.
- `pkg/oms/installed_packages.go` (new): `InstalledPackage` model + narrow `InstalledPackageStore` interface (Upsert / Get / List / SetEnabled / Delete). Sentinel `ErrInstalledPackageNotFound` mirrors the `ErrLineageNotFound` pattern. Optional store — handler degrades cleanly when nil.
- `pkg/oms/handlers_pkg_install.go` (new): `POST /api/v2/pkg/install` handler. Validates manifest.name/version + ontology body, gates `minWeaveVersion` against the new package-level `WeaveServerVersion="0.99.0"`, decodes the ontology JSON envelope, runs `detectPackageConflicts` over every entity collection (objectType/linkType/actionType/queryType/interface/function/sharedProperty/typeGroup/valueType), routes the import via the shared `applyImportRequest` helper, runs migrations through the optional `PackageMigrationRunner`, and records the install in the optional `InstalledPackageStore`. `OnConflict` knob is `fail` (default — 409 with a sorted conflict list) / `overwrite` (replace-mode import) / `skip` (merge-mode import).
- `pkg/oms/handlers_pkg_list.go` (new): `GET /api/v2/pkg`, `GET /api/v2/pkg/{name}`, `POST /api/v2/pkg/{name}/enabled`, `DELETE /api/v2/pkg/{name}`. List endpoint returns empty data array when the store is unwired (NOT 503) so the marketplace UI in US-413/US-454 has a clean degraded path.
- `cmd/server/installed_packages_store.go` (new): `pgInstalledPackageStore` PG implementation using the `pgxpool.Pool`. UPSERT on `name`-key conflict.
- `cmd/server/package_migration_runner.go` (new): `pgPackageMigrationRunner` writes shipped SQL files atomically (tmp+rename) to `${DataDir}/installed_packages/{name}/migrations/` and runs `golang-migrate up` via the same pgx5 driver the core migration tree uses. PG DSN is optional — when empty it persists files to disk only (degraded mode, future operator-side run).
- `migrations/000097_installed_packages.{up,down}.sql` (new): `installed_packages` table with UNIQUE(name), JSONB manifest, TEXT[] migrations file list, indexes on ontology + enabled.
- `cmd/server/main.go`: extended `ServerDeps` with `InstalledPackageStore` + `PackageMigrationRunner`. Wires the PG store off `pgRepo`'s pool when PG is available; the migration runner is wired unconditionally.
- `cmd/server/routes.go`: registers the 5 new routes alongside `/api/v2/ontologies/import`.
- `cmd/weave-cli/cmd_pkg.go`: dispatcher gains `install` subcommand alongside `export`.
- `cmd/weave-cli/cmd_pkg_install.go` (new): `weave pkg install <archive.weavepkg> [--on-conflict=fail|overwrite|skip] [--json]`. Reads the archive via `weavepkg.ReadBytes`, POSTs to `/api/v2/pkg/install`, renders the conflict list inline when 409 fires, and surfaces `PackageMinWeaveVersionUnsatisfied` 400s with both `server` and `required` versions.
- `internal/cliclient/client.go`: `PostInstallPackage` + `ListInstalledPackages` thin wrappers.
- `api/openapi.yaml` + `cmd/server/openapi_spec.yaml`: 5 new operationIds documented for the contract test.

- `go test ./pkg/weavepkg/... -v` PASS — 9/9 reader tests + 11/11 builder tests
- `go test ./pkg/oms/... -run "PackageInstall|CheckMinWeaveVersion|CompareSemver"` PASS — 13 install handler tests + 2 version-comparison tests, no regressions in the existing 200+ oms tests
- `go test ./cmd/weave-cli/... -run Pkg -v` PASS — 7 install CLI tests + 9 export CLI tests
- `go test ./cmd/server/... -run "PGPackageMigrationRunner|ValidatePackageMigrationFilename"` PASS — 6 migration-runner tests
- `go test ./cmd/server/... -run Contract` PASS — both `TestContract_AllRoutesDocumented` and `TestContract_EmbeddedSpecMatchesCanonical` go green after the openapi.yaml additions
- `go test ./...` PASS across all 80+ packages — long suites (test/chinook 242s, test/northwind 165s, pkg/oss 96s, pkg/oss/objectset 67s) all green

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- `pkg/weavepkg.Read` is the symmetric inverse of `Build` and was foreshadowed in the US-411 learnings ("US-412 install will be trivially symmetric"). The `Package` struct mirrors `BuildInput` field-for-field so `Read → Build` would produce a byte-equivalent archive (modulo the `GeneratedAt` stamp). Defence-in-depth is the load-bearing security invariant: `validateMigrationFilename` runs on BOTH sides — `Build` rejects a path-traversing migration at writer time, `Read` rejects it again at parse time so a hostile archive that bypassed the writer (e.g. crafted by hand) can't surface a `..`-prefixed migration to the install handler. This dual gate is the right pattern for any future archive-style format that lands user-supplied filenames on disk.
- The existing `/api/v2/ontologies/import` endpoint (pre-US-412) was a natural template for the install path, but I chose to add a NEW endpoint rather than overload it. Reason: the import path takes a wire-shaped `OntologyExport` envelope plus a `mode: merge|replace` knob, while the install path needs to layer on (a) manifest validation (minWeaveVersion gate), (b) conflict pre-detection, (c) bundled migrations, (d) registry recording. Layering all of that into the existing handler would have widened the wire contract and made the merge-mode default impossible to keep stable. The shared internals (`applyImportRequest`, `detectPackageConflicts`) live as new methods on `OMSHandler`; the existing `ImportOntologyV2` handler is unchanged.
- `WeaveServerVersion` is a package-level `var` (NOT const) because tests need to flip it via `oms.WeaveServerVersion = "1.2.3"` to exercise the matching-version branch. Build-time `-ldflags '-X github.com/liyang/weave/pkg/oms.WeaveServerVersion=...'` overrides remain possible without touching the install path. Treat it as read-only at runtime — the install handler is the only consumer today; future read paths (a `/health` build-info field, telemetry labels) should call a getter shim if they need to mutate-protect the variable.
- Conflict detection runs even in `overwrite` and `skip` modes — the conflict list is computed unconditionally and the mode just decides whether to abort or to translate it to merge-mode behaviour. The reasoning: a future audit log could record "package X overwrote conflicting entries A/B/C" without needing a separate dry-run pass, and the cost of the per-entity GetByAPIName probe is bounded by `len(package.entities)` which is already small. If a future package contains 10K+ entities the right move is to add a batch-lookup repository method, not to gate the conflict scan.
- The migration runner is wired UNCONDITIONALLY (even in degraded mode without PG) so that `weave pkg install` against a no-PG server still leaves the SQL files on disk under `{DataDir}/installed_packages/{name}/migrations/`. The PG `migrate.New(...)` call is gated on `cfg.PGDSN != ""` — when absent, the function returns the file count without applying. This "always persist, sometimes apply" split is the right shape for any future side-effect hook that has a "best effort I/O" component plus a "completes the contract" component: separate them, log when the second skips, never let the first fail because of the second.
- For optional stores that are NOT on `oms.Repository` (this pattern was established by `LineageStore` / `ColumnLineageStore`), the right shape is: define the interface in `pkg/oms`, add `Set...Store` setters on the handler with nil-as-absent semantics, build the concrete PG impl in `cmd/server/` (so `pkg/oms` doesn't import pgx), and surface degraded-mode behaviour as either an empty list (read paths) or a successful no-op (write paths) rather than 503. The marketplace UI test routers can boot without the durable store this way.
- The `CONFLICT 409` envelope's `parameters` bag is a flat `map[string]string` per `apierror.NewConflict`'s shape, but the conflict list is intrinsically a list-of-objects. Workaround: serialise the conflict list as a JSON string under `parameters.conflicts` and let the CLI re-parse it for rendering. This is ugly but stable; the cleaner long-term move would be a typed conflict envelope on `apierror`, but that would touch every existing conflict consumer.
- The CLI's migration-content wire format relies on Go's default `encoding/json` `[]byte` → base64 string handling: the test stub asserts the migrations array shape but not the byte-level encoding. The server handler's `PackageMigrationEntry.Content []byte` field decodes base64 transparently. For non-text SQL bodies (rare but possible — e.g. embedded UTF-16 procedures) this round-trips cleanly without a separate encoding step. If a future US needs to ship binary blobs on the same surface, the existing `[]byte` field already covers it.
- Stdlib `flag` parsing stops at the first non-flag argument — so `weave pkg install archive.weavepkg --on-conflict overwrite` parses with `--on-conflict overwrite` as POSITIONAL args, not flags. CLI tests must put flags BEFORE the archive path: `weave pkg install --on-conflict overwrite archive.weavepkg`. This caught us once and is the same gotcha that hit US-408's index-rebuild CLI. When designing CLI surfaces with multiple positional-after-flag arguments, prefer `--key=value` over `--key value` to make the ordering ambiguity disappear.
---

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- The US-412 iteration already shipped the FULL backend surface for US-413: handler list/get/enable/delete in `pkg/oms/handlers_pkg_list.go`, store interface + PG impl, routes mounted in `cmd/server/routes.go`. US-413 was strictly a frontend story — when an earlier story leaves a "wired but unused" wire surface, audit it FIRST before sketching anything new. The handler comment block (`// The marketplace UI (US-413, US-454) lists rows from this table…`) is the canonical signal.
- The DELETE handler's docstring is explicit: it removes ONLY the registry row. On-disk migrations + ontology entities stay put. The Uninstall confirmation dialog must surface this trade-off to the user (the copy in `UninstallConfirmDialog` mirrors the handler comment verbatim) so they don't think a click drops tables. Future "dangerous delete" UIs in this codebase should follow the same shape — confirmation modal text explicitly mirrors the backend's destructive-vs-soft contract rather than generic "are you sure" copy.
- `useToastStore.push` is the right surface for mutation feedback (success + error) — it's already mounted globally in `<Shell>`. New mutation pages should NOT roll their own banner state. For test isolation, call `useToastStore.getState().clear()` in the test's `beforeEach` so toasts from prior tests don't leak across the run.
- React Query's mutation `variables` field is exposed at top level (e.g. `enableMutation.variables?.name === pkg.name`) and is the cleanest way to scope a "pending" indicator to ONE row in a list — preferable to per-row `useMutation` hooks (which would violate the rules of hooks if the list is dynamic). Pattern: pass the row identity into `mutate({...})`, then read it back from `mutation.variables` to decorate exactly the row that's mid-flight.
- The `parameters` bag on `ApiRequestError` is `Record<string, string>` (per the apierror flat-string contract). For UI error surfaces that need richer rendering (e.g. the install-conflict list mentioned in US-412 learnings), prefer parsing the JSON-string parameter back into a typed shape at the API client layer (`packages.ts`) rather than threading raw `parameters` through the component.
- The Sidebar nav-list construction is a top-down `if (activeOntology) navItems.push(...)` ladder. The Marketplace entry is global (no ontology dependency), so it goes in the unconditional tail block alongside Settings / Mentions / Permission Requests. New global entries follow the same shape; new per-ontology entries should sit inside the `if (activeOntology)` block.
- The MSW DELETE handler must return `new HttpResponse(null, { status: 204 })` — `HttpResponse.json(...)` would set Content-Type even on a body-less response and the React Query mutation success branch swallows the response, but the `request<void>` parser at `web/src/api/client.ts` correctly handles empty body (`if (!text) return undefined as T`). Pattern: `void`-returning DELETE/PUT API clients pair cleanly with msw's `new HttpResponse(null, { status: 204 })`.

## 2026-05-04 - US-414 - 内置示例包 3 个

**Implementation:**
- New source-of-truth files for the three built-in example packages live under `examples/packages/{northwind,chinook,iot-demo}/` as `manifest.json` + `ontology.json` pairs. `embed.go` (package `examplepkgs`) declares `//go:embed all:northwind all:chinook all:iot-demo` so the FS ships in the server binary.
- `pkg/weavepkg/builtin.go` adds `LoadBuiltinPackages(fs.FS)` — first-level directories that contain `manifest.json` + `ontology.json` become `BuiltinPackage` rows; optional `actions/`, `functions/`, `migrations/` subdirectories follow the same shape as `BuildInput`. Returns sorted-by-slug results so catalog rendering is deterministic.
- `cmd/server/builtin_packages.go` wraps the loader with a concrete `oms.BuiltinPackageProvider` impl (`builtinPackageProvider`). It pre-builds the `[]BuiltinPackageMetadata` list (manifest fields + counts via `summariseOntology`) at construction time so `List()` is a slice copy, and `Get()` clones the ontology body so handler-side mutation cannot leak across requests.
- `pkg/oms/handlers_pkg_install.go` refactor: extracted `installPackage(ctx, *PackageInstallRequest) (*PackageInstallResponse, int, *apierror.APIError)` as the single chokepoint shared between the JSON-bodied `POST /api/v2/pkg/install` handler and the new built-in catalog one-click install path. The existing `PackageInstall` handler is now a thin JSON-decode + dispatch shell.
- `pkg/oms/handlers_pkg_builtin.go` new file: `BuiltinPackageProvider` interface (List + Get), `OMSHandler.SetBuiltinPackageProvider(p)` setter, and two HTTP handlers — `ListBuiltinPackages` (GET `/api/v2/pkg/builtin`) and `InstallBuiltinPackage` (POST `/api/v2/pkg/builtin/{slug}/install`). The install handler routes through `installPackage` so behaviour parity with the JSON-bodied endpoint is mechanical.
- `cmd/server/routes.go` mounts the two new routes BEFORE the parameterised `/api/v2/pkg/{name}` getter so the `builtin` literal segment doesn't get captured by the `{name}` matcher.
- `cmd/server/main.go` wires the embedded provider unconditionally — `newBuiltinPackageProvider()` reads the embedded FS at boot. A malformed embedded package is a build-time bug, so the loader logs and degrades to nil rather than panicking.
- `web/src/api/packages.ts`: added `BuiltinPackageMetadata` shape, `listBuiltinPackages`, `installBuiltinPackage(slug, onConflict?)`.
- `web/src/components/marketplace/MarketplacePage.tsx`: introduced an Installed / Built-in tab split. Built-in tab lists embedded packages with per-row Install buttons; the install mutation invalidates the installed-packages query and hops back to the Installed tab on success. Already-installed slugs render as a disabled "Installed" pill so users can't double-install.
- `api/openapi.yaml` + `cmd/server/openapi_spec.yaml`: added entries for both new routes.

**Verification:**
- `go build ./...` clean
- `go vet ./...` clean
- `go test ./pkg/weavepkg ./pkg/oms ./cmd/server ./examples/...` PASS
- New backend suites:
  - `pkg/weavepkg` — 5/5 (`TestLoadBuiltinPackages_*`)
  - `pkg/oms` — 8/8 (`TestListBuiltinPackages_*`, `TestInstallBuiltinPackage_*`)
  - `cmd/server` — 4/4 (`TestBuiltinPackageProvider_*`)
  - Existing PackageInstall suite still 16/16 PASS — confirms the refactor preserved 200 / 201 / 400 / 409 surface behaviour.
- `cmd/server/openapi_spec.yaml` is byte-identical to `api/openapi.yaml` (`TestContract_EmbeddedSpecMatchesCanonical` PASS); `TestContract_AllRoutesDocumented` PASS — both new routes documented.
- Frontend: `npm run test -- --run src/components/marketplace/` 12/12 PASS (7 pre-existing + 5 new).
- `npx tsc -b` clean for the new files; the only typecheck errors visible are the pre-existing baseline (`objectsets composer test fixture`, `objectSetUrl Buffer global`) confirmed in the US-413 progress entry.
- Browser verification: deferred. The vitest suite covers built-in tab rendering, one-click install, already-installed disabled state, install failure toast, and the empty-catalog empty state. Manual browser verification is a follow-up.

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- The single biggest insight from this story: when an existing HTTP handler does important business logic (validation, conflict detection, side effects), extracting an in-process helper (`installPackage`) is far cleaner than having a second handler do an internal HTTP roundtrip. The refactor was mechanically straightforward — replace `apierror.WriteJSON(w, ...)` with `return nil, 0, apierror.NewX(...)`, replace `httputil.WriteJSON(w, status, resp)` with `return &resp, status, nil`. Built-in install becomes a 5-line `Get-from-provider → installPackage → write response` flow. Pattern: when a feature needs to drive an existing endpoint from a different request shape (catalog click, automation rule, event handler), extract a `(ctx, *Request) (*Response, int, *apierror.APIError)` core and let both surfaces share it.
- `embed.FS` paths are relative to the source `.go` file, NOT the module root. That means the embed declaration MUST live in the same directory tree as the embedded files (`examples/packages/embed.go` declaring `//go:embed all:northwind all:chinook all:iot-demo`) — you can't put it in `pkg/oms/embed.go` and reference `../../examples/packages/...`. Future stories that ship binary catalogs (templates, fixtures, sample dashboards) should follow the same shape: drop an `embed.go` next to the data and consume it from cmd/server via a dedicated package.
- chi route ordering matters when a literal segment shares a prefix with a `{param}` getter. `/api/v2/pkg/builtin` had to be declared BEFORE `/api/v2/pkg/{name}` or chi would route to `GetInstalledPackage` with `name="builtin"`. The fix is purely registration order — chi matches in declaration order. Pattern: every time a new literal sub-path is added under an existing parameterised parent, audit the route block to ensure literal-before-param.
- For tests that verify "the built-in catalog is the right shape", a test-local `fstest.MapFS` is the cleanest way to exercise `newBuiltinPackageProviderFromFS` independently of the embedded FS. The embedded-FS test (`TestBuiltinPackageProvider_LoadsEmbeddedCatalog`) is the integration check that the committed source files actually parse; the MapFS test (`TestBuiltinPackageProvider_FromCustomFS`) is the unit check that the loader logic itself is correct under an arbitrary tree. Splitting these keeps a typo in `examples/packages/iot-demo/ontology.json` from masking a regression in the loader.
- The Marketplace UI's tab split surfaces a subtle UX dependency: the Installed query must invalidate AFTER a built-in install so the Installed tab shows the freshly imported row when the user lands there. The mutation's `onSuccess` fires both `queryClient.invalidateQueries({queryKey: PACKAGES_KEY})` AND `setTab('installed')`. If you only do one or the other, you get a confused user staring at either an empty Installed tab or a Built-in card that still says "Install". Pattern: any cross-query mutation in this codebase should invalidate every dependent key and (optionally) navigate to the surface where the result lands.
- The `summariseOntology` helper deliberately uses a loose `json.Unmarshal` into `map[string]any` slices instead of fully decoding into `oms.OntologyExport`. The catalog list endpoint only needs counts, and pulling in the strongly-typed shapes would require importing every related model just to count their occurrences. For thin projection helpers like this one, prefer `[]any` length over typed Slice unmarshalling — it's faster and decouples the projection from schema evolution.

## 2026-05-04 - US-415 - Function Code Repository（本地 Git）

**Implementation:**
- New `pkg/funcrepo` package owns the per-Function bare git repository under `{DataDir}/repos/{rid}/.git`. `Manager.Commit(ctx, rid, CommitInput)` writes via go-git plumbing — blob → single-entry tree (`function.js`) → commit chained onto previous head on `refs/heads/main`, with per-RID `sync.Mutex` so concurrent writers form a linear parent chain. `Manager.Log(ctx, rid, limit)` returns commits newest-first; missing repo → empty slice (no 404). `Manager.HeadCommit` returns the latest commit + source code blob and ErrNoCommits for empty repos. RID validator rejects `..`, path separators, and any input where `filepath.Clean(rid)!=rid`.
- Added the narrow `oms.FunctionRepoStore` interface in `pkg/oms/handlers_function_repo.go` plus `SetFunctionRepoStore` setter; pkg/oms stays free of go-git. Two new handlers `CreateFunctionRepoCommit` (POST /commits) + `ListFunctionRepoCommits` (GET /log) resolve `{functionRid}` via the existing `resolveFunctionRef` helper so the bare repo is always keyed on the canonical RID. Wire body accepts BOTH `sourceCode` and `patch` (alias) — single source path internally. 503 `FunctionRepoNotConfigured` when store is nil so degraded-mode test routers boot cleanly.
- `cmd/server/function_repo_adapter.go` bridges the concrete `*funcrepo.Manager` to `oms.FunctionRepoStore` — keeps the dependency direction one-way (cmd/server → pkg/funcrepo + cmd/server → pkg/oms; pkg/funcrepo and pkg/oms remain orthogonal).
- `cmd/server/main.go`: new `ServerDeps.FunctionRepoStore` field, wired unconditionally at bootstrap from `funcrepo.NewManager(filepath.Join(cfg.DataDir, "repos"))` and forwarded to the OMS handler.
- `cmd/server/routes.go`: registered the two new routes under the existing Function CRUD block.
- `api/openapi.yaml` + `cmd/server/openapi_spec.yaml`: added paths + `FunctionRepoCommit` schema. Contract test (`TestContract_AllRoutesDocumented` + `TestContract_EmbeddedSpecMatchesCanonical`) green.
- `internal/cliclient/client.go`: added `Function`, `FunctionRepoCommit` wire types + `GetFunction`, `CreateFunctionRepoCommit`, `ListFunctionRepoCommits` methods.
- `cmd/weave-cli/cmd_fn.go`: new `weave fn pull|push|log` subcommands. `pull` → fetches `sourceCode` from `GET /functions/{ref}` and writes to file or stdout. `push -f file -m message` → reads the file and POSTs as a commit. `log` → tabular list with hash/when/author/message; `--json` for raw output. `--ref` accepts rid / name / name@version per server-side resolution.
- Added `github.com/go-git/go-git/v5` v5.18.0 dependency to go.mod.

**Files changed:**
- `pkg/funcrepo/manager.go` (new — Manager + Commit + Log + HeadCommit + path validation)
- `pkg/funcrepo/manager_test.go` (new — 9 unit tests covering append, log limit, missing repo, sentinel errors, path-traversal, default identity, isolation, concurrent serialisation, fresh source)
- `pkg/oms/handlers_function_repo.go` (new — `FunctionRepoStore` interface + 2 handlers)
- `pkg/oms/handlers_function_repo_test.go` (new — 9 handler tests, `fakeFuncRepoStore` mock)
- `pkg/oms/handlers.go` (added `functionRepoStore` field on OMSHandler)
- `cmd/server/function_repo_adapter.go` (new — adapter)
- `cmd/server/main.go` (added `ServerDeps.FunctionRepoStore`, wiring + bootstrap construction, funcrepo import)
- `cmd/server/routes.go` (registered POST /commits + GET /log)
- `api/openapi.yaml` (new paths + FunctionRepoCommit schema)
- `cmd/server/openapi_spec.yaml` (mirror of api/openapi.yaml)
- `internal/cliclient/client.go` (Function/FunctionRepoCommit types + 3 methods)
- `cmd/weave-cli/cmd_fn.go` (new — fn subcommand dispatcher + pull/push/log)
- `cmd/weave-cli/cmd_fn_test.go` (new — 6 cli tests)
- `cmd/weave-cli/main.go` (registered `fn` command + usage line)
- `go.mod` / `go.sum` (added github.com/go-git/go-git/v5 + transitive deps)
- `prd.json` (mark US-415 passes: true)

**Verification:**
- `go build ./...` clean
- `go vet ./...` clean
- `go test ./...` all green — 9 funcrepo unit tests, 9 handler tests, 6 CLI tests, full cross-package suite passes including chinook + northwind end-to-end suites
- Contract tests `TestContract_AllRoutesDocumented` + `TestContract_EmbeddedSpecMatchesCanonical` pass

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- go-git plumbing (no worktree) is the right shape for "single-file repo per resource". `git.PlainInit(path, true)` for bare, then write blob + tree + commit + ref via `repo.Storer.SetEncodedObject` + `repo.Storer.SetReference`. Avoids the worktree → in-memory billy.Filesystem complications when each commit replaces a single file body.
- Per-RID `sync.Mutex` map (lazy alloc, never released — kept alive for the lifetime of the manager) is sufficient serialisation for "commits per resource". Concurrent writers form a linear parent chain instead of producing forks. The pattern fits any other "single hot-loop per identity" surface (per-Function async lock, per-Action retry serialisation, …).
- `resolveFunctionRef` (rid / name / name@version) was already the chokepoint for `GetFunctionV2` + `ExecuteFunction` + `ReplayFunction`. Reusing it for the new /commits + /log handlers means the URL stays consistent with the rest of the Function family — every endpoint accepts the same identifier shapes. Pattern: when adding a new sub-resource under an existing parameterised parent, find the parent's resolver and reuse it; don't write a parallel one.
- Path-traversal protection on user-supplied filesystem fragments is a recurring concern (US-411 weavepkg migrations, now US-415 Function RIDs). The validator pattern is the same — reject when `filepath.Clean(s)!=s` OR `s` contains `..` OR `s` contains `/`/`\`. Lift this into a reusable helper next time a third surface needs it (consider `pkg/secfs.ValidateLeafName`).
- The OpenAPI contract test (`TestContract_AllRoutesDocumented` + `TestContract_EmbeddedSpecMatchesCanonical`) is a hard fail when adding new routes. ALWAYS update both `api/openapi.yaml` AND `cmd/server/openapi_spec.yaml` in the same change. The `cp` from one to the other is the dumbest reliable form.
- For the wire shape of a "git commit" envelope, the minimal field set (hash, message, author, email, authorDate) round-trips cleanly across Go (`time.Time`), JSON (`format: date-time`), and shell (`HASH/WHEN/AUTHOR/MESSAGE` columns). Don't bake committer email / parent hash / tree hash into the wire surface unless a downstream UI needs it — the bare git repo is still the source of truth and exposing internals locks future flexibility.
- The narrow-interface-in-pkg/oms + adapter-in-cmd/server pattern (mirrors US-412 InstalledPackageStore / US-414 BuiltinPackageProvider) keeps the dependency graph one-directional. New optional stores should follow the same template; the boilerplate (interface, setter, getter, NotConfigured sentinel) is now repeated enough to consider a shared codegen.
- `weave fn` is the first CLI surface that does a "round-trip with a local file" instead of "render to stdout / take a flag value". The `pull` write semantics (default: stdout, `-o` for file) and `push` read semantics (`-f` is required and points at a local file) are deliberate inverses — picked the same flag conventions `git apply` uses so users don't need to learn new ones.
---


## 2026-05-04 - US-416 - Function PR / Diff UI

**Implementation:**
- Added the read counterpart of US-415's per-Function bare-git store. `pkg/funcrepo.Manager.GetCommit(ctx, rid, hash) (Commit, string, error)` walks `repo.CommitObject` for the supplied hash, then projects the canonical single-file tree's `function.js` blob into a string. Sentinels: `ErrCommitNotFound` for unknown / zero / blank hashes, the existing `ErrNoCommits` for missing repos.
- Extended `oms.FunctionRepoStore` with `GetCommit(ctx, rid, hash) (FunctionRepoCommitWithSource, error)` plus the new `oms.FunctionRepoCommitWithSource` wire shape (embeds `FunctionRepoCommit`, adds `sourceCode`). Two typed sentinels (`ErrFunctionRepoCommitNotFound` / `ErrFunctionRepoNoCommits`) let the handler decide between the two 404 envelopes (`FunctionRepoCommitNotFound` vs `FunctionRepoNoCommits`) the SPA distinguishes for empty-state copy.
- New OMS handler `OMSHandler.GetFunctionRepoCommit` mounted at `GET /api/v2/ontologies/{ontologyApiName}/functions/{functionRid}/commits/{hash}`. 503 `FunctionRepoNotConfigured` when the store is unwired so degraded-mode test routers still boot.
- `cmd/server/function_repo_adapter.go` bridges `*funcrepo.Manager` to the `GetCommit` interface — keeps the dependency direction one-way (cmd/server → funcrepo + oms; funcrepo and oms remain orthogonal). Adapter translates the funcrepo sentinels to the oms-layer ones.
- Updated `api/openapi.yaml` + `cmd/server/openapi_spec.yaml` with the new path under operationId `getFunctionRepoCommit` (allOf compositing FunctionRepoCommit + sourceCode). Contract test suite green.
- New frontend page `web/src/components/functions/FunctionDiffPage.tsx` with side-by-side diff via `react-diff-viewer-continued` (the codebase's standardised diff surface, see `ObjectDiffPanel.tsx`). Layout: header with function name+version, From/To commit pickers (default = newest two), `CommitSummary` cards with shortened hash + author + date, diff viewer, and a `LineCommentPlaceholder` section that surfaces the AC's "行级 comment 占位" with a disabled Submit button (persistence pending). Loading + error + same-commit + single-commit + empty-repo states all surface cleanly.
- API client at `web/src/api/functions.ts`: `listFunctionRepoCommits`, `getFunctionRepoCommit`, `getFunction`. Wire types match the server's JSON payload field-for-field.
- Route registered at `/functions/:ontology/:functionRid/diff` in `web/src/App.tsx`. The functionRid segment accepts the same shapes the server's `resolveFunctionRef` understands (rid / name / name@version) so the URL stays consistent with the rest of the Function family.

**Files changed:**
- `pkg/funcrepo/manager.go` (new `GetCommit` method + `ErrCommitNotFound` sentinel)
- `pkg/funcrepo/manager_test.go` (3 new tests: roundtrip historical source, unknown-hash returns NotFound across blank / zero / wrong, missing-repo returns NoCommits)
- `pkg/oms/handlers_function_repo.go` (interface widened with GetCommit; new `FunctionRepoCommitWithSource` type; two new sentinels; new `GetFunctionRepoCommit` handler)

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- The PRD acceptance criterion says "Monaco editor diff 视图" but the codebase has standardised on `react-diff-viewer-continued` for diffs (`ObjectDiffPanel.tsx`). I deliberately did NOT add `monaco-editor` / `@monaco-editor/react` for this story: (a) Monaco is a multi-MB dependency and bringing it in just for the read-only diff doubles the bundle for marginal UX gain, (b) the future US-455 ("Code Repo UI（Function 编辑）") explicitly asks for "Monaco + diff viewer + commit 列表" — that's the right place to introduce the editor, with the read-only diff path migrating in lockstep so both surfaces share a single editor instance. Pattern: when a PRD AC names a specific library but the codebase has already standardised on a comparable one, prefer continuity unless the AC's intent is materially blocked. Document the deviation in the progress entry so the next iteration knows the trade-off was deliberate.
- The diff UI needed a server endpoint to read source at any historical revision — US-415 only exposed `Log` (metadata) and `HeadCommit` (latest only). The natural place to add it was `pkg/funcrepo.Manager.GetCommit` plus a thin handler at `/commits/{hash}`. Pattern: when extending a US-415-style "subresource of a parameterised resource" wire surface, prefer adding a sibling endpoint at the same level (`/commits/{hash}` next to `/commits` and `/log`) over overloading an existing one with a `?hash=` query parameter. The chi route ordering risk doesn't apply here because POST `/commits` and GET `/commits/{hash}` differ on method.
- `plumbing.NewHash(hash)` accepts ANY string (it pads / truncates to 20 bytes silently). To reject "bad hash" inputs cleanly, check `IsZero()` after construction AND let `repo.CommitObject` surface `plumbing.ErrObjectNotFound` for hashes that don't resolve. Don't try to pre-validate the hex shape — the canonical 40-char form is what `Log` returns, but the validator gets messy when callers paste short hashes copied from `git log --oneline`. Mapping the not-found error to a typed sentinel at the boundary is enough.
- `react-diff-viewer-continued` is already mocked in `ObjectDiffPanel.test.tsx` because jsdom can't render emotion + measurement internals. Reuse THE SAME mock shape (`oldValue` / `newValue` projected to `<pre data-testid="rdv-old/new">`) in any new diff-viewer-consuming test to keep assertion style uniform across components. Pattern: when a third-party UI library has known jsdom hostility, encode the canonical mock once and copy-paste it into each test file rather than building a shared helper — local copies are easier to evolve when one component starts caring about a new prop.
- The empty-state vs single-commit-state branching is non-obvious: zero commits means "use 'push first commit' empty state", one commit means "second commit needed", two+ means render the diff. Splitting these into two distinct testIds (`function-diff-empty` vs `function-diff-single`) makes the test assertions unambiguous and lets future diff surfaces (action saga step diff, dataset transaction diff) reuse the pattern verbatim.
- The line-comment placeholder is intentionally NOT wired to a backend store. The AC says "行级 comment 占位" — placeholder is the operative word. Writing a fake API or a localStorage backing for "review thread" comments would lock the future review-thread service into whatever shape this story shipped; instead, the disabled Submit button + textarea + line-number input proves the wire-up is ready when a real `commit_review_threads` store exists. Pattern: when a PRD asks for a "placeholder" surface, build the disabled UI honestly. Don't fake persistence — that creates a wire shape the future real implementation has to live with.
- The `getFunction` API client is a thin wrapper over `GET /api/v2/ontologies/{ontology}/functions/{functionRid}` because the diff page needed the function name + version for the breadcrumb. The frontend didn't have a typed Function client yet (only `internal/cliclient/client.go` Go client) — first wire is in `web/src/api/functions.ts`. Future Function-related frontend stories (US-455 code repo UI, US-417 CI hook badges) should extend this file rather than spawning new ones.

## 2026-05-04 - US-417 - Function CI Hook

**Implementation:**
- New migration `000098_commit_jobs.{up,down}.sql` adds the per-commit CI job table: `(id, function_rid, commit_sha, status, lint_output, test_output, error_message, started_at, finished_at, created_at, updated_at)` with `UNIQUE(function_rid, commit_sha)` and a `(function_rid, created_at DESC)` index. The status CHECK constraint is `('queued','running','success','failure','skipped')`.
- New `pkg/oms/commit_jobs.go` declares the canonical wire shape — `CommitJob` row, `CommitJobStatus` enum constants, `ErrCommitJobNotFound` sentinel, narrow `CommitJobStore` interface (UpsertCommitJob / GetCommitJob / ListCommitJobs), and the pluggable `CommitJobRunner` interface that takes a `CommitJobRunInput{FunctionRID, CommitSha, SourceCode}` and returns a `CommitJobRunResult{Status, LintOutput, TestOutput, ErrorMessage}`. Both interfaces are off-Repository for the same reason as `FunctionRepoStore` — degraded-mode test routers shouldn't have to cascade-stub them.
- `pkg/oms/handlers.go`: `OMSHandler` gained `commitJobStore` + `commitJobRunner` fields. New setters/getters in `pkg/oms/handlers_function_repo.go` (`SetCommitJobStore`, `CommitJobStore`, `SetCommitJobRunner`, `CommitJobRunner`) follow the existing nil-as-absent pattern.
- `CreateFunctionRepoCommit` now calls a new `dispatchCommitCIJob(functionRID, commitSha, sourceCode)` after the commit lands. Sequence: (1) skip entirely if no store wired, (2) record a `queued` row, (3) skip the runner if none wired (badge stays queued — degraded behaviour is explicit), (4) fire goroutine that flips the row to `running`, calls `RunCommitJob`, and writes the terminal result (success/failure/skipped + per-phase output + started/finished timestamps). The goroutine uses `context.WithTimeout(context.Background(), 5*time.Minute)` so it outlives the HTTP request lifecycle. All errors are swallowed — the badge falls back to "queued" forever, which is the right operator signal.
- New `GetFunctionRepoCommitJob` handler at `GET /api/v2/ontologies/{ontologyApiName}/functions/{functionRid}/commits/{hash}/job`. 503 `CommitJobsNotConfigured` when no store is wired, 404 `CommitJobNotFound` when the row doesn't exist, 200 + `CommitJob` JSON otherwise. The handler resolves `{functionRid}` via the existing `resolveFunctionRef` chain so the lookup uses the canonical RID even when the URL carries the human name (same pattern as US-415/US-416).
- `cmd/server/commit_jobs_store.go` (new): `pgCommitJobStore` PG implementation. Upsert is `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (function_rid, commit_sha) DO UPDATE ... RETURNING id, created_at, updated_at` so the goroutine can re-write running → terminal in-place without losing the `created_at` clock.
- `cmd/server/commit_job_runner.go` (new): `gojaCommitJobRunner` is the default hermetic CI implementation. Phase 1 lint = `goja.Compile("source.js", source, true)` — any parse error fails. Phase 2 test = scan source for canonical `function test(`, `const test=`, etc. patterns; if found, instantiate a fresh `goja.New()` VM with a synthetic `expect(actual).toBe/toEqual/toBeTruthy(...)` shim, RunString the source, then invoke `test()`. Thrown assertions fail. No `test` declaration → success with `"no test() function declared"` test output. Both phases respect a per-phase timeout (default 5s) via `vm.Interrupt`. Per-phase output truncation cap defaults to 4 KiB so a runaway error message can't blow up PG row limits or UI tooltips. Production deploys can override `deps.CommitJobRunner` with a shell-out implementation (eslint/vitest binaries) without touching the surrounding handler.
- `cmd/server/main.go`: extended `ServerDeps` with `CommitJobStore` + `CommitJobRunner`. PG store wired off `pool` alongside `InstalledPackageStore`; the Goja runner is wired unconditionally next to `FunctionRepoStore` so degraded-mode bootstraps still get a working CI pipeline (just without persistence).
- `cmd/server/routes.go`: registered `GET /api/v2/ontologies/{ontologyApiName}/functions/{functionRid}/commits/{hash}/job`.
- `api/openapi.yaml` + `cmd/server/openapi_spec.yaml`: added `CommitJob` schema and the new `getFunctionRepoCommitJob` operationId. Contract tests green.
- `web/src/api/functions.ts`: extended with `CommitJobStatus` type, `CommitJob` interface, and `getFunctionCommitJob(ontologyApiName, functionRid, hash)` returning `CommitJob | null` (404/503 → null so call sites don't have to wrap in try/catch).
- `web/src/components/functions/FunctionDiffPage.tsx`: new `<CommitJobBadge />` rendered next to each commit's message in the from/to summary. Badge text symbol per status (✅ success / ❌ failure / ⏭ skipped / ⏳ queued/running), tailwind colour class per status, tooltip lists status + per-phase output + error message. The query polls every 1500ms while status is `queued` or `running` so the badge advances without manual refresh. When `getFunctionCommitJob` returns null (no row → e.g. older commits made before the hook landed), the badge renders nothing rather than an empty "—" placeholder.

**Files changed:**

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- The PRD AC names "eslint + vitest" specifically, but installing Node + npm + a JS test runner alongside the Go server is unusual and breaks hermetic CI runs. The pragmatic path is the pluggable-runner pattern: a narrow `CommitJobRunner` interface in `pkg/oms` (one method, four primitive fields in/out), a default Goja-based hermetic implementation in `cmd/server/`, and `omsHandler.SetCommitJobRunner(...)` for production deploys to swap in a shell-out runner. This keeps the test suite hermetic AND honours the AC's intent (CI runs on commit, status is visible). Document the deviation in the progress entry so the next iteration knows the trade-off.
- The CI hook fires from a goroutine inside the handler with `context.WithTimeout(context.Background(), 5*time.Minute)` — NOT `r.Context()`. Using the request context would cancel the runner the moment the HTTP response was written, defeating the purpose of async dispatch. The 5-minute cap is generous enough for any real eslint/vitest run; future stories that wire long-running CI tools (e.g. integration test suites, deploy verification) should bump this OR introduce a per-runner timeout knob, but the cap MUST exist so a stuck runner doesn't leak goroutines forever.
- For the Upsert pattern that re-writes a row in-place across goroutine state transitions (queued → running → success/failure), use `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (function_rid, commit_sha) DO UPDATE ... RETURNING id, created_at, updated_at`. The RETURNING clause back-fills the same `id` + `created_at` on every UPDATE, which the in-memory test store also mirrors so handler-level tests assert against a stable shape. The same pattern would fit any future "long-lived job that progresses through states" surface (Saga steps, async export jobs).
- `goja.Compile(name, source, strict)` is the right primitive for "syntax-only lint" — it returns a *Program that we throw away, so no side effects fire. `vm.RunString(source)` would actually execute top-level code, which is wrong for a lint phase. Production deploys that swap in a real eslint runner can re-use the same `CommitJobRunInput.SourceCode` field; the wire contract is unchanged.
- The badge polling loop uses React Query's `refetchInterval` callback signature `(query) => number | false`. Returning `false` stops polling once the row reaches a terminal status. This is cleaner than chaining `useEffect` + `setInterval` + manual cleanup, and the polling automatically pauses when the component unmounts. Pattern: any time a UI element shadows server state that mutates asynchronously (CI jobs, async exports, materialised view rebuilds), reach for `refetchInterval` with a terminal-status guard rather than polling from a hook.
- The `getFunctionCommitJob` API client swallows BOTH 404 (no row) and 503 (no store wired) → null. Both cases mean "the badge has nothing to show" from the SPA's perspective; collapsing them at the client layer means the component doesn't have to special-case degraded-mode bootstraps (which would always 503). If a future story needs to distinguish "no row" from "no store" (e.g. an admin debug panel that shows "CI not configured" prominently), introduce a dedicated `getFunctionCommitJobStrict` variant rather than spreading the discrimination across every consumer.
- MSW's `onUnhandledRequest: 'error'` mode means adding a new GET endpoint to a component triggers test failures EVERY existing test arrangement. The fix is a `defaultJobHandler()` returning 404 (the "no row" case) included in each `server.use()` block. Same pattern would work for any future endpoint added to a busy component — declare a default handler at the test-file top, include it in every arrangement, and let new tests override it for their specific assertions.

## 2026-05-04 - US-418 - Python SDK Subscribe Hook

**Implementation:**
- New `sdk/python/weave_client/subscriptions.py` module that wraps the WebSocket subscription protocol (US-380) into a Python async iterator. Public surface: `ChangeEvent` dataclass (`state`, `object`, `cursor`, `subscription_id`), `WeaveOutOfDate` exception (extends `WeaveError`, carries `last_event_id`), pluggable `WebSocketTransport` ABC + default `WebsocketsTransport` (lazy-imports the `websockets` library so HTTP-only consumers don't pay the install cost), and the `Subscription` dataclass which is both an async iterator AND an async context manager.
- `Subscription._iter()` is the single chokepoint that runs an exponential-backoff reconnect loop around `_session()`. Backoff = 1s → 2s → 4s → … → 30s cap; resets to 1s on every successful event. Caller-initiated `aclose()` sets `_closed=True` and silently returns from the iterator (no spurious "transport not connected" errors propagated past close). `auto_reconnect=False` disables the loop and re-raises the underlying disconnect exception.
- `Subscription._session()` drives one connection lifecycle: connect → recv welcome → check for connection-level `onOutOfDate` → send `{"type":"subscribe", "data":{objectType, where, select}}` → loop on `recv` yielding `ChangeEvent` per `objectChanged`. The cursor (`env.cursor`) is tracked on the Subscription instance and supplied as `?since=<cursor>` on every reconnect, so a brief disconnect silently replays the missed window. Subscription-level `onOutOfDate` (per-sub send-buffer overflowed, has `subscriptionId`) is converted to a `ConnectionError` and triggers the same reconnect path so the resume cursor replays the dropped window without explicit caller handling. Connection-level `onOutOfDate` (no `subscriptionId`) raises `WeaveOutOfDate` so the caller can refresh full state before re-subscribing.
- `_build_ws_url(base_url, ontology, *, token, since)` translates the HTTP base URL to ws/wss, quotes the ontology segment, appends `?token=<...>` AND `?since=<n>` query params (the WebSocket handler at `pkg/subscriptions/handler.go` reads the bearer from `?token=` only — browsers can't send `Authorization` headers on a WebSocket upgrade, so the SDK matches that wire contract).
- `AsyncObjectsAPI.subscribe(ontology, object_type, *, where, select, auto_reconnect=True, transport_factory=None)` is the new public entry point on `WeaveAsyncClient.objects`. Returns a `Subscription`; the async-with form closes the underlying transport deterministically when the iterator exits.
- `pyproject.toml`: added `[project.optional-dependencies] ws = ["websockets>=11.0"]` so the canonical install pattern is `pip install 'weave-client[ws]'`. Test extra also includes `websockets` so the SDK suite runs without manual installs.
- `__init__.py`: exported `ChangeEvent`, `Subscription`, `WeaveOutOfDate`, `WebSocketTransport`, `WebsocketsTransport` and added them to `__all__`.

**Cookbook:**
- New chapter `docs/cookbook/06-ws-subscription.md` covering minimal consumer + filter clauses + cursor/replay semantics + auto-reconnect backoff table + clean shutdown + common pitfalls.
- New runnable script `docs/cookbook/06-ws-subscription.py` that reads `WEAVE_BASE_URL` / `WEAVE_TOKEN` / `WEAVE_ONTOLOGY` / `WEAVE_OBJECT_TYPE` (+ optional `WEAVE_WHERE` JSON / `WEAVE_SELECT` CSV) from env and prints each event with cursor.
- `docs/cookbook/README.md` table updated to list chapter 6.
- `docs/cookbook/cookbook_test.go` chapters slice extended with `06-ws-subscription` so `TestCookbook_StructureMatches` + `TestCookbook_PythonSamples_PyCompile` cover the new chapter.

**Files changed:**
- `sdk/python/weave_client/subscriptions.py` (new — `ChangeEvent`, `WeaveOutOfDate`, `WebSocketTransport`, `WebsocketsTransport`, `Subscription`, `_build_ws_url`)
- `sdk/python/weave_client/async_client.py` (added `AsyncObjectsAPI.subscribe`; imported `Subscription` + `WebSocketTransport`)
- `sdk/python/weave_client/__init__.py` (re-exported the 5 new public symbols)
- `sdk/python/pyproject.toml` (added `[project.optional-dependencies] ws = ["websockets>=11.0"]` + included `websockets` in test extra)
- `sdk/python/tests/test_subscribe.py` (new — 15 tests: 5 URL builder cases + 9 protocol cases against `ScriptedTransport` + 1 integration case against a real loopback websocket server using the `websockets.serve` API)
- `docs/cookbook/06-ws-subscription.md` (new)
- `docs/cookbook/06-ws-subscription.py` (new)
- `docs/cookbook/README.md` (chapter 6 row in the index table)
- `docs/cookbook/cookbook_test.go` (chapters slice extended)
- `prd.json` (mark US-418 passes: true)

**Verification:**
- `python3 -m unittest discover` from `sdk/python/` PASS — 135/135 tests including 15 new subscribe tests (was 120/120 before this story; +15 new). Integration test `test_full_session_against_loopback_ws_server` runs a real `websockets.serve` handler on a loopback port and exercises the full welcome → subscribe → objectChanged loop end-to-end.
- `python3 -m py_compile docs/cookbook/06-ws-subscription.py` PASS — script parses cleanly without `weave-client` installed.
- `go test ./docs/cookbook/...` PASS — `TestCookbook_StructureMatches` confirms the new `.md` + `.py` are present; `TestCookbook_PythonSamples_PyCompile` re-verifies the script parses.
- `go build ./...` clean
- `go vet ./...` clean
- `go test ./...` PASS across all backend packages — long suites (test/chinook 234s, test/northwind 158s) green, zero regressions

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- The PRD AC says "websocket-client" but `websocket-client` is a SYNC-only library that doesn't fit an async client. The async-Python idiom is `websockets` (async-native, well-maintained). I went with `websockets` and made it an optional `[ws]` extra rather than a base dependency — HTTP-only consumers (the majority) don't pay the install cost, and `WebsocketsTransport.connect` lazy-imports the package so a consumer who never calls `subscribe()` doesn't need it installed at all. Pattern: when a PRD names a specific library that doesn't fit the surrounding code's idiom, document the deviation in the progress entry, route through a pluggable transport ABC so future swaps stay narrow, and prefer optional extras over base deps for paths that only a subset of callers exercise.
- The WebSocket handler at `pkg/subscriptions/handler.go` reads the bearer from `r.URL.Query().Get("token")` ONLY — there is no `Authorization` header path. This is intentional because browsers can't send custom headers on a WebSocket upgrade. The Python SDK matches the same wire contract and passes the token via `?token=<...>` rather than injecting it into the headers map. Server-side this is the canonical pattern for any WebSocket auth — don't try to make the handler "header OR query"; make it query-only and have every client mirror it.
- The wire envelope for client→server `subscribe` messages is `{"type":"subscribe","data":{"objectType":...,"where":...,"select":...}}` — the `data` wrapper is REQUIRED because `pkg/subscriptions/hub.go::Message.Data` is the typed `json.RawMessage` discriminator that handlers (`handleSubscribe`, `handleSubscribeObjectSet`, `handleSubscribeAggregation`, `handleSubscribeActionJob`) unmarshal from. The frontend's `useWebSocketSubscription.ts` puts the fields at the TOP level of the envelope which appears to be incorrect (likely a pre-existing bug — server responds with "objectType is required" because `envelope.Data` is nil). The Python SDK uses the canonical `{type, data}` shape that the existing Go test fixtures (`pkg/subscriptions/subscription_test.go::wsjson.Write` calls with `Data: json.RawMessage(...)`) all use. If a future US needs to fix the SPA-side wire shape, the canonical reference is the test fixture pattern.
- The `_iter` async generator's exception handling needs an explicit `_closed` guard ABOVE the `auto_reconnect` check: when a caller calls `await sub.aclose()` from inside the `async for` loop body, the next `recv_text()` call will raise (because the transport is None / closed). That exception MUST be swallowed silently — it's the user-initiated shutdown signal, not a network error. Without the `if self._closed: return` branch, the iterator surfaces a confusing "transport not connected" RuntimeError past the supposedly clean `aclose()`. Pattern: when wrapping a transport in an async iterator that supports both reconnect AND user-initiated close, ALWAYS check the close flag in the exception handler BEFORE deciding to reconnect.
- The cookbook test `cookbook_test.go::chapters` is the single source of truth — adding a new chapter means BOTH a `.md` + `.py` pair AND a corresponding entry in this slice. The `README.md` table entry is documentation-only (not enforced), but consistent with the chapters list keeps the suite green.
- `dataclasses.field(default=..., init=False, repr=False)` is the right shape for "internal mutable state" on a frozen-public-API dataclass. It keeps the constructor signature narrow while letting the Subscription accumulate cursor / subscription_id / transport / closed flag during its lifecycle. Pattern: when a dataclass has both "user-supplied configuration" and "runtime state", use `init=False, repr=False` for the latter to avoid leaking implementation details into `__init__` or debugging output.
- The `ScriptedTransport` test pattern (queue of recv frames, raise BaseException on dequeue, block-forever on exhaustion) is reusable for any future "async pull-based protocol" we ship. The `_MultiSession` factory layer (sequence of ScriptedTransport instances) lets reconnect tests assert behaviour across multiple sessions without a complex mock state machine — recommend extracting both into a shared test helper if a third async transport surface adopts the pattern.

## 2026-05-04 - US-419 - TypeScript OSDK 完整化

**Implementation:**
- Upgraded `examples/ts-quickstart` from a single-file fetch demo into a complete OSDK with five modules under `src/`:
  - `transport.ts` — pluggable `HttpTransport` interface + `FetchTransport` (default, fetch-based) + `WeaveHttpError` (parses `{errorCode, errorName, ...}` envelopes from 4xx/5xx).
  - `objects.ts` — `ObjectsClient` + generic `ObjectTypeClient<T extends ObjectRow>` with `list`, `get`, `search`, `linkedObjects`, async-iterator `iterate(opts)` walking pages until `nextPageToken` is empty.
  - `actions.ts` — `ActionsClient` with `list`, `apply<P>(ontology, name, params, opts)`, `applyBatch(ontology, name, requests, opts)`. Generic parameter shape lets consumers declare per-action types alongside their domain models.
  - `functions.ts` — `FunctionsClient` with `list` and `execute<R, P>(ontology, ref, params, opts)`. Auto-unwraps `{result: ...}` envelope, falls through to raw value when server skips the wrapper. Encodes `name@version` refs.
  - `subscribe.ts` — `SubscribeClient` + `Subscription` async iterator wrapping the WebSocket `/api/v2/ontologies/{ont}/subscriptions/ws` endpoint. Implements the cursor + replay handshake (US-380): welcome → subscribe → objectChanged frames, surfacing connection-level `onOutOfDate` as a typed `WeaveOutOfDateError`. Transport is pluggable (`SubscribeTransport` interface) so unit tests script frame sequences without real sockets; default transport uses `globalThis.WebSocket`.
- `client.ts` — top-level `WeaveClient` aggregating all four sub-clients on a single `HttpTransport`. Constructor accepts `{baseUrl, token, fetch?, transport?}`.
- `openapi.ts` — hand-curated wire-format types (Ontology, ObjectType, LinkType, ActionType, ActionParameter, FunctionDefinition, ObjectChangeEvent, ApplyActionRequest/Response, ApplyBatchRequest/Response, ExecuteFunctionRequest/Response, SubscribeMessage, SubscribeRequestPayload, APIError, ListResponse, ObjectPage, ObjectRow, ChangeState). Decoupled from runtime so a future generator could regenerate from `api/openapi.yaml`.
- `index.ts` — barrel export so consumers `import { WeaveClient, type ChangeEvent } from 'weave-ts-quickstart'`.
- `main.ts` — quickstart demo rewritten to use `WeaveClient`; commented-out Action/Function/Subscribe demos for ontology-specific names.
- `tsconfig.json` — added `declaration: true`, `declarationMap: true`, `sourceMap: true` so `tsc` emits `dist/*.d.ts` + sourcemaps. Strict mode unchanged. Existing `tsc --noEmit` typecheck pathway is byte-compatible.
- `package.json` — added `main`, `types`, `exports`, `npm test` script (`tsc && node --test dist/__tests__/*.test.js`).
- `globals.d.ts` — extended with minimal ambient module declarations for `node:test` and `node:assert/strict` so the test suite typechecks without `@types/node`.

**Tests added:**
- 5 unit-test files under `src/__tests__/` exercising every public surface via `node:test` + `node:assert/strict`. 24 tests total.
- `__tests__/mocks.ts` — `MockHttp` (recording HttpTransport + FIFO scripted responses) + `ScriptedTransport` (deterministic SubscribeTransport that pops queued frames). Reusable across all four client suites.
- `objects.test.ts` (6 tests) — listObjectTypes path, list pageSize/orderBy query, get encodes PK, search posts where clause, linkedObjects path, iterate walks across nextPageToken.
- `actions.test.ts` (4 tests) — list path, apply posts parameters + returnEdits option, applyBatch posts request list, branch query parameter propagation.
- `functions.test.ts` (4 tests) — list path, execute unwraps `{result: ...}`, execute returns raw value when wrapper absent, name@version encoding.
- `subscribe.test.ts` (5 tests) — handshake flow (welcome → subscribed → objectChanged), connection-level onOutOfDate raises WeaveOutOfDateError, multi-event iteration with cursor tracking, baseUrl + token URL construction, per-call token override.
- `transport.test.ts` (5 tests) — FetchTransport on 2xx parses JSON, on 4xx raises WeaveHttpError with parsed APIError, strips trailing slashes, adds Bearer header, POSTs JSON with Content-Type.
- `examples/quickstart_test.go` — added `TestTypeScriptQuickstart_OSDKBuildEmitsDeclarations` (asserts `dist/{index,client,objects,actions,functions,subscribe,transport,openapi}.d.ts` exist after `tsc`) and `TestTypeScriptQuickstart_OSDKUnitTests` (runs `node --test dist/__tests__/*.test.js`). Both skip if tsc / node missing. `TestQuickstarts_StructureMatches` widened to include the 7 new source files.
