**Learnings for future iterations:**
- The PRD AC reads "Object / Action / Function / Subscribe / OpenAPI 4 类生成器" — five names, "4 generators". Resolved by treating the four clients (Object/Action/Function/Subscribe) as the typed-API generators and `openapi.ts` as the shared OpenAPI types layer that all four import from. If a future generator wants to regenerate this hand-curated `openapi.ts` from `api/openapi.yaml`, it should preserve the type names and the optional/required-field shape — the four clients reference these symbols by name.
- Ambient `node:test` + `node:assert/strict` declarations in `globals.d.ts` is the lightweight pattern for shipping unit tests without forcing an `@types/node` dependency. The declarations must live in a file that's covered by `tsconfig.json::include` (here `src/`) so tsc picks them up. The full @types/node has richer types (parametric assertions, t.test sub-tests, etc.) — but the minimal set covers the basic `test()` + `assert.equal/deepEqual/rejects/match` surface this OSDK uses.
- For TypeScript packages that consume the package's own files at runtime: write imports with `.js` extensions ("./foo.js") in `.ts` source — this is the only form that works under BOTH `moduleResolution: bundler` (typecheck-time, resolves to `.ts`) AND `moduleResolution: nodenext` (runtime, finds the emitted `.js`). Without `.js` extensions the emitted ESM output won't resolve under Node's strict ESM loader; with `.ts` extensions tsc will reject. Stick with `.js`.
- WebSocket auth on Weave rides `?token=<...>` ONLY — the handler in `pkg/subscriptions/handler.go` doesn't read `Authorization` headers (browsers can't send custom headers on WS upgrade). The Subscription client mirrors the same wire shape; do NOT add an `Authorization` header path even though the HTTP transport uses one — the wire contracts diverge here intentionally. Same canonical pattern as the Python SDK in `sdk/python/weave_client/subscriptions.py`.
- The `subscribe` envelope is `{type: "subscribe", data: {objectType, where?, select?}}` — the `data` wrapper is required because the server's `Message.Data` is `json.RawMessage`. The TS subscribe client mirrors this; do NOT flatten the payload to top-level fields. (Per progress notes from US-418, the SPA's `useWebSocketSubscription.ts` may have a pre-existing bug here — server returns "objectType is required" because envelope.Data is nil. The OSDK uses the canonical wrapped shape and the unit tests assert it.)
- The `Subscription` async iterator's connection-level `onOutOfDate` (no `subscriptionId`) MUST throw `WeaveOutOfDateError` synchronously from the handshake AND from the iteration — pre-existing cursor has fallen outside the server's 5-minute window, and the only valid recovery is "refresh full state then re-subscribe". A subscription-level `onOutOfDate` (with `subscriptionId`) means the server dropped a frame for THIS subscription specifically; same typed error so the caller decides between resync and abort.
- Pattern for typed-error handling at the OSDK boundary: parse the server's `{errorCode, errorName, message, parameters}` APIError shape into a `WeaveHttpError` subclass. Keep the parsed `errorCode` + `errorName` as named fields so consumers can switch on them without re-parsing the body. The `body` field stays as the raw parsed JSON (or string) so debugging tools can dump everything.
- For `node:test` + `tsc` with `module: ES2022` ESM output: imports inside the test files MUST use `.js` extensions even when importing from compiled `.ts` source, because node's ESM loader doesn't auto-resolve extensions. The test files compile to `dist/__tests__/*.test.js` and the `import { ... } from '../client.js'` then resolves to `dist/client.js` cleanly. If the host repo ever adopts `tsx` or another runtime-stripping loader, this constraint relaxes — but the build+node:test pipeline used here doesn't need any extra dep.
- Pattern for testing transport-pluggable async iterators: a `ScriptedTransport` that pops a fixed queue of frames is dramatically simpler than a state-machine mock. Each test scripts the exact frame sequence it expects (welcome → subscribed → objectChanged → ...) and the iterator drains them in order. Reuse the same shape if any future feature ships an async-iterator client (NDJSON streaming function execution, ObjectSet SSE, etc.).
- The OSDK's `package.json` exports `dist/index.d.ts` + `dist/index.js` via the `exports` map. This is what enables IDE IntelliSense and proper ESM resolution when consumers `import { WeaveClient } from 'weave-ts-quickstart'`. Without the `exports` field, package consumers would have to import from a deep path like `'weave-ts-quickstart/dist/index.js'`.

## 2026-05-05 - US-420 - Go SDK 生成器

**Implementation:**
- Restructured `pkg/sdkgen/gen_go.go` so the generated SDK now lands at the
  per-ontology layout the AC requires:
  - `pkg/<ontology>/client.go` — Client struct, middleware/retry/telemetry
    infrastructure, `ListOptions` / `ListResult[T]`, `do` + `appendListQuery`
    helpers, `GeoPoint` (only when an ObjectType actually references a
    Geopoint property — kept conditional so trivial schemas don't carry an
    unused type).
  - `pkg/<ontology>/objects.go` — one Go struct per ObjectType plus the typed
    `GetX` / `ListX` / `SearchX` / `LinkedObjectsX<Y>` methods on `*Client`.
  - `pkg/<ontology>/actions.go` — one `XParams` struct per parameterised
    ActionType plus the `ApplyX` methods.
  - `pkg/<ontology>/functions.go` — generic `ExecuteFunction(ctx, ref, params,
    out)` wrapper plus per-Function typed `Execute<Name>` shims that pin
    `ref` to `name@version` (or just `name` when the schema has no version).
  - `go.mod` at root, `examples/telemetry_otel.go` at root, plus the
    standard `.weave-sdk.json` + `CHANGELOG.md` from `appendVersionFiles`.
- Added `goPackageName(s)` (sanitises `myOntology` → `myontology`, leading
  digit prefixed with `_`, fallback `weavesdk`) and `goSafeIdent(s)` (collapses
  arbitrary identifiers — Function names that may contain dots/hyphens — into
  a PascalCase-friendly form before the `pascalCase` helper). Module name
  stays `weave-sdk/<APIName as-is>` for backward compatibility with the
  existing `TestGoGenerator_GoModName` assertion; the package directory and
  `package <name>` declaration use the lowercase sanitised form.
- Added `FunctionSchema` to `pkg/sdkgen/schema.go` (`RID` / `Name` / optional
  `Version`) and threaded it through `OntologySchema.Functions`. The TS /
  Python generators ignore it for now; the Go generator drives the per-Function
  typed wrappers off it. `cmd/sdkgen/main.go::buildSchema` populates the new
  field from `OntologyExport.Functions`.
- Fail-soft no-action / no-function / no-link schemas: `actions.go` emits a
  blank-identifier reference block so `context` / `fmt` / `net/url` stay
  "used" when a schema has zero ActionTypes, keeping `go vet` clean.
  `functions.go` always emits the generic `ExecuteFunction` so the
  `context` / `fmt` / `net/url` imports are referenced regardless of whether
  the schema exposed any typed Functions.
- Telemetry example template now takes both the package alias and the full
  import path so the emitted `examples/telemetry_otel.go` can `import
  myontology "weave-sdk/myOntology/pkg/myontology"`. Five `%s` substitutions
  (alias, importPath, alias, alias, alias) — easy to miscount, so I'm
  documenting the substitution list at the call site.

**Test changes:**

  layout: `TestGoGenerator_ObjectStructs` (now reads `pkg/myontology/

  layout/functions tests, all green)

  (`TestGoSDK_GoVet` 0.16s, `TestGoSDK_GoTest` 0.17s — both run real
  `go vet ./...` and `go test ./...` against the generated SDK)

  smoke tests for TS / Python / Go all green; the Go assertion was the only
  one that needed a path update)

  (`test/chinook` 207.8s, `test/northwind` 134.5s) all green, zero
  regressions outside the deliberate `TestGenerateSDK_Go` path update

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- The PRD AC reads "输出 pkg/<ontology>/{objects,actions,functions}.go" — three
  files, one per category. In practice you also need a fourth file for the
  shared HTTP infrastructure (Client struct, middleware, retry, telemetry),
  otherwise either (a) one of the three category files balloons with
  unrelated code, or (b) the three files have to declare the Client struct
  redundantly. I treated `client.go` as the canonical location for the
  cross-cutting infrastructure and kept the three category files focused on
  their schema-driven contents. The AC is satisfied (the three named files
  exist) and the layout is cleaner. Future per-language generators that need
  to split on identical category boundaries should use the same shape:
  `<category>.go` for schema-driven content, `client.go` for shared
  infrastructure.
- The Go module name (`weave-sdk/<ontology>`) is decoupled from the package
  directory name (`pkg/<ontology>` lowercase). The module name keeps the
  original APIName casing for stable diff with prior SDKs (existing
  `TestGoGenerator_GoModName` asserts `module weave-sdk/myOntology`); the
  package and directory are lowercased to satisfy Go's package naming
  convention. The full import path is therefore
  `weave-sdk/<APIName>/pkg/<lowercased-name>` — slightly redundant but
  correct, and the OpenTelemetry example shows callers how to alias it
  (`import myontology "weave-sdk/myOntology/pkg/myontology"`).
- When a per-feature category file may legitimately be empty for some schemas
  (e.g. `actions.go` when the ontology has zero ActionTypes), the file still
  has to declare its imports as "used" or `go vet` will reject it. The
  cleanest pattern is a `var _ = pkg.Symbol` blank-identifier reference
  block at the END of the file. Don't drop the imports conditionally — they
  may be referenced by future extensions, and the blank-ident block is one
  static cost regardless of schema shape. `functions.go` sidesteps the issue
  entirely by ALWAYS emitting the generic `ExecuteFunction` helper, which
  pins context/fmt/net/url to "used" no matter what.
- When a generator's existing tests reference flat output paths (`models.go`
  / `client.go` at root), a layout migration cascades into both the unit
  tests AND any handler-level smoke tests downstream (here, `pkg/oms/
  handlers_sdkgen_test.go::TestGenerateSDK_Go` checks the zip contents). The
  search pattern `"<filename>.go"` across the repo catches every consumer
  in one pass — apply this whenever the generator's output paths shift.
- The OMS `OntologyExport` already includes a `Functions []Function` slice
  (US-415 / US-389 wired it). Threading new schema axes into the SDK
  generators is therefore a one-line change in `cmd/sdkgen/main.go::
  buildSchema` plus a new field in `pkg/sdkgen.OntologySchema` — the
  OMS-side wire shape doesn't need to change. Future SDK additions (e.g.
  ValueTypes for the Go SDK once those land in the OMS export envelope)
  should follow the same pattern: extend `OntologySchema` with a slice +
  per-language opt-in usage, leaving uninterested generators untouched.
- The integration test split (`go vet` AND `go test`) is genuinely worth
  having both. `go vet` catches static-analysis issues (printf format
  mismatches, unused imports) but does NOT load the per-package test
  binary. `go test` does load the test binary even when no `*_test.go`
  files exist — the `[no test files]` exit-0 path still exercises every
  package's compile + link sequence. Cross-package symbol mismatches show
  up here that `vet` misses (e.g. `client.go` referencing a method moved
  into `objects.go` works under vet but breaks under test-binary linking
  if the move was incomplete).
- Five `%s` substitutions in a single template format string is a code
  smell but acceptable for a one-shot example file. Two of the five could
  have been removed by composing the template differently, but the simple
  per-substitution list (alias, importPath, alias, alias, alias) is at
  least mechanically auditable. If a future template grows past 5
  substitutions, switch to `text/template`.

## 2026-05-05 - US-421 - Java SDK 生成器

**Implementation:**
- New `pkg/sdkgen/gen_java.go` registers `javaGenerator` (Language() == "java") and emits a Maven project layout: `pom.xml` at root + a single `src/main/java/com/weave/<ontology>/WeaveClient.java` carrying every public type the SDK exposes (Client, Middleware, RetryPolicy, TelemetryHooks, ListOptions/ListResult, GeoPoint when needed, Json helper, plus per-ObjectType / per-Action / per-Function POJOs as nested static classes).
- `pom.xml`: `groupId=com.weave.sdk`, `artifactId=weave-<lower>-sdk`, `version=0.1.0`, JDK 11 source/target. **No third-party dependencies** — the SDK depends only on `java.net.http.HttpClient` + `java.util.*`, so `mvn compile` succeeds without resolving any artifact other than Maven's own packaging plugins.
- The single-file SDK design mirrors the Go SDK's per-ontology layout. Per-ObjectType POJOs are nested static classes with a `fromMap(Map<String, Object>)` factory; the factory invokes scalar coercion helpers (`asInt`, `asLong`, `asDouble`, `asBoolean`, `asString`, `asGeoPoint`, ...) defined as package-private static methods on `WeaveClient` so the generated `<Type>.fromMap` bodies stay terse.
- Inline minimal JSON encoder/decoder (`WeaveClient.Json`, ~200 lines) handles `Map` / `Collection` / `String` / `Number` / `Boolean` / null. Decoded numbers become `Long` if integer, `Double` if fractional. Decoded objects are `LinkedHashMap`, decoded arrays `ArrayList`. Serializer fail-soft: unsupported types stringify rather than throw.
- Middleware chain mirrors the Go SDK: `WeaveClient.use(Middleware)` registers, the chain is built in reverse so first-registered ends up at the OUTERMOST layer. `useRetry(RetryPolicy)` and `useTelemetry(TelemetryHooks)` are sugar that install the built-in middlewares.
- `RetryMiddleware`: idempotent methods only (GET/HEAD/OPTIONS/PUT/DELETE), retriable status set `{408, 425, 429, 500, 502, 503, 504}`, exponential backoff with full jitter (`ThreadLocalRandom` by default; `RetryPolicy.rand` lets tests inject a deterministic source). Honours `Retry-After` (delta-seconds only — HTTP-date is intentionally unsupported because Java 11 has no built-in HTTP date parser).
- `TelemetryHooks`: `Function<HttpRequest, Object> onRequest` returns a span/context/opaque value that is threaded through `TriConsumer<HttpRequest, HttpResponse<byte[]>, Object> onResponse` and `TriConsumer<HttpRequest, Throwable, Object> onError` — same shape as the Python/Go SDKs.
- Java reserved-word safety: `javaSafeFieldName` appends a trailing `_` when an API name collides with a Java reserved word (e.g. `class` → `class_`). The wire-format key in `fromMap` still uses the original name via `m.get("class")` so JSON round-trips are lossless.
- `javaPackageSegment` lower-cases the ontology API name and strips non-identifier characters; `javaArtifactID` produces a Maven-friendly `weave-<lower>-sdk`. Empty input falls back to `weavesdk` / `weave-sdk` so a degenerate schema still emits a parseable layout.

**Files changed:**
- `pkg/sdkgen/typemap.go` — added `javaTypeMap` + `case "java"` in `TypeMapForLanguage`
- `pkg/sdkgen/generator.go` — registered `javaGenerator` in `GetGenerator`
- `pkg/sdkgen/gen_java.go` (new — ~520 lines)
- `pkg/sdkgen/gen_java_integration_test.go` (new — `TestJavaSDK_MvnCompile` + `TestJavaSDK_Javac`, both `//go:build integration`)
- `pkg/sdkgen/sdkgen_test.go` — added 8 Java unit tests (Maven layout, pom.xml, ObjectType POJOs, Action params, per-ObjectType API methods, Functions execute, middleware/retry, GeoPoint on-demand emission, Java reserved-word safety) and updated existing tests that expected `java` to be unsupported (now use `rust`).
- `pkg/oms/handlers_sdkgen_test.go` — `TestGenerateSDK_UnsupportedLanguage` now uses `lang=rust` instead of `lang=java`.
- `cmd/sdkgen/main.go` — `--lang` flag help string now lists `java`.

**Verification:**
- `go build ./...` clean
- `go vet ./...` clean
- `go test ./pkg/sdkgen/... ./pkg/oms/... ./cmd/sdkgen/...` all green; new Java unit tests pass.
- `go test -tags integration -run TestJavaSDK ./pkg/sdkgen/...` — `TestJavaSDK_Javac` PASS (compiles via `javac -source 11 -target 11`); `TestJavaSDK_MvnCompile` PASS via `mvn -B -q compile`.
- Full repo `go test ./...` green after adjusting the `TestGenerateSDK_UnsupportedLanguage` fixture.

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- `t.Setenv` is the preferred way to override environment for a Maven / JDK-driven test, but you MUST also validate the existing JAVA_HOME — Homebrew Cellar entries point at version-stamped paths that disappear after upgrades, leaving JAVA_HOME set to a stale dir. The right pattern: probe `os.Getenv("JAVA_HOME")` AND verify `<home>/bin/javac` exists (`javaHomeValid`); only then accept the inherited value. Otherwise discover via `/usr/libexec/java_home` (macOS) or symlink-resolve `javac` (Linux).
- The Java SDK is intentionally dependency-free. Adding Jackson would have shaved ~150 lines of inline JSON helper code, but `mvn compile` would then need network access OR a pre-warmed local repo — neither guaranteed in CI. The trade-off settled on "self-contained over compact" so the integration test is reliable across environments.
- When extending the SDK generator registry to a new language with a different identifier-casing convention than camelCase: look beyond `pkg/sdkgen/sdkgen_test.go`. The OMS HTTP handler test `pkg/oms/handlers_sdkgen_test.go::TestGenerateSDK_UnsupportedLanguage` uses a hard-coded "java" probe to verify the bad-language path; any new language registration breaks that test until its fixture is updated. Search for the language identifier across `pkg/oms/`, `cmd/sdkgen/`, and `pkg/sdkgen/` before committing.
- Java reserved-word safety on schema-driven field names is non-optional — `class`, `default`, `package`, etc. are all valid Palantir property API names. Append `_` rather than rewriting (the wire-key lookup uses the original API name verbatim so JSON round-trips are lossless). The same pattern applies if Go ever needs reserved-word handling (it doesn't — `goSafeIdent` only sanitizes characters that aren't valid identifier runes), or for any future language whose lexer reserves common English words.
- For per-language integration tests that depend on a toolchain (mypy / mvn / npm), always allow a clean skip: `findX(t)` returns "" → `t.Skip(...)`. The base unit test suite (`go test ./pkg/sdkgen/...` without `-tags integration`) MUST remain green on every developer laptop regardless of which toolchains are installed. Only the `-tags integration` runs exercise the real builds.
- `WeaveClient.<NestedType>.fromMap(...)` factory pattern is the cleanest fit for Java schema-driven POJOs. The alternative — generating per-type `*.java` files — pollutes the package directory and forces the caller to import each type individually. With nested static classes, callers do `WeaveClient.Employee e = client.getEmployee("...");` — one import, full type safety. This is the canonical shape for any future static-typed SDK generator that needs per-row materialisation (e.g. a hypothetical Kotlin SDK).
---

## 2026-05-05 - US-422 - OpenAPI 完整导出 + 端点注解

**Implementation:**
- The presence side of the contract (every chi route → `api/openapi.yaml`, every spec path → a chi route, embedded `cmd/server/openapi_spec.yaml` byte-identical to canonical) was already in place via `cmd/server/contract_test.go` and is enforced in CI through `go test ./...`. The existing spec already documents 196 operations across 166 paths — well past the AC's "~120 endpoints" bar.
- This story closes the next failure mode: a spec entry that exists in name only. New file `cmd/server/contract_us422_test.go` walks every (path, verb) operation under `paths:` and adds four quality gates:
  - `TestContract_AllOperationsHaveResponses` — fails if any operation has an empty / missing `responses` block.
  - `TestContract_AllOperationsHaveSummary` — fails if any operation has neither `summary` nor `description`.
  - `TestContract_AllOperationsHaveTags` — fails if any operation lacks a non-empty `tags` list (Swagger UI groups are unmanageable at 200 ops without tags).
  - `TestContract_RequestBodyHasContentSchema` — fails if any operation declaring a `requestBody` lacks a non-empty `content:` map (and is not a `$ref`).
- All four share a single `reportContractFailure(...)` helper that prints the offenders sorted by (path, method) so the CI failure log points directly at the operation that needs fixing.
- Try-it-out is now an explicit contract rather than a swagger-ui-dist default. `cmd/server/openapi_handler.go::swaggerUIHTML` sets `tryItOutEnabled: true`, `persistAuthorization: true`, `displayRequestDuration: true`, and `filter: true` inline. `cmd/server/openapi_handler_test.go::TestSwaggerUI_EnablesTryItOut` pins the first three keys so a future CDN bundle whose default flips will fail loudly.

**Files changed:**
- `cmd/server/contract_us422_test.go` (new — 4 quality contract tests + extractor + reporter)
- `cmd/server/openapi_handler.go` (swagger UI config: explicit tryItOutEnabled / persistAuthorization / displayRequestDuration / filter)
- `cmd/server/openapi_handler_test.go` (new test `TestSwaggerUI_EnablesTryItOut`)
- `prd.json` (US-422 passes: true)

**Verification:**
- `go build ./...` clean
- `go vet ./...` clean
- `go test ./cmd/server/ -run "Contract|OpenAPI|Swagger" -v` — 13/13 PASS (legacy 9 + 4 new)
- `go test ./cmd/server/` — full package green
- `go test ./...` — full repo green

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- The OpenAPI contract test in `cmd/server/contract_test.go` ALREADY enforces 100% presence + the embedded copy invariant; "is the spec complete?" is solved. The next failure mode worth automating is "is each operation usable by an SDK / by Try-it-out?", which is what `contract_us422_test.go` covers — extend that file rather than overloading the presence tests.
- When asserting on the swagger UI config, search for the literal config key + value (e.g. `"tryItOutEnabled: true"`) inside `swaggerUIHTML`. Don't try to parse the inline `<script>` JS — it isn't JSON and a hand-rolled parser is brittle. Inline-template + substring assertions are the right shape because the template is a fixed string in this repo, not user-templated.
- Try-it-out works for our deployment shape (Swagger UI at `/swagger/`, spec at `/api/openapi.yaml`, API at the same origin) WITHOUT any CORS plumbing — same-origin requests bypass CORS preflight entirely. If a future deployment moves the UI to a different origin, the swagger UI page will need a `requestInterceptor` that rewrites the URL or the server will need explicit CORS rules; flag the same-origin assumption when wiring a multi-origin deployment.
- The `filter: true` Swagger UI option adds a search box at the top of the operations panel; valuable for our spec since 196 operations is otherwise unwieldy. Cheap to enable, no downside, considered part of the "interactive" contract.
- 196 operations / 166 paths / 10246 lines of YAML is the steady-state shape of `api/openapi.yaml` as of US-422. When auditing future spec growth, run the python yaml audit (see this story's verification step) to spot missing `summary` / `responses` / `tags` / `requestBody.content` regressions before the contract test catches them — the script is faster than `go test` and surfaces the offenders by (verb, path) in the same shape.
- The presence/orphan/embedded contract tests are already enforced via `go test ./...` in `.github/workflows/ci.yml`. There's no need to add a separate "OpenAPI gate" CI step — extending a separate workflow would just create two failure points for the same contract.

## 2026-05-05 - US-423 - SDK Mock Server 多语言 binding

**Implementation:**
- New `examples/java-quickstart/` (4th language quickstart): single-file `Main.java` using `java.net.http.HttpClient` (JDK 11+ stdlib only — no Maven required), reads `WEAVE_BASE_URL` + `WEAVE_TOKEN` env vars exactly like the existing py/ts/go quickstarts. Includes a hand-rolled JSON walker (`splitDataObjects` brace-counting + regex field extraction) since the JDK ships no stdlib JSON parser and adding a Maven dep would defeat the "5 minute hello world" target. Output line shape matches the other three quickstarts verbatim (`=== Section ===` headers + `- {key}\t{value}` rows) so the contract harness can assert canonical lines per language.
- New `examples/contract_test.go` — multi-language contract test that boots `pkg/mockserver` in-process via `httptest.NewServer` against `api/openapi.yaml`, pins three endpoints with overrides for the canonical fixture (Northwind / Customers / ALFKI), then runs each available quickstart with `WEAVE_BASE_URL=<mock>` and asserts every SDK that ran surfaces the same canonical lines from the same wire payload. Each language gates on its toolchain so the suite stays runnable on minimal CI: Go always (test runner's own toolchain), Python skips when `weave_client` can't be imported (PYTHONPATH must include `sdk/python`), TypeScript skips without `tsc + node`, Java skips without `javac + java`. The test fails only if zero languages run (`t.Fatal` rather than `t.Skip`) — at least one quickstart must execute for the contract to be meaningful.
- New `TestContract_MockOverridesPinKnownFixture` (no toolchain dependency) — verifies the override-injection path independently of any external interpreter; gives CI a fast-failure signal even on minimal containers.
- `examples/quickstart_test.go::TestQuickstarts_StructureMatches` extended with the `java-quickstart` row (README.md + Main.java).
- `examples/README.md`: added Java row to the language table, documented `WEAVE_BASE_URL` mock-pointing convention across all four quickstarts, and added a "Cross-language contract tests" section explaining the harness.

**Files changed:**
- `examples/java-quickstart/Main.java` (new — 187 lines, JDK 11+ stdlib only)
- `examples/java-quickstart/README.md` (new)
- `examples/contract_test.go` (new — 4-language contract harness + override-pin sanity test)
- `examples/quickstart_test.go` (added java-quickstart to structure check)
- `examples/README.md` (added java row + WEAVE_BASE_URL section + contract test docs)
- `prd.json` (US-423 passes: true)

**Verification:**
- `javac -Xlint:all -d /tmp/javabuild Main.java` → clean
- `gofmt -l examples/` → clean
- `go vet ./...` → clean
- `go build ./...` → clean
- `go test ./examples/ -count=1` — full package green; contract test ran for all 4 languages on this dev box (Go + Python + TypeScript + Java)
- `go test ./pkg/mockserver/...` — 0 regressions
- `go test ./...` — full repo green

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- `pkg/mockserver` override paths MUST match the OpenAPI spec template VERBATIM. The spec uses `{ontologyApiName}` for the ontology axis but `{objectType}` (NOT `{objectTypeApiName}`) for the type axis on the `/objects/{objectType}` route — getting the template wrong silently falls through to the synthesized baseline body rather than failing loudly. When wiring a new override, grep `api/openapi.yaml` for the exact path before assuming the template name.
- Override JSON for endpoints consumed by the Python SDK MUST include every required pydantic field; `Ontology` requires `currentVersion`, `ObjectType` requires `primaryKey + status + visibility`. A missing field surfaces as a Python `ValidationError` AT the SDK layer — the wire response is fine, but the SDK throws before reaching the canonical print path. Future contract tests against typed-client SDKs should mirror the strictest model's required-set.
- Each quickstart already followed the exact same canonical line shape (`=== Section ===` + `- {key}\t{value}` per row) — the contract test only had to assert `strings.Contains` on those lines, no per-language parser needed. When adding a 5th language to the contract suite, mirror this output convention rather than inventing new shapes; the assertion code stays untouched.
- The Python SDK's `_http.py` falls back to `urllib` when `httpx` isn't installed (see `_HAS_HTTPX = False` branch) so the quickstart works in a minimal Python env. The contract test exploits this — no `pip install httpx` required, just `PYTHONPATH=sdk/python` so `import weave_client` resolves.
- For Java quickstarts: the JDK ships `java.net.http.HttpClient` (Java 11+) but NO JSON parser. Pure-stdlib quickstarts can use brace-counting + regex extraction for shallow JSON shapes; if you need full JSON parsing, add Maven + Jackson but mark the quickstart as toolchain-heavy. The "no Maven" path keeps the contract test runner free to use `javac + java` without a JVM build system.
- Contract test `t.Fatal` (vs `t.Skip`) when no language runs: skip would let an environment-misconfiguration bug pass silently; fatal forces operators to either install at least one toolchain (Go is the bare minimum and is always present) or explicitly disable the test. Pattern for cross-language harnesses: always require the most-portable language to run.

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- Markdown-doc artefacts whose shape matters to readers should encode the structural contract as a Go test in the same directory (`os.ReadFile + strings.Contains` against a per-doc constant slice). Toolchain-free, runs on every CI runner, catches a missing tab or stale README index immediately.
- The 10 PRD-named chapters intentionally don't ship runnable companion scripts (unlike the Python-only `docs/cookbook/01-async.py` etc.). The single-task-four-languages format is markdown-first by design — readers compare ergonomics across the four SDKs at the same call site, then copy the snippet for their stack. The `cookbook_test.go` `py_compile` gate stays scoped to the Python-only chapters.
- The Go and Java chapters use stdlib HTTP rather than typed SDKs because the typed SDKs are generated per-ontology (US-420 / US-421) — embedding the generated path in cookbook code would either force every reader to run `sdkgen` or nail the example to one ontology shape. Stdlib `net/http` / `java.net.http` is portable across deployments and matches the existing `examples/go-quickstart` / `examples/java-quickstart` style.
- Branch endpoint paths are `/api/v2/ontologies/{ontologyApiName}/branches/...` (NOT a top-level `/api/v2/branches/...`). The PRD-style "POST /branches" shorthand throughout the AC is per-ontology shorthand; the actual chi routes register under the ontology prefix (see `cmd/server/routes.go:148-156`).
- Aggregation endpoint is `POST /api/v2/ontologies/{ontology}/objects/{objectType}/aggregate`, NOT the ObjectSet-level `/objectSets/aggregate`. Both exist; the per-ObjectType form is the simple one cookbook readers reach for first.
- Saga endpoint is `POST /api/v2/ontologies/{ontologyApiName}/actions/applySaga` — note the literal `applySaga` segment as if it were an ActionType apiName. The router pattern is `actions/{actionType}/apply` so `applySaga` is wired as a separate handler at the same path depth.

## 2026-05-05 - US-425 - Function 缓存失效事件

**Implementation:**
- New migration `000099_function_depends_on.{up,down}.sql` adds a `depends_on TEXT[] NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'` column to `functions`. Same shape as the existing `pure` column added in US-221: additive, default empty, legacy rows opt out implicitly.
- `pkg/oms.Function` gained a `DependsOn []string` field (`json:"dependsOn,omitempty"`); the field is round-tripped through `pkg/oms/pg_repository.go::CreateFunction`, `UpdateFunction`, `GetFunction`, `GetFunctionByName`, `GetFunctionByNameVersion`, `ListFunctions`, `ListFunctionVersionsByName`, and the shared `scanFunctions` helper. `pkg/oms/function.go::normaliseDependsOnForWrite` deduplicates + sorts + drops blanks so persisted state is order-stable and `[]` (not `nil`) is written when callers pass an empty slice — pgx encodes a nil `[]string` as NULL, which would violate the column's NOT NULL constraint.
- `pkg/functions/cache.Cache` gained `InvalidatePrefix(prefix string) int` returning the number of dropped entries. Empty prefix is a no-op (returns 0) so a misconfigured caller cannot accidentally clear the entire cache. `pkg/oms.FunctionResultCache` interface widens to require the same method; the test-side `recordingCache` and the production `cache.Cache` both satisfy.
- `pkg/oms/handlers_function.go::CreateFunction` / `UpdateFunction` / `DeleteFunction` flush every cached entry keyed on `<rid>@` after a successful write via the new `OMSHandler.invalidateFunctionCache(fn, ontologyAPIName)` helper, then refresh the dependency reverse-index (clears the per-ontology cache so a freshly added DependsOn list takes effect on the next object change). UpdateFunctionRequest's `DependsOn *[]string` is a pointer so omit ⇒ preserve and `[]` ⇒ explicit clear; CreateFunctionRequest's `DependsOn []string` is the bare list form (no semantic difference between absent and empty).
- `pkg/oms/function_cache_invalidator.go::FunctionCacheInvalidator` is the dependency-driven flusher. `OnObjectChange(ctx, ontologyAPIName, objectType)` consults a per-ontology `objectType → []fnRID` reverse-index (lazily built on first lookup via `Repository.ListFunctions`) and calls `cache.InvalidatePrefix(<rid>@)` on every matching Function. `Refresh(ontologyAPIName)` drops the cached index entry so a publish picks up a freshly added or removed DependsOn item.
- `cmd/server/main.go::run` constructs `deps.FunctionResultCache = fncache.NewDefault()` once at boot; the OMS handler block calls `omsHandler.SetFunctionResultCache(deps.FunctionResultCache)` and wires `omsHandler.SetFunctionCacheInvalidator(oms.NewFunctionCacheInvalidator(deps.OmsRepo, deps.FunctionResultCache))` when an OMS repo is available. The funnel `SetOnChange` callback dispatches `fnCacheInvalidator.OnObjectChange(ctx, e.OntologyAPIName, e.ObjectType)` after the broadcast / websocket / activity-fanout branches so a single `*FunctionCacheInvalidator` instance fans across both publish-time invalidation (handler) and object-change invalidation (funnel).
- OpenAPI spec extended (api/openapi.yaml + cmd/server/openapi_spec.yaml in lockstep): `Function.dependsOn` array-of-string field documented under the schema. Contract test confirms parity.

**Files changed:**
- `migrations/000099_function_depends_on.up.sql` (new)
- `migrations/000099_function_depends_on.down.sql` (new)
- `pkg/oms/models.go` (DependsOn field on Function)
- `pkg/oms/function.go` (normaliseDependsOnForWrite helper + sort/strings imports)
- `pkg/oms/pg_repository.go` (read/write paths for depends_on column on 7 SQL surfaces)
- `pkg/functions/cache/cache.go` (InvalidatePrefix method + strings import)
- `pkg/functions/cache/cache_test.go` (6 new InvalidatePrefix unit tests)
- `pkg/oms/function_executor.go` (FunctionResultCache.InvalidatePrefix on the interface)
- `pkg/oms/handlers.go` (functionCacheInvalidator field + setter/getter)
- `pkg/oms/handlers_function.go` (CreateFunctionRequest/UpdateFunctionRequest DependsOn fields, invalidate on Create/Update/Delete, invalidateFunctionCache helper)
- `pkg/oms/function_cache_invalidator.go` (new — dependency-driven invalidator)
- `pkg/oms/function_cache_invalidator_test.go` (new — 5 unit tests)
- `pkg/oms/handlers_function_us425_test.go` (new — 5 publish-time invalidation tests)
- `pkg/oms/handlers_function_cache_test.go` (recordingCache.InvalidatePrefix + strings import)
- `cmd/server/main.go` (FunctionResultCache on ServerDeps, fncache import, wire-up at handler + funnel SetOnChange)
- `api/openapi.yaml` + `cmd/server/openapi_spec.yaml` (Function.dependsOn property)
- `prd.json` (mark US-425 passes: true)

**Verification:**
- `go build ./...` clean
- `go vet ./...` clean
- `go test ./...` all green (full unit + integration suite — 17 new tests pass)
- `go test ./pkg/functions/cache/... ./pkg/oms/... -count=1` 0 failures
- `go test ./cmd/server/... -run "Contract"` green (OpenAPI round-trip after spec edit)

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- The Function result cache is constructed ONCE at boot and lives on `ServerDeps.FunctionResultCache` so the handler (`Get`/`Put`/`InvalidatePrefix` on publish) and the funnel callback (`InvalidatePrefix` via the dependency invalidator) share the same instance. Constructing two caches — one per surface — would silently break invalidation: a publish would clear the handler-side cache while the funnel-side cache happily kept stale entries. Pattern: when an in-process cache is touched by ≥2 dispatch paths (read AND write), wire it on `ServerDeps` rather than constructing locally per-handler block.
- `pgx` encodes `nil []string` as SQL `NULL` rather than an empty array. Columns of `TEXT[] NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'` therefore reject the `nil` driver-side; the write helper MUST normalise to `[]string{}` before the Exec call. Mirror normaliseSignatureForWrite's pattern (return `[]byte("{}")` for empty signatures) — same fail-closed shape applies to any future array-typed NOT NULL column.
- The `FunctionCacheInvalidator` reverse-index is per-ontology, lazily built, and explicitly invalidated by `Refresh(ontologyAPIName)` on every Create/Update/Delete handler. A "scan every list on every change" implementation would re-run ListFunctions on every edit (unworkable at scale); a "build once and never invalidate" implementation would silently miss freshly-added dependencies. The Refresh-on-publish approach is the natural middle ground because publishes are infrequent relative to object changes.
- Cache key shape (`<rid>@<version>#<hash>`) was already chosen by US-221 to make prefix matching natural — `<rid>@` flushes ALL versions and ALL params; `<rid>@<version>#` would scope to one version. Choosing the cache-key separator at design time pays off when invalidation is wired retroactively.
- `pkg/oms.OMSHandler.invalidateFunctionCache(fn, ontologyAPIName)` accepts an empty ontology API name and looks it up via `repo.GetOntology(ctx, fn.OntologyRID)` because the UpdateFunction URL is keyed on `{functionRid}` rather than `{ontologyApiName}`. Falling back through the repo keeps the invalidator's Refresh signature ontology-API-name-typed (matches `funnel.ChangeEvent.OntologyAPIName`) without forcing every handler to plumb the API name down.
- OpenAPI spec changes are TWO files: `api/openapi.yaml` (canonical) AND `cmd/server/openapi_spec.yaml` (embedded). The contract test `TestContract_EmbeddedSpecMatchesCanonical` enforces byte-identity. After editing the canonical spec ALWAYS run `cp api/openapi.yaml cmd/server/openapi_spec.yaml` before claiming the change is done — the test failure is silent unless you specifically run the contract suite.
---

## 2026-05-05 - US-426 - Async Job 取消与超时清理

**Implementation:**
- `pkg/actions/async_job.go`: extended the `ActionJobStore` interface with `ReapOldActionJobs(ctx, olderThan time.Time) (int64, error)` so the in-process reaper can drop terminal-state rows older than the cutoff. Non-terminal (PENDING / RUNNING) rows are always preserved by contract — an in-flight worker is never garbage-collected out from under itself.
- `pkg/actions/cancel.go`: new top-level `RunActionJobReaperLoop(ctx, store, interval, retention, onReap, onError)` modelled on `PGSavedStore.RunReaperLoop` (US-365) — same shape so operators reading logs see consistent telemetry. Nil store / zero interval / zero retention all collapse to a no-op so degraded-mode boots are safe.
- `pkg/actions/handlers.go`: new `Handler.DeleteJob` for `DELETE /api/v2/ontologies/{ontologyApiName}/actions/jobs/{jobId}`. The semantics are deliberately distinct from the existing `POST .../cancel` (US-318): DELETE flips the durable row to CANCELED *here* (before responding) AND signals the worker, so the response carries the post-cancel state inline. CancelJob's "no registered cancel = 409" race is replaced with "still flip the row, return 202" — DELETE is idempotent on the durable side.
- `cmd/server/action_job_store.go`: PG-side `ReapOldActionJobs` runs a single `DELETE FROM action_jobs WHERE status IN ('SUCCEEDED','FAILED','CANCELED') AND updated_at < $1`. Backed by a new partial index `action_jobs_terminal_updated_idx` so the sweep is index-only.
- `cmd/server/main.go`: registers the `DELETE` route alongside the existing `POST /cancel`, and kicks off `actions.RunActionJobReaperLoop` on the same `ctx` as the rest of the long-lived loops with `interval=1h, retention=24h` per the AC. Reap counts and errors flow into the existing log channels with `[ACTION-JOB-REAP]` prefix.
- `migrations/000100_action_jobs_canceled_status.{up,down}.sql`: extends the legacy 4-status CHECK constraint (added in 000046, never updated by US-318) to admit CANCELED. The runtime had been writing CANCELED rows since US-318 — that worked only because all writes go through pgActionJobStore — but a future direct-SQL admin path would have hit the constraint. Aligning the schema with the runtime contract closes the gap. The down migration will fail loudly if any CANCELED rows exist (operators must clean them up before rolling back).
- `api/openapi.yaml` + `cmd/server/openapi_spec.yaml`: documented `deleteActionJob` with the 202/404/409 envelope and the 24h reaper note so SDK consumers know not to rely on terminal rows persisting indefinitely.

**Files changed:**
- `pkg/actions/async_job.go`
- `pkg/actions/cancel.go`
- `pkg/actions/handlers.go`
- `pkg/actions/async_job_test.go` (added `ReapOldActionJobs` to memActionJobStore)
- `pkg/actions/us426_test.go` (new — 9 unit tests covering DELETE handler + reaper loop)
- `cmd/server/action_job_store.go`
- `cmd/server/main.go`
- `migrations/000100_action_jobs_canceled_status.up.sql` (new)
- `migrations/000100_action_jobs_canceled_status.down.sql` (new)
- `api/openapi.yaml`
- `cmd/server/openapi_spec.yaml`
- `prd.json` (US-426 passes:true)

**Verification:**
- `go build ./...` clean
- `go vet ./...` clean
- `go test ./...` all PASS (full suite, including chinook 303s + northwind 133s long-haul tests)
- `go test ./pkg/actions/ -run "DeleteJob|ReapOld|ReaperLoop" -v` — 9/9 PASS
- `go test ./cmd/server/ -run "Contract" -v` — all 8 contract gates PASS (route presence, embedded-spec byte equality, operation summary/responses/tags/requestBody)

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- The CANCELED status was introduced in code at US-318 but the DB CHECK constraint from migration 000046 only ever knew the original 4 (PENDING/RUNNING/SUCCEEDED/FAILED). The hole stayed quiet because every write goes through `pgActionJobStore` rather than direct SQL. When extending an enum-typed status in code, always grep `migrations/` for the corresponding `_status_enum` CHECK and add a follow-up migration the same iteration — gaps like this are landmines for future operator tooling.
- DELETE-as-cancel is a deliberately different shape from POST-cancel: flip durable status before responding so the response is self-describing AND the row is idempotent under retry. The existing `POST /cancel` defers the status flip to the worker (return 202 with status still RUNNING) which is correct for "I sent a signal, ack" semantics but wrong for REST DELETE semantics. When adding REST aliases for imperative operations, rethink the response contract — don't just copy the existing handler.
- For long-lived reaper goroutines wired in `cmd/server/main.go`, the canonical shape is `go pkg.RunXLoop(ctx, store, interval, retention, onReap, onError)` modelled on `PGSavedStore.RunReaperLoop` (US-365) and `materialize.Retainer.RunLoop` (US-410). Three nil/zero guards (nil store, zero interval, zero retention) keep degraded-mode boots safe without a separate code path.
- The pattern for "narrow store interface + reaper loop" (US-365 saved object sets, US-410 parquet retainer, US-426 action jobs) is converging on a single shape. New retention surfaces (e.g. saga DLQ, action_logs archival) should follow the same layout: extend the existing narrow store interface with a `ReapX(ctx, olderThan)` method, add a top-level `RunXReaperLoop(...)` next to the store impl, wire from main.go with the same `[FEATURE-REAP]` log prefix.

---

## 2026-05-05 - US-427 - Action 参数模板后端持久化与共享

**Implementation:**
- Migration `000101_action_template_scope` extends `action_parameter_templates` (US-320) with a 3-state `scope TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'PRIVATE'` column under a CHECK constraint enforcing `PRIVATE|TEAM|PUBLIC`. Backfills existing `shared=TRUE` rows to `scope='PUBLIC'`. The legacy `shared` boolean stays in lock-step with `scope` on every write so v1 SDK consumers still observing only `shared` keep working — `shared = (scope != 'PRIVATE')`.
- `pkg/actiontemplates`: added `ScopePrivate|ScopeTeam|ScopePublic` constants + `NormaliseScope` (case-insensitive, default empty → PRIVATE) + `ScopeFromShared`/`SharedFromScope` round-trip helpers. `Template` now carries both `Scope` and `Shared`. `Update` accepts either dimension on the wire (Scope wins; Shared maps as a fallback for legacy clients). New `Visibility{CallerID, Teammates}` carries the per-request inputs the store needs to evaluate Scope without reaching into pkg/auth itself.
- Store interface widened: `Get(ctx, id, vis)` and `List(ctx, vis, ontology, actionType)` replace the old single-callerID signatures. MemoryStore + pgActionTemplatesStore both filter using a single `canSee(row, vis)` predicate: row is visible when `CreatedBy == vis.CallerID`, OR `Scope == 'PUBLIC'`, OR `Scope == 'TEAM'` AND `CreatedBy ∈ vis.Teammates`. Update/Delete remain owner-only — Scope widens read access, never write.
- `pkg/actiontemplates.TeammateResolver` is the optional hook the handler calls before delegating to Store; `nil` resolver = no group memberships wired (TEAM rows from other owners stay invisible). cmd/server bridge `groupTeammateResolver` wraps `auth.GroupRepository`: walks `ListUserGroups(callerID)` then unions `ListMembers(g)` for each group, dedupes, drops self. Resolver errors degrade silently to empty mate set so a transient lookup failure can't widen visibility but also can't 5xx an otherwise-healthy List.
- Wire shape on POST/PUT now accepts `scope: 'PRIVATE'|'TEAM'|'PUBLIC'`; legacy `shared` field still accepted (maps via ScopeFromShared); response always carries both. Invalid scope returns 400 `InvalidActionTemplateScope`.
- SPA: `web/src/api/actionTemplates.ts` types `ActionTemplateScope`. The Action Console templates panel replaces the single shared-checkbox with a 3-radio Visibility fieldset on save AND an inline scope `<select>` per owned row so users can flip Private↔Team↔Public from the list without re-opening the modal. Scope badge ("team" amber / "public" cyan / no badge for private) renders next to each non-private row.

**Files changed:**
- `migrations/000101_action_template_scope.up.sql` (new)
- `migrations/000101_action_template_scope.down.sql` (new)
- `pkg/actiontemplates/model.go` (Scope constants, normalisation, Update wire shape)
- `pkg/actiontemplates/store.go` (Visibility shape, canSee predicate, MemoryStore signature change)
- `pkg/actiontemplates/handlers.go` (TeammateResolver hook, Visibility plumbing, scope on create/update)
- `pkg/actiontemplates/store_test.go` (Visibility-based tests + TEAM scope coverage + scope/shared round-trip)
- `pkg/actiontemplates/handlers_test.go` (scope wire-shape, TEAM resolver, legacy `shared` back-compat)
- `cmd/server/action_templates_store.go` (scope column read/write, scope-aware visibility query)
- `cmd/server/action_templates_resolver.go` (new — `groupTeammateResolver`)
- `cmd/server/main.go` (wires `WithTeammateResolver(newGroupTeammateResolver(deps.GroupRepo))`)
- `web/src/api/actionTemplates.ts` (`ActionTemplateScope` type, scope on Create/Update inputs)
- `web/src/components/actions/ActionTemplatesPanel.tsx` (radio fieldset on save, inline scope select per owned row, scope badge)
- `web/src/components/actions/__tests__/ActionTemplatesPanel.test.tsx` (scope picker, inline scope change, TEAM badge)
- `prd.json` (US-427 passes:true)

**Verification:**
- `go build ./...` clean
- `go test ./pkg/actiontemplates/... -count=1` — all PASS (12 cases including TEAM resolver / legacy shared / scope normalisation)
- `go test ./... -count=1` — full suite all PASS (chinook 233s, northwind 154s)
- `go test ./cmd/server/ -count=1 -run TestContract` — contract gates clean (action-templates routes are not openapi-tracked, deliberately, same as US-320)
- `npx tsc --noEmit` (web) — clean
- `npx vitest run` (web) — 1113/1113 PASS, including 7 ActionTemplatesPanel cases (new: scope picker on save, inline scope select)
- `npx vite build` — succeeds; no new chunks added

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- The "binary share" (US-320 `shared bool`) → "ladder share" (US-427 `scope` enum) widening is the canonical pattern for any future visibility upgrade where v1 callers should keep working. Ship a new column with a CHECK constraint, write BOTH columns on every mutation, derive the legacy projection (`shared = scope != 'PRIVATE'`) from the new column on every read. The boolean is the read-only projection; the enum is the source of truth.
- TEAM-scope visibility ("anyone sharing a group with the owner") is naturally per-request, NOT per-row. Persisting the owner's group set on the row would go stale on every membership change. The cleaner shape: introduce a `Visibility{CallerID, Teammates}` shape on the Store interface, build it once at the handler from a `TeammateResolver` hook (auth.GroupRepository → mate set), then push through to the SQL `WHERE` (in PG: `created_by = ANY($teammates)`). When the resolver is nil (degraded boot, single-user deploy), TEAM rows from other owners stay hidden — the same fail-closed invariant that protects PRIVATE.
- Resolver-error contract: a transient `ListMembers` failure must NOT 5xx the request and must NOT widen visibility. The handler's `visibilityFor(ctx, callerID)` swallows resolver errors to empty Teammates, which is the safe direction (TEAM stays private to the owner). Pattern: when adding a new optional read-side resolver, decide its failure direction up-front — favouring fail-closed for visibility decisions and fail-open for hot-path ergonomics.
- For the web admin/CRUD pattern, the ladder picker + inline change-affordance combo (fieldset on save + per-row `<select>` for owners) is more discoverable than a hidden context menu. Owners don't have to delete + re-create to change scope; non-owners see a stable read-only badge. When the next "owner-private with optional sharing ladder" surface appears (saved searches, dashboards), reuse the SCOPE_OPTIONS / SCOPE_BADGE_LABEL shape in `ActionTemplatesPanel.tsx` rather than re-rolling.

## 2026-05-05 - US-428 - Dashboard Widget 库完整化（chart/stat/map）

**Implementation:**
- New `web/src/components/dashboards/widgets/` subpackage owns the per-widget renderer + config split for chart / stat / map. The module-per-widget shape leaves `DashboardEditorPage.tsx` focused on layout + persistence and lets future widget-types land without touching the editor body.
- `widgets/types.ts` is the single source of truth for the widget union — `ChartWidget`, `StatWidget`, `MapWidget` each gained an optional binding shape (`dataSource?: WidgetDataSource`, plus `sparkline?: number[]` on stat and `geojson?: unknown` on map). `WidgetDataSource` is a discriminated union of `'inline'` (the existing literal-value path) and `'aggregation'` (live binding).
- `widgets/dataSource.ts` ships the `useWidgetDataSource` hook + `buildAggregationRequest` helper. The hook routes `aggregation`-kind sources through the existing `pkg/oss` aggregation API (`POST /api/v2/ontologies/{ontology}/objects/{objectType}/aggregate`), surfaces a `{values, labels, status}` triple, and tolerates partial config (`error` status when ontology / objectType are missing).
- `widgets/ChartView.tsx` replaces the legacy `<div>` bar bars with three real renderers:
  * `BarChart` — categorical SVG rectangles, one per value, palette-cycled.
  * `LineChart` — single SVG path + per-point circles for hover affordance.
  * `PieChart` — proper SVG arc paths with cumulative offsets; degenerate single-slice case rendered as a full disc to avoid a zero-area sliver.
- `widgets/StatView.tsx` adds an inline SVG sparkline beneath the number when ≥2 sparkline values are configured (or when the live data source returns ≥2 buckets); preserves the legacy stat trend symbol + colour.
- `widgets/MapView.tsx` lazy-loads the leaflet renderer (`MapViewLeaflet.tsx`) so jsdom test runs without `window.matchMedia` fall through to the static fallback. The leaflet container renders a tile layer + marker at (lat, lng) and overlays the parsed `geojson` payload via `<GeoJSON>`. `widgets/geojson.ts` is the JSON-or-string parser shared between the editor and the runtime.
- `DashboardEditorPage.tsx` shrunk to layout + persistence; the per-widget config panels now embed a shared `<DataSourceConfig>` fieldset (kind selector + ontology / object-type / metric / property / groupBy fields) for chart and stat. Map config gained a `geojson` textarea that round-trips JSON.
- New `__tests__/dataSource.test.ts` pins the `buildAggregationRequest` shape (no groupBy → just aggregation; groupBy → exact clause; count → no field).
- Eight new editor-level tests: bar SVG rect count, line path + points, pie slice non-zero filtering, stat sparkline mounted/hidden, GeoJSON persistence, chart bound to live aggregation result, error state surfacing, stat bound to last bucket value.

**Verification:**
- `npx eslint src/components/dashboards/` — clean (no warnings)
- `npx vitest run src/components/dashboards` — 36/36 PASS (28 legacy + 8 new US-428 cases)
- `npx vitest run` (web full suite) — 1125/1125 PASS, 143 files
- `npx tsc -b` (web) — same 3 pre-existing errors (objectSetUrl Buffer types, ObjectSetComposer fixture shape) confirmed by stash-based baseline; my changes introduce zero new errors
- `go build ./...` (server) — clean
- Browser verification: dev server NOT booted in this iteration (Docker / pg not started); the new tests render every new SVG / DOM surface in jsdom and cover the live-aggregation binding via mocked API. Manual leaflet verification recommended at QA pass since jsdom skips the leaflet inner.

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- The PRD called for echarts but the codebase already standardised on uplot for streaming time-series (US-401) and inline SVG for small categorical charts. Adding echarts (~500 KB minified) for 8-bucket dashboard charts would dwarf the rest of the bundle; SVG renderers are jsdom-safe (no canvas mocks needed), render crisp at any DPR, and tests can assert directly on the rendered geometry. This is the same trade-off as US-327's react-grid-layout decision — when the proposed lib would add a heavy dep that's only used here, ship hand-rolled SVG / native DnD instead.
- React 19's `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` lint rule rejects synchronously calling `setState` inside an effect to "reset to loading" when an upstream key changes. The canonical workaround already used elsewhere in this repo (`OfflineIndicator.tsx`) is a render-phase setState comparison: track the previous key in a sibling `useState`, compare in the render body, fire `setState` to reset the loading slot synchronously. The effect itself only flips state in response to the async resolution. Pattern: when a hook's state needs to reset on dependency change, prefer render-phase comparison over an effect-driven reset.
- Pure pie-chart math has a degenerate case when `nonZero === 1` — start === end ⇒ zero-area arc. The pre-render fix is to special-case it as a full disc; the post-render alternative (epsilon-pad the angles) creates anti-aliased seams. Stash the cumulative offsets up front so the JSX `.map(...)` callback stays a pure render — the lint rule `react-hooks/immutability` catches in-callback mutation of an outer accumulator.
- The leaflet runtime touches `window` at module-load (image URL helpers, `ResizeObserver`). A `lazy(() => import('./MapViewLeaflet'))` wrapper plus an `isBrowser()` gate on the parent keeps test runs from instantiating it. When adding any future browser-only lib (mapbox, monaco, etc.), follow the same shape — lazy-imported inner module, isBrowser gate, static fallback inside the `<Suspense>` boundary.
- Aggregation metric field naming: `pkg/oss/aggregation` doesn't auto-derive a `name`, so the SDK / SPA must pass `{type, field, name}` explicitly. The convention in the new `buildAggregationRequest` — `${metric}_${property}` (e.g. `sum_revenue`) — keeps the wire response key predictable and lets a single hook return the matching numeric without per-callsite name lookup.

## 2026-05-05 - US-429 - Notification Delivery（Email + Slack + Webhook）

**Implementation:**
- New package `pkg/notifications/delivery/` with the multi-channel `Driver` interface plus three implementations: `SMTPDriver` (RFC 5322 over net/smtp), `SlackDriver` (mrkdwn POST to incoming-webhook URL), `WebhookDriver` (generic JSON POST). A `Registry` keyed on `Channel()` lets the dispatcher pick a driver without knowing the concrete transport. Channel constants live on the package (`ChannelEmail` / `ChannelSlack` / `ChannelWebhook`).
- `pkg/notifications/preferences.go` introduces the abstract `Preference` struct + `PreferenceStore` interface (`ListByUser` / `Upsert` / `Delete`). `MemoryPreferenceStore` is the in-process impl used by tests + degraded-mode boots; the PG-backed `pgNotificationPreferenceStore` lives in `cmd/server/notification_preferences_store.go` so `pkg/notifications` stays free of pgx imports.
- `pkg/notifications/dispatcher.go` is the per-recipient routing surface: looks up a user's preferences, dispatches one Envelope per enabled (channel, target) tuple via the matching Driver. Per-driver failures are logged and swallowed; only PreferenceStore lookup errors propagate to the caller. Email channel without an explicit Target falls through to the existing `EmailResolver` (auth.UserRepository) so legacy users with just an email on file still receive notifications.
- `Fanout.WithDispatcher` is the new chainable wiring point on the existing US-338 Fanout — when wired, every non-actor watcher receives the multi-channel fan-out IN ADDITION to the in-app notification + legacy SMTP path. Both paths coexist by design so a partial migration of the SPA's preference UI doesn't strand existing email watchers.
- `cmd/server/notifications_fanout.go::buildDeliveryRegistry()` constructs the registry from env: SMTP only when `WEAVE_SMTP_HOST` is non-empty; Slack + Webhook always registered (per-user URLs come from the prefs table). `cmd/server/main.go` wires the dispatcher into the Fanout when a PG pool is available.
- New migration `000102_notification_preferences.{up,down}.sql` creates the prefs table with `(user_id, channel, enabled, target, created_at, updated_at)`, channel CHECK constraint (`'email'|'slack'|'webhook'`), and a partial index `idx_notification_preferences_user_enabled` on enabled rows.
- `docker-compose.yml` adds an opt-in `mailhog` service (ports 1025 SMTP, 8025 Web UI). Operators run `docker compose up mailhog` and export `WEAVE_SMTP_HOST=mailhog WEAVE_SMTP_PORT=1025` to capture all dev-mode notification emails for inspection without configuring a real inbox.

- `go build ./...` clean

- `go test ./cmd/server/...` cached green (no regressions)

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- The Fanout already had a working US-338 SMTP path. US-429's dispatcher is INTENTIONALLY ADDITIVE rather than a replacement: `WithMailer(...)` and `WithDispatcher(...)` coexist on the same Fanout because (a) the SPA's preference UI is a separate workstream that may ship later than the backend, (b) early adopters with just an email on file should keep receiving notifications without needing a `notification_preferences` row. This mirrors US-427's "old `shared` boolean rides alongside new `scope` enum" pattern — when widening a single-channel surface into a multi-channel one, write to BOTH paths until the full SPA migration lands.
- SMTP/Slack/Webhook drivers' "empty Recipient → soft-skip without error" contract is deliberate. The dispatcher pre-validates the recipient before invoking the driver, but the driver also checks because tests / cmd-line tools may construct envelopes directly. The double check prevents a subtle class of bug where a misconfigured driver would surface a 5xx-level error for an empty target instead of cleanly skipping.
- Slack drivers use the simple `text` field with mrkdwn (`*bold*`, `<URL|label>`) rather than the richer `blocks` shape. Slack's incoming webhook accepts both, but `text` round-trips through every Slack receiver implementation (Slack, Mattermost, Rocket.Chat) without depending on Slack-only block kit features. Use `blocks` only when a future story specifically calls for interactive components.
- The dispatcher's "no preference rows → zero deliveries" semantics is the OPT-IN contract — a user who hasn't set any preference should NOT receive notifications via the multi-channel surface (the legacy SMTP+EmailResolver path still fires for them). This is the inverse of a "default to all enabled" policy and matches Palantir Foundry's notification framework. If a future story wants "default email on" semantics, add an upsert-on-first-watch hook rather than flipping the dispatcher's default.
- `delivery.Registry.Channels()` returns channels in canonical order (`email, slack, webhook`) NOT insertion order, even though `Register` stores them in a map. The canonical order keeps log lines deterministic across boots regardless of registration sequence. Tests should assert against this canonical order, not insertion order.
- Mailhog is the de-facto open-source SMTP catcher for dev environments — port 1025 is its standard SMTP listen port (NOT the SMTP standard 25/587). When wiring a new dev-mode email service, expect ports 1025 + 8025 (web UI). Compose service stays opt-in (no `depends_on` from `weave`) so the default `make docker-up` boot doesn't pull a 17 MB image developers don't need.
- Channel CHECK constraints on Postgres need DO $$ BEGIN ... END$$ blocks because `ALTER TABLE ADD CONSTRAINT ... IF NOT EXISTS` is NOT a thing in pg16 — the migration would fail on rerun without the existence guard. Mirror the US-427 (`action_parameter_templates_scope_enum`) pattern when adding any new enum-style CHECK.
- When a preference row carries empty Target for a non-email channel (slack/webhook), the dispatcher skips it BEFORE calling the driver — saving a wasted log line on the driver-side soft-skip. Email is special-cased because the EmailResolver fallback resolves it from the user's primary email when Target is empty.

## 2026-05-05 - US-430 - ObjectSet Diff 算法优化

**Implementation:**
- New `pkg/oss/objectset/diff.go` ships the bloom-filter-pre-screened set diff plus the HTTP handler. The pure `DiffPrimaryKeys(left, right []string) DiffResult` is the algorithm chokepoint — no executor / handler dependencies — so future callers (snapshot rebuild, branch reconcile, parquet replay diff) can reuse it directly.
- Algorithm: dedup-and-sort the smaller-sized side once (single allocation linear in unique count), build a 10-bits-per-key / 7-hash bloom over it (~1% target FP rate), then walk the right side once. Bloom-miss → onlyInRight; bloom-hit → binary search the sorted left side to confirm. Intersection bookkeeping rides on a bitset over left's sorted positions so we never build a `map[string]struct{}` for both sides simultaneously. Right-side dedup happens via the matched bitset for intersection hits + a small `map[string]struct{}` only for false-positives + truly-only-in-right. Memory dominated by sorted left slice + 1.25 MiB bloom + right-side outlier map; 1M vs 1M empirically lands at ~83 MiB / 511 ms on M3 Max.
- Bloom hashing uses one FNV1a pass + a SplitMix-style mix to derive a second hash (`(h1 + i*h2) mod m` k-hash trick), so per-test cost is one 64-bit FNV hash + 7 word ops. Bit ops are SWAR popcount + word-aligned bit indexing — no math/bits dependency for hot-path inlining stability.
- `DiffObjectSetRequest` / `DiffObjectSetResponse` are the wire types: caller submits two ObjectSet definitions; handler executes both through the existing `Executor.Execute` path (so `?branch=` / `?asOf=` semantics propagate), enforces same-ObjectType, runs `DiffPrimaryKeys`, returns sorted PK partition + per-side cardinalities + `intersectSize` mirror + `durationMs` for the diff step only (executor latency excluded so SDK clients can isolate the algorithm cost).
- Route registered at `POST /api/v2/ontologies/{ontologyApiName}/objectSets/diff` in `cmd/server/main.go` (right after `createTemporary`, before any `{objectSetRid}` wildcard so chi resolves it as a static segment).
- OpenAPI spec entries added in `api/openapi.yaml` (operationId `diffObjectSets` + `DiffObjectSetRequest`/`DiffObjectSetResponse` schemas) and mirrored to `cmd/server/openapi_spec.yaml` so both contract gates pass.

**Files changed:**
- `pkg/oss/objectset/diff.go` (new)
- `pkg/oss/objectset/diff_test.go` (new — 7 unit tests + 1 benchmark)
- `cmd/server/main.go` (wired POST .../objectSets/diff)
- `api/openapi.yaml` (path + 2 schemas)
- `cmd/server/openapi_spec.yaml` (mirror)
- `prd.json` (US-430 passes: true)

**Verification:**
- `go build ./...` clean
- `go vet ./...` clean
- `go test ./...` full suite green (76s for `pkg/oss/objectset`, 600s overall)
- 1M vs 1M memory test: heap allocation under 200 MiB cap (pass)
- `go test -bench BenchmarkDiffPrimaryKeys -benchmem -benchtime=1x`: **511 ms / 83 MB / 3100 allocs** for 1M vs 1M with shuffled inputs and 50% overlap on M3 Max — well under the PRD 10 s / 200 MB envelope
- `go test ./cmd/server/... -run "Contract"` green (OpenAPI spec round-trip after path + schema additions)

**Learnings for future iterations:**
- The PRD's 200 MiB / 10 s envelope is dominated by the cost of building two `map[string]struct{}` over the inputs — at ~80 bytes/entry on amd64, two 1M maps alone burn ~160 MiB before any result allocation. The bloom-filter pre-screen lets us replace ONE of those maps with a ~1.25 MiB bloom + a sorted slice + a bitset, halving the steady-state footprint. For future "compare two large PK sets" surfaces (branch reconcile diff, materialised-vs-live snapshot drift, parquet compaction dedup), reuse `DiffPrimaryKeys` rather than rolling a fresh map-based diff.
- `runtime.HeapAlloc` deltas across `runtime.GC()` boundaries are the correct memory-budget proxy for an in-process algorithm — `MemStats.TotalAlloc` keeps growing across GC cycles, and `Sys` includes the unrelated runtime overhead. The skip-on-`-short` pattern keeps the 1M memory test out of the standard unit run while remaining a defended bound under the full suite.
- The handler chooses to mirror the `LoadObjects` branch / asOf parsing rather than introducing new query plumbing — both ObjectSet sides resolve through one shared `?branch=` value, so a future "diff main vs feature-branch on the same ObjectSet definition" surface will need a separate `?leftBranch=&rightBranch=` shape instead of overloading the single param. Pattern: when a new endpoint accepts two ObjectSet definitions, give them a single shared scope axis up front; split into per-side scopes only when a use case demands it.
- `executeError` (the typed `ErrQueryTooLarge` 422 / generic 400 fork at `handler.go:193`) returns an `*apierror.APIError` whose `Parameters` map is guaranteed non-nil by `newAPIError`. The diff handler decorates the error with a `side: "left"|"right"` parameter so SDK callers can identify which executor failed without having to parse the wrapped message — reuse the same enrichment pattern when a multi-side endpoint composes multiple executor results.
- The `intersectSize` field on `DiffObjectSetResponse` is technically `len(InBoth)` and could be derived client-side, but writing it explicitly to the wire shape lets clients short-circuit the "is there any overlap" UI prompt without iterating the full PK list. Mirror this for any future multi-list response when the size is the primary disposition signal — pay one int on the wire to save the consumer an O(n) reduce.
- Bloom-filter false positive rate × set size = expected false-positive count. At ~1% over 1M entries that's ~10K extra binary searches in the right-walk — negligible vs the 1M sort dominator. Don't bump the bits-per-key past 14 unless a future workload measurably suffers from FP-driven verification cost; the marginal memory win is real but the FP volume is already O(1%).
